Checked 18 November 2026. The Embassy's older 2023 student-document notice remains linked for X1/X2 document distinctions; re-open it and obtain the institution's latest checklist before filing.

MALAYSIA

International student pass — EMGS, eVAL and post-arrival steps

Start with a genuine offer from an eligible institution. Higher-education applicants must have an offer from a public university or approved private higher education institution authorised to enrol international students. Institution and course eligibility must be checked, not assumed from a marketing brochure.

1. Apply through the institution / EMGS.

Malaysia Immigration states that higher-education applicants must be outside Malaysia when the application is submitted. The institution or student may submit through the relevant EMGS/STARS process. EMGS screens the application and eligible cases are forwarded for eVAL approval. Students from visa-required countries must then obtain the required Single Entry Visa through the official eVISA portal before travel.

2. Core application documents.

EMGS lists a white-background passport photo, passport bio-data and visa pages, passport validity of at least 18 months, offer letter and health declaration form. Academic records and English-language evidence are also relevant to the VAL process. The institution may request additional documents. Follow the applicant's institution-specific upload checklist and do not assume every applicant has an identical document set.

3. After arrival — medical and Student Pass endorsement.

Malaysia Immigration and EMGS require new students to complete post-arrival medical screening at an EMGS panel clinic (or the permitted public-university clinic) within 7 days of arrival (Immigration wording; do not convert this to 7 working days). This is a working-day deadline, not simply “7 calendar days”. Failure to attend can prevent Student Pass endorsement. After a satisfactory medical result and EMGS support letter, the institution submits the Student Pass endorsement application to the Immigration Office responsible for its location. If the student fails the medical, the EMGS guidance provides a short appeal window (currently 3 working days); obtain immediate institution/clinic advice. Arrival is not the final Student Pass endorsement.

4. Part-time work is restricted and needs approval.

Work rights must be explained conservatively. EMGS public FAQ states that international students at public/private higher education institutions may work up to 20 hours per week ONLY during semester breaks or holidays longer than 7 days, at permitted sectors such as restaurants, petrol kiosks, mini markets and hotels, with prior Immigration approval through the institution. Immigration’s Student Pass page lists approved locations and 20 hours/week but does not state the holiday-only condition on that page. To avoid overpromising, counsel clients using the stricter EMGS holiday-only rule and require prior written approval. Language/training/accredited-centre students are excluded. Cashier/front-desk and other prohibited roles must not be promised. Never use expected wages as proof of tuition/living funds.

5. Dependants are limited.

Immigration guidance permits eligible dependants only for Student Pass holders at Master's or PhD level. Do not tell diploma, foundation, bachelor's or language students that they can automatically bring dependants under this facility.

6. Budget and financial evidence.

The public Immigration and EMGS pages reviewed here do not set one single universal financial balance for every institution, programme and applicant. Obtain the latest EMGS/institution financial-document instructions and any Sri Lanka-specific visa requirements. Medical insurance is compulsory for international students throughout their period of study; confirm the institution's current EMGS-compliant policy, premium and coverage rather than quoting an old package price. Keep tuition, insurance, living costs, visa fees and sponsor funds separate in the budget; confirm the exact fee schedule before payment.

7. eVAL validity and travel timing.

EMGS states that the eVAL is valid for six months from its issue date. A new student must not travel to Malaysia while the eVAL is still being processed; travel only after the eVAL is issued and any required Single Entry Visa has been obtained. Check the actual expiry date on the student's eVAL and current SEV requirements for the applicant's nationality before booking flights.

8. Graduate Pass — nationality restriction (critical for Sri Lankan clients).

EMGS currently lists the nationalities eligible for the 12-month Graduate Pass. Sri Lanka does NOT appear on the published eligible-nationality list. Although the pass is described as a post-study option for eligible Bachelor’s degree or higher graduates, do not tell a Sri Lankan student that they can automatically stay and work for one year after graduation. Re-check the live EMGS nationality list at the time of advice. The current page also requires, among other conditions, a passport valid at least 18 months, valid Student Pass during application, local health insurance and a Malaysian citizen sponsor/personal bond.

9. Fee and processing-time safeguards.

Immigration lists RM60 as the Student Pass fee only; this is not the complete visa/application cost. EMGS processing and eVAL fees, medical screening, insurance, iKad/courier and any nationality-based visa fee may be separate. Use the live EMGS fee calculator for the exact applicant/course/pass duration and confirm the current payment invoice with the institution. EMGS FAQ states a 14-working-day client charter for complete applications, but checks, incomplete documents and Immigration clearance can extend the timeline; never guarantee 14 days.

Official references

  • Malaysia Immigration Department — Student Pass, work permission and dependants
  • Education Malaysia Global Services — required documents
  • EMGS — post-arrival medical screening deadline and appeal guidance
  • EMGS — compulsory student medical insurance (2026 guidance)
  • Education Malaysia — eVAL six-month validity and travel FAQ
  • EMGS FAQ — holiday-only work restriction, 14-working-day client charter and Graduate Pass rules
  • EMGS — Graduate Pass eligible-nationality list and requirements
  • EMGS — fee definitions and refund policy
  • MyGovernment Malaysia — student pass process (updated 18 June 2026)
  • Official Malaysia eVISA portal

Counsellor stop-checks

  • Institution, campus and course authorised for international students
  • Offer letter and EMGS application route verified
  • Passport validity, photo, health declaration and English evidence checked
  • eVAL and any required SEV obtained before travel
  • 7-day post-arrival medical deadline explained
  • Student Pass endorsement confirmed after arrival
  • Work allowed only during qualifying breaks under EMGS FAQ and after Immigration approval
  • Graduate Pass eligibility checked against the live nationality list; Sri Lanka currently absent
  • Full itemised EMGS/Immigration fee estimate checked, not just RM60 Student Pass fee
  • Dependants eligibility checked against Master's/PhD rule

Source basis: Malaysia Immigration Department Student Pass page; EMGS FAQ, required-documents, insurance and Graduate Pass pages. Checked 18 November 2026. Where Immigration and EMGS public descriptions of student work differ in detail, this guide adopts the more restrictive EMGS holiday-only condition pending written confirmation from Immigration for the individual student.

THAILAND

Non-Immigrant ED / ED Plus — study route and Sri Lanka e-Visa process

Choose the category by the real programme. Non-Immigrant ED is the education route for qualifying study, training and exchange activities. ED Plus is a separate facility for eligible full-time students enrolled in a Thai higher-education institution in a degree programme at bachelor's level or above. Do not describe every language school, short course or diploma as ED Plus eligible.

1. Sri Lanka application channel — online e-Visa only.

The Royal Thai Embassy in Colombo states that applicants in Sri Lanka and the Maldives must submit visa applications through the official Thai e-Visa platform; applications are accepted exclusively through the official online Thai e-Visa portal; the portal states that applicants do not normally need to submit their passport or supporting documents in person. The applicant must be outside Thailand when applying and remain physically in Sri Lanka or the Maldives throughout processing. Its current general guidelines say applicants must remain physically in Sri Lanka or the Maldives throughout processing, apply at least one month before travel. The Embassy warns that Non-Immigrant applications may require additional processing time; its 3–7 working-day estimate is specifically for tourist visas and must NOT be quoted as an ED/ED Plus service standard. Upload clear PDF documents, and be ready for additional-document requests or an interview. Visa fees are non-refundable and payment does not guarantee approval. The Embassy says payment does not guarantee approval. Select the correct Non-Immigrant category and check the live category-specific upload list in the portal.

2. Admission and institution approval.

Obtain the official acceptance/confirmation letter from the Thai university, school or course provider. For private or non-formal providers, check whether the institution and course have the required registration/approval from the relevant Thai authority. A generic enrolment receipt or agent letter is not a substitute for the required institutional acceptance and approvals. The published Thai ED checklist from another Thai mission lists passport, photo, acceptance letter, provider registration/approval and criminal-record evidence; treat this as an example only, not the Colombo checklist.

3. Financial evidence — Colombo-specific amount not confirmed.

The Royal Thai Embassy Colombo's current general visa-guidelines page includes financial requirements for tourist applications, but that tourist amount must not be copied into a Non-Immigrant ED student case. The sources reviewed did not establish one universal current financial threshold for all Sri Lankan ED/ED Plus applicants. Check the ED/ED Plus-specific live e-Visa document list and obtain any required amount, bank statement period, sponsor rules and accepted evidence from the Embassy or receiving institution. Record the exact source and date in the case file.

4. Initial stay and extensions.

A Non-Immigrant ED entrant is generally granted an initial stay of up to 90 days. Thai government guidance advises applying for an extension about one month before the permitted-stay expiry, with an institution letter and study records; the Immigration Bureau decides the extension and the period granted. Treat this as general guidance, not a guarantee of a one-year extension. The Thai government also states that formal-school extensions may be granted for up to one year at a time, while non-formal institutions may be limited to 90 days per extension and one year total; the exact category and institution approval matter. ED students who leave Thailand and wish to preserve their remaining permission to stay generally need a re-entry permit before departure. ED Plus degree students have a different re-entry facility during their course and are generally exempt from obtaining a re-entry permit for course-related departures/returns while their permission remains valid. Students staying in Thailand continuously for more than 90 days must comply with the applicable 90-day address report (TM.47); check the due date and permitted online/in-person filing method with Immigration. This report is separate from a visa extension. Verify the current local Immigration Bureau procedure and the student's entry stamp/permission expiry; a visa validity date is not the same as the permitted-stay expiry.

5. Work is not an assumed student benefit.

Do not tell ED or ED Plus holders that student status automatically gives part-time work rights. Thailand's Ministry of Foreign Affairs states that foreigners may not work unless they have the appropriate work permit, and must hold the correct immigration status to be eligible to apply. A course internship, paid placement or job must be checked with the university, Immigration Bureau and Department of Employment before any activity begins; obtain the required status and work authorisation first. Never use expected Thai earnings as the applicant's primary tuition or living-cost funding.

6. ED Plus post-graduation facility — not a work permit.

Thai government information describes ED Plus as a route for qualifying full-time bachelor's degree or higher students at Thai higher-education institutions, with an application for a stay extension of up to one year after graduation to seek employment, travel or undertake other activities. This is not automatic for every student: the student must meet the ED Plus conditions and the university/Immigration Bureau must support and approve the extension. Some universities publish additional case-specific eligibility/document rules, so confirm the graduating institution’s current written process. It does not itself authorise employment. If hired, the graduate must obtain the appropriate immigration status and work authorisation before working. Confirm implementation and eligibility with the university and Thai Immigration Bureau.

7. Visa validity is not the same as permission to stay.

The visa's validity period is the window in which it may be used to enter Thailand. The immigration officer's arrival stamp records the actual permitted period of stay. Counsellors must record and monitor the arrival-stamp expiry date, not calculate the student's deadline from the visa issue date alone.

  • Royal Thai Embassy Colombo — new visa measures (4 September 2026), Non-Immigrant processing caveat

  • Thailand Government — ED extension, formal/non-formal school distinction and 90-day reporting

  • Thailand Government — ED extension timing and 90-day reporting overview

  • Thailand Government — student visa preparation and extension overview
  • Thailand Government — education visa and work restriction overview

Counsellor stop-checks

  • ED or ED Plus selected to match the real programme and degree level
  • Thai institution and course approval verified
  • Latest Colombo e-Visa checklist and upload categories checked
  • Applicant remains in Sri Lanka/Maldives and outside Thailand during online application
  • Do not quote tourist 3–7 working-day processing time for ED/ED Plus
  • Financial evidence requirement confirmed for the exact visa category
  • 90-day initial stay, extension and reporting obligations explained
  • No part-time work promised without separate legal authorisation
  • ED Plus graduate stay not represented as a work permit

Source basis: Royal Thai Embassy Colombo guidelines updated 11 November 2026 and new measures dated 4 September 2026; official Thai e-Visa portal; Thai MFA; Thailand Government ED extension guidance. ASIA COUNSELLOR TOOL

Japan · South Korea · China · Malaysia · Thailand — document comparison and pre-submission checklist

Use this as an intake and quality-control aid, not as a substitute for each country's live Embassy, immigration, university or EMGS checklist. Mark every item as received, verified, not applicable or pending. Never transfer a document rule or bank-fund threshold from one country to another.

Japan — Student / COE

  • Confirm school type, course length and correct residence status.
  • Confirm school/representative's COE application and collect the school's current sponsor/funds evidence list.
  • Prepare passport, photo, admission/enrolment and education documents requested by school/Embassy.
  • Do not use the old general Colombo visa page as a complete current student checklist.
  • Explain separate permission is required before part-time work; verify the student's actual permission and limits.

South Korea — D-2 / D-4

  • Confirm degree D-2 versus language/training D-4-1 and the institution's eligibility.
  • Use the Colombo D-2 checklist dated 25 February 2026; D-4-1 has a separate checklist.
  • Check 3-month original bank statement and balance letter issued within 14 days for D-2; verify parent sponsorship documents if used.
  • Check academic records, MOFA certification, police report, TB report and Korean/English language evidence against the Embassy list.
  • Part-time work requires prior immigration permission and eligibility checks.

China — X1 / X2

  • Classify study duration: X1 over 180 days; X2 up to 180 days.
  • For X1, verify original JW201/JW202 and Admission Notice as required by the Colombo notice; confirm X2 admission evidence separately.
  • Ask the Embassy and receiving institution for current financial evidence and submission channel; do not import another mission's amount.
  • Explain post-entry residence-permit formalities for X1 and confirm deadlines with the host institution.

Malaysia — Student Pass / EMGS

  • Verify institution, campus and course are authorised to enrol international students.
  • Check offer letter, passport validity (EMGS guidance: at least 18 months), photo, health declaration, academic and English evidence.
  • Track EMGS screening and eVAL; obtain required SEV/eVisa before travel.
  • Plan post-arrival medical examination within 7 days and Student Pass endorsement.
  • Check the restricted part-time work facility and approval; do not promise work to all course categories.

Thailand — ED / ED Plus

  • Match ED or ED Plus to course type and degree level; verify provider registration/approval.
  • Use Thai e-Visa portal and the current Royal Thai Embassy Colombo category-specific upload list.
  • Ensure applicant remains in Sri Lanka/Maldives during processing and applies early enough.
  • Confirm funds requirement directly; do not reuse tourist visa balance for ED student case.
  • Explain stay extension/reporting and that student status does not itself authorise employment.

Universal counsellor file review — before submission

  • Passport name, date of birth, passport validity and prior travel history match every form and certificate.
  • Client reference and age range recorded internally; do not use passport number as client reference.
  • Course, level, duration, campus, intake and institution recognition independently verified.
  • Academic certificates/transcripts and translations/certifications meet the destination's current format and validity period.
  • Language evidence matches the exact programme and visa route.
  • Bank evidence is genuine, traceable, current, in the accepted account holder's name and meets the exact destination checklist; sponsor relationship and income/source verified.
  • Tuition, insurance, visa fees, living costs and travel costs itemised separately; expected part-time income is not counted as guaranteed funding.
  • Medical, TB, police clearance, biometrics and insurance requirements checked for the applicant's exact case.
  • Current official checklist URL, page/update date and date checked saved to the case record.
  • Applicant has been told that admission, eVAL/COE/JW form, visa fee payment or submission does not guarantee visa approval or border entry.
  • Work rights, dependant eligibility and post-study options explained only where the exact permit/category qualifies; never promise a job.
  • Counsellor reviewer initials, outstanding items and human approval recorded before filing.

Control rule: any unclear requirement, conflicting source or unverified threshold must be marked Needs review and escalated to the institution/Embassy. Do not convert an unverified agent statement into a confirmed legal rule.

EUROPE / SCHENGEN

Do not treat Schengen as one student-visa system

The common Schengen short-stay rules do not replace national long-stay student visa and residence-permit rules. The host country decides the long-stay study process.

Study up to 90 days

Check whether a short-stay Schengen visa is required and apply to the country responsible for the main destination. A short-stay visa is not a substitute for a national student residence permit for a longer course.

Study longer than 90 days

Use the national long-stay visa / student residence procedure of the country where the institution is located. Financial means, accommodation, insurance, admission and other evidence vary by country.

Germany — Sri Lanka example

For a Sri Lankan applicant planning long-term study in Germany, use the German Embassy Colombo's national-visa student instructions and online portal. The Embassy's student page lists items including passport, application forms, motivation letter/CV, academic certificates, admission, language evidence and proof of subsistence. It currently displays a blocked-account figure of EUR 11,904 per year / EUR 992 per month; verify the live figure and accepted alternatives before counselling because financial thresholds can change.

The Embassy's current national-visa page lists Students among the categories that must register through the Federal Foreign Office online portal (Auslandsportal). Follow the student-specific portal instructions and attend the in-person appointment when instructed. The Colombo student checklist is the applicant-specific document reference; do not confuse the national student visa with a short-stay Schengen visitor visa.

Colombo student checklist — details counsellors must not miss.

The live Embassy student page asks for a passport with at least six months' validity; completed national-visa application and declaration; three biometric photos; copies of all used passport pages; motivation letter and CV; O/L and A/L certificates authenticated by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Foreign Employment & Tourism; Bachelor's degree for master's applicants; employment evidence where applicable; admission or conditional acceptance from a German university; language evidence in the language of instruction and German evidence where applicable; and proof of subsistence. The page asks applicants to prepare two identical document sets and bring original certificates. Check the live checklist and portal-generated requirements again before submission.

Funding proof and blocked account.

The Embassy student page currently states EUR 11,904 per year / EUR 992 per month for a blocked account, and lists a German-institution sponsorship/scholarship or a German-resident sponsor's formal Declaration of Commitment as alternatives. The federal 2026 study guidance also lists EUR 11,904 for the ordinary admitted-student route. Do not confuse this with the separate higher-education-place-seeking visa under Section 17(2), for which the federal portal lists EUR 1,091 per month in 2026; that is a different purpose/category and must not be substituted for the admitted-student amount. A normal family support letter from Sri Lanka should not be assumed to replace one of the accepted forms. A blocked account has provider fees and a monthly withdrawal cap; only use the amount and proof format shown by the competent mission/Consular Services Portal at the actual application date.

Fee, processing and appointment.

The Embassy student checklist currently displays a EUR 75 national-visa fee payable in Sri Lankan rupees and says it is non-refundable if refused. The Embassy's general long-stay page describes processing as about three months, while its student page cautions that processing takes at least several weeks. Treat these as planning information, not a service guarantee; do not promise a decision date. Appointment/portal route and fees must be checked live.

Academic eligibility is not just an offer letter.

Check whether the applicant's school-leaving qualification gives access to the intended German higher-education programme, whether Studienkolleg or another preparatory route is required, and the institution's language and subject prerequisites. A conditional admission is not proof that every academic or visa condition has been met.

Student work rights.

The Federal Government's current Make it in Germany portal confirms students may work up to 140 full days or 280 half days per year, or up to 20 hours per week. It lists the 2026 blocked-account minimum as EUR 11,904 (EUR 992/month). This was cross-checked on 16 November 2026 against the federal study-visa page; the Embassy Colombo student checklist is still the controlling local document reference. Check the current residence-permit conditions, statutory rules, permitted counting method and latest financial amount before advising; do not treat work as guaranteed income or as a substitute for required proof of funds.

France — student long-stay route (national rules)

For studies lasting more than three months, France-Visas states that an eligible student is generally issued a long-stay visa equivalent to a residence permit (VLS-TS) with student status, subject to the arrival formalities. A course not exceeding three months follows a short-stay route. Do not describe this as a single visa valid for studying long-term in every Schengen country.

Admission and financial resources.

The applicant must be over 18, have selected a course and be accepted by a higher-education establishment. France's official Public Service guidance currently states minimum resources of EUR 877.50 per month for the student residence route. Confirm the current amount, exemptions and accepted proof for the applicant's exact case before advising; do not reuse Germany's blocked-account figure.

After arrival — VLS-TS validation.

Where a VLS-TS is issued, it must be validated online within three months of arrival. The current French Public Service guidance lists a EUR 150 validation fee for the student VLS-TS. Verify the current online procedure and fee at the time of application.

Work during study.

A non-EU student holding the relevant student VLS-TS or student residence card may generally work up to 964 hours per year (60% of the annual legal working time) without a separate work authorisation, subject to the employer's required declaration and applicable exceptions. This is a France-specific rule, not a Schengen-wide work entitlement. Part-time work is supplementary income and should not be presented as a way to fund all study and living costs.

Counsellor check: verify course duration, institution/admission, correct visa category, monthly funds evidence, insurance/accommodation and the consular process applicable to a Sri Lankan applicant. Do not assume the Études en France procedure applies to every nationality; check the current country list and France-Visas instructions.

Spain — long-term study authorisation (national rules)

For studies longer than 90 days, Spain's national long-term study authorisation applies; it is not issued by a central Schengen office. Confirm that the institution is authorised and the programme meets the current study-category and attendance requirements. The Ministry's current guidance includes higher education and specified post-secondary programmes; some non-university programmes require at least 50% in-person attendance.

Admission, funds and insurance.

Check the exact course, institution, enrolment and fee payment. The current Spanish Ministry guidance requires sufficient means for the stay and return; its published formula is generally 100% of IPREM per month for the student's maintenance, unless accommodation is prepaid for the full stay. Verify the current IPREM amount and accepted evidence at filing; do not use an old euro figure. Health insurance and other case-specific documents are also required. The Ministry guidance generally requires filing at least two months before the course starts, subject to stated exceptions.

Work during study.

For eligible higher-education study authorisations, employment may be authorised automatically where compatible with the studies, subject to a maximum of 30 hours per week. This is not a promise of employment or income; check the exact authorisation, course category, geographic limits and current regulations. Do not generalise the 30-hour rule to every short course or training category.

Family members.

Eligible family members may have a related stay authorisation, but the Spanish Ministry states that student family members are not authorised to work under that family status. Assess each family member separately and verify current requirements.

Italy — national study visa and residence permit (Sri Lanka checklist)

For a course lasting more than 90 days, a Sri Lankan applicant normally needs an Italian national study visa. Use the Italian Embassy in Colombo's current checklist, not a generic Schengen tourist-visa checklist. The Embassy's Version 3/2026 checklist dated 3 September 2026 states that study visa processing may take up to three months; apply well ahead and do not promise expedited processing.

Admission, UNIVERSITALY and academic evidence.

For relevant university degree routes, check the required pre-enrolment through UNIVERSITALY and the university's enrolment/admission confirmation. The Colombo checklist requests a Declaration of Value or CIMEA certificate where applicable. For bachelor's, master's, single-cycle master's and PhD applicants, it specifies language evidence at no lower than B2 in the course language, even if the institution did not request it. Confirm the requirement for the exact course and applicant.

Financial evidence — use the Colombo figure, not another Schengen country's amount.

The Embassy Colombo Version 3/2026 checklist specifies funds of at least EUR 10,179.85 per year for the listed degree-course categories, with scholarship evidence as an alternative and/or additional evidence. It also asks for traceable source-of-funds documentation, including recent tax/income and employment evidence and 12 months of bank statements for the applicant and/or parent/relative sponsor. For third-party sponsorship, check the sponsor relationship/reason letter, employment/business evidence and recent bank statements. Verify the live checklist and any later MUR circular before quoting the amount.

After arrival — residence permit deadline.

Students staying more than three months must apply for the Italian residence permit (permesso di soggiorno) within eight working days of arrival. Follow the official postal-kit and Questura appointment steps, retain the application receipt and check renewal deadlines well before expiry. The visa is the entry document; it is not a replacement for the residence permit.

Work during study.

The official Italian Ministry guidance states that a study residence permit allows part-time work up to 20 hours per week and no more than 1,040 hours per year. Check the student's actual permit and current law; do not promise a job or rely on earnings as the main proof of funds.

  • EU Immigration Portal — country-specific long-stay guidance
  • German Embassy Colombo — Students
  • German Embassy Colombo — Long-stay national visa
  • Federal “Make it in Germany” portal — Student visa and 2026 funding guidance
  • German Embassy Colombo — Online visa portal
  • German Embassy Colombo — Opening / unblocking a blocked account
  • Spain Ministry — Study stay authorisation, admission and funds
  • Spain Ministry — Student employment, 30-hour limit
  • Italian Embassy Colombo — Study Visa Checklist, Version 3/2026
  • Study in Italy — Residence permit after arrival
  • Italian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Study in Italy FAQs and student work limit
TÜRKIYE

Student visa and student residence permit — Sri Lanka route, 2026 work rules and fees

Scope: Non-Turkish nationals entering Türkiye for formal associate, undergraduate, graduate or postgraduate higher-education study. Visa requirements, residence permit rules and work authorisation are separate checks. Confirm the exact course and current consular checklist before taking an application.

1. Admission and correct entry visa.

Obtain admission and active registration from a recognised Turkish higher-education institution. Sri Lankan ordinary passport holders require a visa to enter Türkiye. An e-Visa is for tourism or commerce only; study requires a regular visa issued by a Turkish Embassy/Consulate. Do not tell a student to travel on a tourist e-Visa and convert it after arrival. The Turkish Embassy in Colombo publishes an online visa application process; the applicant should check the current study-visa category, document list and submission channel before booking. The embassy's older information note gives the visa.gov.tr portal and original-document submission instructions, but the current authorised intake arrangement must be reconfirmed.

2. Student residence permit after arrival.

Students enrolled in associate, undergraduate, graduate or postgraduate programmes are eligible to apply for a student residence permit under Law No. 6458. Apply through the official e-İkamet system and follow the appointment/file-submission instructions of the Provincial Directorate of Migration Management. The permit may cover the study period; where the course is shorter than one year, it cannot exceed the course period. Keep the application form, passport and visa copies, biometric photographs, active student certificate, address/accommodation proof, insurance evidence and payment receipt ready. A student visa is the entry document; it is not a substitute for the residence permit.

3. 2026 residence permit costs — Sri Lankan nationals.

The 2026 residence-permit document/card fee is TRY 964. The Presidency of Migration Management lists Sri Lanka among nationalities exempt from the ordinary residence-permit fee under reciprocity. Do not confuse this exemption with the separate document/card fee. Check the fee generated by the official e-İkamet application and do not pay unofficial agents for an appointment; the official appointment is free.

4. Financial means and health insurance.

Do not publish one universal fixed bank-balance amount as a statutory student minimum: the official student-permit conditions require supporting information and documents related to the purpose of stay, and additional evidence may be requested by the provincial migration authority. Prepare credible evidence of tuition payment, living-cost funding, sponsor relationship and source of funds, plus accommodation. Students may request inclusion in Turkish general health insurance (GSS) within three months of enrolment, subject to the applicable rules; otherwise arrange qualifying private health insurance covering the requested residence period. Missing the three-month GSS window may mean private insurance is required.

5. Student work rights — current criteria effective 11 March 2026.

Foreign students in formal associate and undergraduate programmes may apply for a work permit only after completing their first year; their work is part-time. Graduate students enrolled in formal education programmes are not subject to that first-year/part-time limitation in the Ministry's current evaluation criteria, but they still need a work permit. A student residence permit alone does not authorise employment. Employer and student must use the Ministry of Labour's work-permit process; do not promise a job or assume part-time income will cover visa funds.

6. After graduation.

A foreigner who applies within six months after graduating from a higher-education programme in Türkiye may be granted a short-term residence permit once, for a maximum of one year, to support the post-graduation transition. This is not an automatic work permit and does not guarantee employment. Any work must be separately authorised.

7. Counsellor stop-check.

Record institution and programme registration, correct study visa (not tourist e-Visa), current Colombo submission channel, e-İkamet application/appointment, address and insurance, TRY 964 card fee versus Sri Lankan residence-fee exemption, sponsor and living-cost evidence, work permit eligibility by study level/year, and graduation-date deadline. Keep the case Needs review until the institution and provincial migration authority confirm the applicant-specific steps.

  • Presidency of Migration Management — 2026 residence permit document fee and Sri Lanka fee exemption
  • Official e-İkamet — Residence permit application

The Colombo visa submission channel and applicant-specific financial evidence must be reconfirmed at filing.

NEW ZEALAND

Student visa — work conditions

Immigration New Zealand says eligible student visa holders may be allowed to work up to 25 hours per week, with full-time work during specified study breaks/holiday periods where the visa conditions allow it. The work entitlement depends on the course and the individual eVisa or visa letter; it is not automatic for every student visa.

Monitoring review — 13 November 2026: VisaWatch detected a page-text change on the current INZ student visa page. The live page and INZ's 27 October 2026 announcement were checked against the notes below. The key changes are already recorded here; this alert alone is not evidence of a further legal change. Recheck the applicant's application date and actual eVisa conditions.

Important change from 27 October 2026 — Master's by research.

INZ removed unlimited in-study work rights for new master's-by-research students. New master's students are subject to the standard 25-hours-per-week in-study limit and full-time work during eligible holidays, where their visa conditions permit. INZ states this change applies to new student visa applications made on or after 27 October 2026. Do not apply this new rule automatically to earlier applications or already-granted visas; check the application date and individual eVisa/visa letter.

English-language ability and genuine study.

INZ's 27 October 2026 announcement says English-language ability is a key factor in assessing whether a student can meet visa conditions. Counsellors should check the exact course/provider admission requirements and current INZ genuine-intention instructions; do not present this announcement as a new universal IELTS score threshold.

Student visa end date after course completion.

INZ says the post-course end-date period is extended from 1 month to 2 months for the relevant student visas. The 31 March expiry date remains unchanged. Check the visa's actual expiry date and the course/duration category rather than calculating an assumed date for a client.

Financial evidence — tertiary students.

INZ's current Fee Paying Student Visa page requires tertiary, English-language and other non-compulsory students to show NZD 20,000 for each year of study (if studying for 1 year or more), or NZD 1,667 per month for study shorter than 1 year. Compulsory-school students have a separate NZD 17,000/year or NZD 1,417/month figure. This is living-cost evidence, separate from tuition and outward travel. Verify the live INZ amount and the applicant's course category before quoting it.

Tuition evidence must cover 1 course or 1 year of study, whichever is shorter. If the programme is longer than one year, a credible plan for later tuition and living costs can strengthen the application; INZ says those later-year funds do not all need to be available immediately. The applicant must also show money or a ticket for onward travel, in addition to living funds, and hold acceptable medical/travel insurance for the required period.

INZ lists evidence such as bank statements, paid accommodation, scholarship evidence, qualifying education-loan documents, fixed-term deposits, sponsor sharing ID, or a completed Financial Undertaking for a Student. A guarantor must show funds that are their own and not borrowed for the undertaking. INZ's INZ 1014 form requests evidence including the relationship, identity and funds; its form gives the tertiary maintenance rate as NZD 20,000/year or NZD 1,667/month.

Important for Sri Lankan applicants: INZ specifically says that citizens of Sri Lanka (among other listed South Asian countries) applying from South Asia may only use immediate family to sponsor them or provide a financial undertaking. Immediate family is defined on the INZ page as partner, parents, siblings, grandparents and parents-in-law. Do not assume a family friend or distant relative is acceptable for a Sri Lankan applicant applying from Sri Lanka; verify the precise sponsor/guarantor route and current evidence rules.

Funds Transfer Scheme (FTS) — Sri Lankan applicants: INZ lists Sri Lanka among the nationalities that may be able to use the FTS. Do not tell every applicant that FTS is compulsory or automatically available. INZ says it will tell the applicant in an Approval in Principle (AIP) letter if FTS must be used. If directed, the applicant opens an FTS savings account and everyday account with one participating bank (ANZ or BNZ), transfers the required maintenance funds, and INZ waits for bank confirmation and tuition-fee receipt before finalising the visa. The FTS maintenance minimum is separate from tuition. Using FTS does not guarantee visa approval. Check bank-specific eligibility and current instructions at application time.

Eligibility and age.

INZ states student work conditions can only be granted to students aged 16 or older. Tertiary students must meet the applicable full-time study/course criteria. Students must work for an employer and cannot be self-employed under these student work conditions.

Transition for older visas.

INZ says the allowed hours increased from 20 to 25 per week on 3 November 2025. A student whose earlier visa still states 20 hours may need to apply for a variation of conditions if eligible. Always check the actual eVisa or visa letter.

Post-study work pathway — do not promise eligibility from the course title.

A Post-Study Work Visa (PSWV) may allow up to 3 years, depending on the qualification and time studied in New Zealand. Current INZ criteria include a Level 7 degree studied full-time for at least 30 weeks in New Zealand; a Level 8–10 qualification studied full-time for at least 30 weeks; or a non-degree Level 4–7 qualification that appears on INZ's eligible list and is completed full-time in New Zealand for its full required duration. For non-degree Level 7 or lower qualifications, employment must relate to the study and the visa conditions specify the permitted work. Degree Level 7 or higher generally has open work conditions, subject to lawful work and any occupational registration.

Most applicants must apply within 3 months after their student visa expires; doctoral graduates have 6 months, and narrow consecutive-qualification situations can have 12 months. Applicants need at least NZD 5,000 for living expenses and must meet health, character and other requirements. The PSWV is generally available only once. Check the exact qualification, study duration, application deadline, visa history and current INZ list before recommending a course.

Official postponement — reviewed 16 November 2026.

INZ's notice published 13 November 2026 confirms that the planned 16 November system and policy changes have been postponed by one week to 23 November 2026 because of the Microsoft outage. This postponement specifically includes the new Short-Term Graduate Work Visa, the Level 7 Graduate Diploma Post-Study Work Visa expansion, and the related online-system changes. Therefore, do not accept or promise applications under these new graduate routes as available on 16 November; recheck the live INZ application portal and official notice on or after 23 November.

Planned Short-Term Graduate Work Visa from 23 November (not yet open on 16 November): six months of open work rights for eligible NZQCF Level 5–7 graduates who studied full-time in New Zealand for at least 24 weeks, excluding English-language, foundation and bridging qualifications, and whose qualification does not already qualify for a PSWV. Applicants need at least NZD 5,000, must meet health requirements, must not previously have held a PSWV or this visa, and must apply within 3 months of their student visa expiring. Work must be for an employer; business ownership is not allowed. It cannot be extended or granted a second time.

Planned Level 7 Graduate Diploma PSWV expansion from 23 November: eligible graduates must complete the NZQCF Level 7 Graduate Diploma full-time in New Zealand for its full duration and also hold a bachelor's degree from New Zealand or overseas. Cross-crediting or recognition of prior learning does not count toward the full-duration requirement. The PSWV duration for this route is the time spent studying the Graduate Diploma, up to 1 year. A past PSWV holder is not eligible for another PSWV. Save the bachelor's degree certificate and official transcript. Check the live online application route when the change commences.

Counsellor stop-rule: until 23 November and confirmation on the live INZ portal, do not market these two graduate routes as open for applications. Verify the exact NZQCF level, qualification list, full-time duration, in-country study, prior qualifications, deadline and individual visa conditions. A visa pathway is not a job offer or residence guarantee.

Official references

  • Immigration New Zealand — Financial Undertaking for a Student (INZ 1014)
  • Immigration New Zealand — Funds Transfer Scheme (FTS), ANZ/BNZ process and AIP requirement
  • Immigration New Zealand — Changes to student visas (27 October 2026)
  • Immigration New Zealand — Working on a student visa
  • Immigration New Zealand — Check or change student visa conditions
  • Immigration New Zealand — Post-Study Work Visa (eligibility, timing and conditions)

  • Immigration New Zealand — Qualifications needed for a Post-Study Work Visa
  • Immigration New Zealand — New and updated post-study work visa options (planned 23 November 2026; postponed)
  • Immigration New Zealand — Student fund requirements
IRELAND

Non-EEA student pathway — visa, provider approval, funds, work and graduate permission

Scope: This summary is for non-EEA/non-Swiss applicants. Sri Lankan applicants should confirm their visa-required status and the exact application-office checklist for their place of residence. An Irish entry visa is not itself residence permission: the permission granted at the border and registered immigration stamp determine what the student may do.

Important geography and travel warning: Ireland is not part of the Schengen Area and issues its own national visas. An Irish visa or Irish Residence Permit (IRP) does not by itself authorise travel to Schengen countries; a Sri Lankan passport holder must check and, where required, obtain a separate Schengen visa from the relevant Schengen country. Likewise, a Schengen visa does not by itself authorise entry to Ireland. Do not market an Irish student visa as a “Schengen visa” or as permission to travel around Schengen.

1. Choose the correct visa by course duration.

For study longer than 90 days, a visa-required applicant needs a Long Stay “D” study visa. For study under 90 days, the relevant route is a Short Stay “C” visa; do not use a short-stay visit/study route as a substitute for long-term student residence. ISD says a long-stay study visa can be applied for up to 3 months before travel and the applicant must apply from their home country or a country where they are legally resident. Apply through AVATS and follow the submission instructions on the generated summary form.

2. Verify provider AND exact programme — ILEP / TrustEd Ireland.

Non-EEA/non-Swiss students may only be recruited to a programme listed on the Interim List of Eligible Programmes (ILEP), or an eligible programme offered by a provider authorised to use the TrustEd Ireland statutory quality mark. The ILEP is closed to new providers; its final update was published 20 June 2025. Existing ILEP-listed programmes may continue only while the provider remains compliant and the ILEP remains in operation. New providers must obtain TrustEd Ireland authorisation through QQI. TrustEd authorisation does not automatically make every course at that provider eligible: verify the exact course and applicable immigration criteria. Save a dated official register check, provider legal name, campus, programme title, NFQ level, full-time status, dates, award body and written offer.

Stop rule: if the exact programme is not on the current ILEP and the provider/course does not meet the TrustEd route, do not accept a deposit or describe it as eligible for Stamp 2. An agent partnership, college website or QQI mark alone is not enough to establish that every advertised programme is immigration-eligible.

3. Check admission, course and fee evidence.

The long-stay study visa acceptance letter must confirm full-time enrolment with at least 15 hours of organised daytime tuition each week, course details, total fees payable, fees paid and whether the college arranged medical insurance. For course fees below €6,000, the full fee must be paid before applying; where fees exceed €6,000, at least €6,000 must be paid before applying, with payment reflected in the acceptance letter. This is the immigration minimum; the college may require more or full payment. Use a traceable bank transfer or approved student-fee payment service and retain the official receipt; do not pay course fees in cash. The applicant must show academic ability and English/Irish language ability appropriate to the course. Explain any gaps in educational history and previous visa refusals accurately. Check the provider's published refund policy, learner-protection arrangements and all fees (including exam fees) before payment. ISD says programme fees must not be paid in cash.

4. Financial evidence — do not count future part-time work.

For academic courses beginning after 1 July 2023, visa-required applicants must show immediate access to at least €10,000 for one academic year, in addition to course fees. They must also show ready access to €10,000 for each subsequent year of study, in addition to tuition. For a course lasting less than 8 months, the requirement is €833 per month of stay or €6,665, whichever is lower. Funds must be available without relying on casual employment or public funds.

Provide the applicant's up-to-date bank statement showing six months of transactions. Explain large or irregular deposits and show source-of-funds evidence. If using a sponsor, identify every sponsor, prove the relationship, provide each sponsor's six-month bank history, employment confirmation and recent payslips or business evidence, and explain the amount and duration of support. Credit cards are not accepted as proof of funds. A savings/deposit account needs bank confirmation that funds can be withdrawn. Degree-programme students may be able to use the approved €10,000 education-bond route as an alternative; this is a pilot with specific provider/payment-service and holding conditions, not an option to assume for every course.

5. Private medical insurance and application file.

Private medical insurance is required for non-EEA students. The college may arrange it, but the acceptance letter must state this; otherwise the applicant must arrange and evidence their own cover. The long-stay visa checklist includes passport (normally valid at least 12 months after intended arrival), photographs, signed AVATS summary, application letter, acceptance letter, proof of fee payment, finances, academic ability, English/Irish ability, education-gap explanations and previous refusal details where relevant. Check the current local visa office instructions; additional documents or biometrics may be requested. Apply up to 3 months before travel, not earlier.

6. Stamp 2 conditions and work limits.

Eligible full-time students with Stamp 2 permission may work casual employment up to 20 hours per week during term time. The permitted 40-hour periods are June, July, August and September, and 15 December to 15 January. These are limited holiday periods, not a year-round 40-hour entitlement. Stamp 2A students cannot work. Students must not engage in self-employment or business. A visa sticker or college promise is not work permission: check the actual immigration stamp/IRP and its expiry date. Applicants must show sufficient funds without relying on work income.

Full-time English-language students must meet attendance and exam requirements: ISD's student guidance specifies at least 85% attendance and a recognised end-of-course examination. English-language study is limited to three eligible 25-week programmes (up to 8 months permission each), after which progression to an eligible higher-education programme is required for further student permission. Student-pathway time limits and academic progression must be checked.

7. Arrival, registration and family expectations.

A long-stay visa allows travel to Ireland; it does not guarantee entry or replace immigration permission. For stays over 90 days, the student must register their permission with ISD within 90 days. Stamp 2 conditions govern study and work. Student applicants should not assume spouse/children can accompany them: ISD says students are typically not permitted to bring family members, with only exceptional circumstances considered.

8. Third Level Graduate Programme (Stamp 1G).

Eligible graduates with an Irish NFQ Level 8 award may receive up to 12 months; eligible Level 9 or higher graduates may receive 12 months initially and a further 12 months subject to conditions. The graduate must hold current Stamp 2 and an up-to-date registration card, apply within 6 months of written notification of the award, provide the final award evidence, passport and medical insurance, and meet the overall student-permission time limits. Level 8 graduates may receive up to 12 months (subject to the 7-year overall student-pathway cap). Level 9 or higher graduates receive 12 months initially and may renew for a further 12 months if they show appropriate steps toward suitable graduate-level employment, subject to the 8-year overall cap. Stamp 1G permits full-time employment while seeking graduate-level employment and an employment-permit route, but does not permit self-employment or running a business. It is not a guaranteed job, employment permit or permanent-residence pathway.

Counsellor stop-rule: Do not quote a total budget without separating tuition, €10,000 living funds, later-year funding plan, travel, insurance, registration and other costs. Do not count projected part-time earnings. Do not promise work, visa approval, family accompaniment or Stamp 1G eligibility until the exact course, award level, provider route, applicant documents and current ISD rules are verified.

Sri Lanka-specific filing route — important correction: The New Delhi Visa Office (NDVO) is the decision/processing office for visa applications from Sri Lankan residents. However, the NDVO student leaflet (25 April 2025) states that VFS submission is available only to residents of India or Nepal; Sri Lanka applications are submitted to the Irish Consulate. The March 2026 NDVO VFS checklist also describes the India/Nepal VFS channel, so do not tell a Sri Lankan resident to book a VFS appointment in Colombo or India unless the Embassy/Consulate gives current written instructions. Complete AVATS first and follow the application summary plus the current Consulate/NDVO instructions for physical document and passport submission. The New Delhi student leaflet says study applications may be lodged up to five months before the course start date; the current long-term study visa page gives the general maximum of three months before travel. Because these official materials state different advance windows, use the date window confirmed by NDVO/Consulate for the specific Sri Lankan application, and do not book travel before visa issue.

Review check: 18 November 2026. Current ISD guidance rechecked for Stamp 2 work limits, student finance evidence, D visa route and course eligibility. Confirm the live ISD checklist and the competent Irish visa office instructions for each Sri Lankan applicant before filing.

  • Immigration Service Delivery — New Delhi Visa Office jurisdiction includes Sri Lanka

DAILY MONITOR ALERT TRIAGE

How to review a possible page-text change

A monitor alert is a lead for a counsellor, not a legal conclusion. Text fingerprints can change because of page redesigns, navigation, cookie notices, updated timestamps, translations or other dynamic content. Never copy an alert directly into client advice.

1. Open the exact official source.

Use the source link shown in VisaWatch Update Monitor. Confirm that it is the responsible authority for the applicant's destination and visa category.

2. Compare the relevant rule, not just the page.

Find the exact paragraph, condition, fee table or legal instrument that changed. Check linked legislation, policy instructions and transition provisions where relevant. If the page only changed layout or navigation, record “No substantive rule change identified” rather than a policy alert.

3. Capture the dates separately.

Record the page publication/updated date, the legal instrument registration date (if any), the commencement/effective date, the date the change applies to applications or permits, and the date checked. If a date is not stated, write “Not stated” rather than estimating it.

4. Check who the change applies to.

Confirm nationality, application location, application date, visa subclass/category, course and provider, family/dependant situation, existing visa conditions, exceptions and transitional rules. Do not assume a general page applies to every client.

5. Record the human review decision.

Use the company's approved case/audit record to capture source URL, exact wording or evidence reference, previous wording, new wording, substantive impact, affected applicants, reviewer name, review date, decision and follow-up action. Keep the rule “Pending human review” until this is complete.

6. Escalate uncertainty.

If the official wording is unclear, pages conflict, or a legal interpretation is needed, escalate to the authorised immigration compliance/legal reviewer or the responsible authority. Do not resolve uncertainty by guessing.

System limitation: VisaWatch currently detects source availability and possible page-text changes. This reference page does not itself save reviewer decisions or provide a persistent approval/audit log. Record review outcomes in the company's approved record system until a secure, access-controlled review workflow is implemented.

FRANCE

Student long-stay visa — admission, financial proof, work and arrival formalities

Scope: for a Sri Lankan applicant planning higher education or a course in France for more than 90 days, assess the long-stay student route (commonly VLS-TS étudiant for stays of up to one year). A course of three months or less generally uses a short-stay route. The exact visa type and document list must be generated through France-Visas for the applicant's nationality, residence and course.

1. Confirm genuine admission and the correct application route.

The applicant must have selected a course and been accepted by a higher-education establishment. Check the institution's legal name, campus, exact programme, qualification, language of instruction, duration, tuition and enrolment status. France-Visas lists the countries covered by the mandatory Études en France (EEF) procedure; Sri Lanka is not listed on the currently published list. Do not automatically tell a Sri Lankan applicant to use EEF: confirm the live country-specific instructions with Campus France / the French Embassy and follow the route shown for the applicant's residence. A conditional offer or agent letter is not necessarily final enrolment evidence.

2. Financial resources — current indexed rule from 1 August 2026.

French law (CESEDA Article R.422-2, effective 1 August 2026) requires monthly resources of at least 47% of the gross monthly SMIC applicable on the date the application is filed. The French government's Service-Public page, verified 1 August 2026, currently displays €877.50 per month (€10,530 for 12 months). The binding legal formula is 47% of the gross monthly SMIC in force on the filing date; therefore re-check the displayed amount and current SMIC on the actual application date. The amount is indexed: calculate against the SMIC in force on the actual filing date rather than treating €877.50 as a permanent fixed figure. This replaces the former €615 benchmark. This is living-resource evidence, not tuition payment or a guarantee of visa approval; separately document tuition and accommodation. Service-Public describes evidence routes including applicant funds, scholarship, employment income and third-party support with identity and evidence of regular transfers/support. For Sri Lankan applicants, confirm the precise accepted evidence and any local checklist additions with France-Visas and the French Embassy/VFS Colombo before filing.

3. Visa application and fees — verify the current local checklist.

For a course longer than three months, France-Visas indicates a long-stay visa for study exceeding three months. Its student page gives an indicative visa fee of €99 for countries outside the EEF procedure and €50 for EEF countries, while noting that special cases may differ. Sri Lanka is not in the published EEF-country list; do not direct Sri Lankan applicants into the EEF route unless the Embassy/Campus France gives updated country-specific instructions. For Sri Lanka, the France-Visas country page says applications are decided by the French Embassy in Colombo and received through VFS Global Colombo 9. It currently states normal processing is 15 calendar days from a complete admissible application, extendable up to 60 calendar days where further checks or documents are needed; this is not a guaranteed turnaround. The current page lists a VFS basic service fee of €19.48 in addition to the visa fee. Recheck all amounts, appointment availability and processing guidance before quoting. Typical core evidence includes passport, completed online application, admission/enrolment certificate, proof of resources, accommodation or temporary address, and any course- or applicant-specific documents shown by the wizard.

4. Work during studies — maximum 964 hours per year.

France-Visas states that foreign students may work up to 964 hours per year (60% of normal annual working hours). This is a legal maximum, not a job offer or guaranteed income. The official guidance warns that part-time work is not sufficient to cover all living expenses and should be treated as supplementary. Do not count future part-time earnings as the initial visa financial proof.

5. Post-study route — eligibility depends on the qualification.

The “Job Search / Business Creation” route is limited to qualifying French higher-education graduates (for example, a professional licence, Master or equivalent, specified Mastère Spécialisé / labelled MSc and other listed qualifications). A graduate who has left France may apply for the corresponding long-stay visa within four years of obtaining the qualifying French diploma. The permit allows a one-year stay to seek work or create a business linked to the studies. If employed under this route, the job must be related to the studies and pay at least €2,800.53 gross per month under the Service-Public page checked 18 November 2026; this is not an unrestricted work permit or job guarantee. Graduates who remain in France must follow the prefecture route and deadlines before their student permission expires. Check the exact diploma and current salary threshold before advising. France-Visas — Job Search / Business Creation.

6. Arrival — validate the VLS-TS within three months.

A VLS-TS étudiant must be validated online within three months after arrival through the French authorities' ANEF portal. Service-Public currently lists a €100 tax payable when validating the student VLS-TS. Keep evidence of validation and payment. If the applicant's visa is a different long-stay category requiring a residence card application, follow the deadline printed on that visa rather than assuming the VLS-TS process applies.

7. Health cover and realistic budget.

France-Visas says non-EU students under 28 must register with the French social-security system. Check the applicant's age, course status and actual health-cover arrangements; additional complementary insurance may be useful because social security does not reimburse every expense. Budget tuition (which can range from a few hundred euros to over €10,000 annually at some private institutions), accommodation, deposit, food, transport, insurance, visa/service fees and arrival costs separately.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before advising, save the France-Visas wizard checklist, admission evidence, course and campus details, financial calculation, sponsor evidence, accommodation plan, fee quote and application route. Confirm the current 47%-of-gross-SMIC calculation (currently displayed as €877.50/month), visa fee and local appointment/document rules on the date of filing. Do not promise visa approval, employment or that part-time work will fund the course.

Official references

  • France-Visas — Student visa, work limit and study stay information
  • France-Visas — current student campaign and €877.50/month financial threshold
  • Service-Public — foreign student visa/residence permit, VLS-TS validation and financial evidence
  • Légifrance — CESEDA Article R.422-2: 47% gross monthly SMIC rule effective 1 August 2026
  • French Government Decree 2026-526 — indexed student financial resources threshold
  • France-Visas Sri Lanka — Embassy decision authority, VFS Colombo, processing and service fee
  • Service-Public — post-study job-search/business route, eligible diplomas, one-year stay and salary conditions
  • Campus France — VLS-TS student visa and three-month validation deadline
  • Campus France India — 24 July 2026 notice: €877.50/month from 1 August 2026 (confirm applicability in Sri Lanka)
  • Service-Public — residence route for job search or business creation after eligible French qualification
  • Service-Public — employment routes after studies, by qualification

NETHERLANDS

Student residence permit — recognised sponsor, study norm, work and post-study route

Scope: this section focuses on international students applying for a Dutch university or higher professional education (HBO) programme. Do not assume the same route or financial amount applies to secondary or vocational (MBO) study. For a Sri Lankan national, confirm the current MVV/residence process and local embassy instructions with the Dutch institution and IND before filing.

1. Verify the institution and exact programme first.

Only an educational institution recognised by the IND as a sponsor can apply for the student residence permit for university/HBO study. Check the legal institution name in the live IND Public Register of Recognised Sponsors and confirm the exact campus and programme. The programme must be full-time and accredited by NVAO in the Netherlands or by an eligible quality-assurance route recognised by IND (including EQAR in Europe). An agent partnership, university marketing page or admission offer alone does not prove that the institution can sponsor the residence permit.

2. 2026 financial study norm — €1,130.77 per month.

For university/HBO students, the IND's published 2026 study norm is €1,130.77 per month, valid from 1 January to 31 December 2026 only. Do not carry this figure into applications filed in 2027 without checking the new IND table. This is the living-cost norm and does not include tuition fees. The student must show sufficient funds for 12 months; the institution checks proof of income and the requirement is renewed each study year. For 12 months, the living-funds calculation is €13,569.24, before tuition and other costs. This is a 2026 living-cost figure, not a tuition-inclusive budget or a guaranteed bank-balance rule for later years. The separate IND 2026 amount for secondary/vocational MBO study is €928.58 per month; do not apply the HBO/university figure to an MBO applicant or assume the same permit route. Recheck the IND table for the application year and exact study type.

Accepted evidence routes include the student's freely accessible bank funds; a deposit to the educational institution's account; an eligible scholarship; or financial support from a person abroad. For a private sponsor abroad, IND requires the prescribed Statement of financial support (private person), a copy of the sponsor's passport/ID and bank evidence in the sponsor's name showing at least 12 months of the study norm, free access to the funds and required bank details. These documents must generally be no older than 3 months. For funds in the student's own account, the account must be in the student's name, freely withdrawable, and the supporting bank document no older than 3 months; foreign bank accounts also require an original bank statement. For a sponsor resident in the Netherlands, the evidence route is different: prescribed support statement, municipal BRP extract showing household composition, ID and proof of independent, sustainable and sufficient income. Do not assume a family sponsorship letter alone is sufficient. Documents must be in Dutch, English, German or French, otherwise arrange translation.

3. Institution applies for MVV and residence permit.

A Sri Lankan applicant normally needs an MVV (Type D entry visa) because Sri Lanka is not on the IND's MVV-exempt nationality list. The IND-recognised educational institution submits the student residence application and applies for the MVV and residence permit together. The IND page, last updated 21 September 2026, lists an application fee of €254 and a statutory decision period of up to 60 days; this can be extended if the application is incomplete or further checks are needed. The institution receives the decision and coordinates the next steps. The institution pays the fee through its process. Do not tell the student to independently lodge the ordinary university/HBO application at VFS.

4. Sri Lanka — MVV sticker after IND approval.

After a positive IND decision, read the decision letter: it specifies where the student can collect the MVV. If it authorises collection in Sri Lanka, book the appointment at the Dutch Embassy in Colombo within 3 months of the decision. Bring the passport (at least 6 months' validity and 2 blank pages), required photograph, the IND approval letter or the institution's email with the V-number, and any additional documents listed in the decision. The passport is retained while the sticker is processed; NetherlandsWorldwide says this can take up to 10 working days. The MVV is valid for 90 days for travel. Do not book non-refundable travel before the sticker is issued.

5. Maintain study progress — at least 50% of credits each study year.

The student must make sufficient progress and normally obtain at least 50% of the credits for each study year. The institution assesses progress and reports insufficient progress to the IND unless there are valid reasons under the applicable rules. A residence permit can be affected if the student does not meet the study-progress requirement. Counsellors should not describe the permit as guaranteed for the full course regardless of academic performance.

6. Work during study — 16 hours/week OR full-time in June, July and August.

For employee work, the employer must obtain a Dutch work permit (TWV). The student must choose either up to 16 hours per week during the year, or full-time work in June, July and August. The employer must obtain the TWV before the student starts employee work; the student cannot apply for the TWV personally. These are alternatives, not an entitlement to combine 16 hours weekly with unlimited summer work. A traineeship that is part of the study programme does not need a TWV. IND also states that self-employment may be possible without a TWV while the student continues to meet residence-permit requirements, but registration with the Chamber of Commerce and tax/health-insurance obligations may apply. Never promise that a job will be found or that part-time earnings can cover tuition or initial proof of funds.

7. Post-study orientation year — eligibility is limited, not automatic.

Eligible graduates may apply for a one-year orientation-year residence permit to seek work or start a business. It can cover, among other categories, graduates of accredited Dutch bachelor/master programmes, certain Dutch post-master programmes, Erasmus Mundus Joint Masters, qualifying research, and specified foreign master's/doctoral/post-master qualifications from designated institutions. For a qualifying foreign institution, IND applies top-200 ranking tests across specified ranking publishers and additional conditions, including an English-language requirement such as IELTS 6.0 or an accepted equivalent unless an exemption applies. Foreign diplomas need Nuffic evaluation. The orientation-year permit is valid for one year and cannot be extended. Do not promise this route to every Dutch graduate or every overseas degree holder.

8. Permit validity, preparatory period and family members.

The IND can issue the study residence permit for a maximum of 5 years; it normally remains valid for 3 months after the study programme ends, provided the student continues to meet the conditions. A preparatory period, such as language or pre-master preparation required by the institution, usually lasts up to 1 year and is included in the study period. The institution may also apply for residence permits for eligible family members. Family members must meet their own requirements and, under the student-family route, are not allowed to work in the Netherlands. Confirm the exact family permit and work annotation before advising.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before recommending a Dutch course, save evidence of IND sponsor status, programme accreditation, full-time status, admission, tuition quote, 12-month financial calculation, sponsor documents, MVV/permit process and the Colombo MVV appointment route stated in the IND decision letter, work conditions/TWV, 50% annual study-progress policy, permit end date, and any family application/work restrictions. Record the version/date of the IND financial threshold and do not reuse 2026 amounts for 2027 applications. Escalate any uncertainty about institution recognition, programme accreditation or sponsor evidence before taking a client deposit.
