The €1,130.77/month university/HBO and €928.58/month MBO study norms are the published 2026 amounts and exclude tuition. Recheck the live IND table and applicant-specific process at filing.

HUNGARY

Study residence permit — eligibility, funds, work and post-study route

Scope: Third-country nationals, including Sri Lankan applicants, seeking full-time study at a state-recognised Hungarian higher-education institution or an authorised foreign higher-education institution operating in Hungary. A qualifying preparatory course organised by a higher-education institution may also be covered. A generic short course should not be assumed eligible.

1. Admission and eligibility.

Obtain the institution's admission certificate or student-status evidence and show the language knowledge required for the programme. The application must include proof that tuition fees have been paid. Confirm that the institution and course fit the statutory study-permit category; a marketing partnership or offer letter alone does not establish eligibility.

2. Sri Lanka application route — Colombo VFS with Embassy appointment coordination.

The Embassy of Hungary New Delhi general consular page lists visahungary.del@mfa.gov.hu or consulate.del@mfa.gov.hu for D-visa appointments, while the Sri Lanka VFS one-pager separately says to request an appointment at appointment.del@mfa.gov.hu. These official pages therefore show inconsistent appointment addresses. Counsellors must ask the Embassy/VFS to confirm the currently accepted address in writing before the applicant emails; do not assume the three addresses are interchangeable. Once the Embassy gives the appointment instructions, the D-type long-stay application is lodged in person at the VFS Hungary centre, 4th Floor, No. 675, Dr Danister De Silva Mawatha, Colombo 09, with biometrics and a full passport copy plus an extra photocopy set. An online consular interview with the Embassy in New Delhi may be arranged. The competent NDGAP regional directorate decides the residence application, not VFS.

3. Financial evidence — no universal fixed monthly euro minimum.

The Hungarian NDGAP/OIF student factsheet does not prescribe one fixed monthly euro amount for all applicants. The applicant must show legally acquired resources sufficient for living costs, accommodation, return/onward travel and healthcare. Evidence may include the applicant's bank certificate/statements and transaction history, regular income, a notarised family-support declaration together with evidence of the sponsor's ability to support, or scholarship evidence. A family sponsorship letter by itself is not enough. The application must also declare a real Hungarian accommodation address and provide evidence of comprehensive health insurance or ability to cover healthcare costs. Do not quote an agent's monthly figure as a universal government minimum.

4. Government application fee and decision time.

The current OIF procedural-fees page lists €110 for issuing a residence permit together with the entry visa for collecting it when applying at a diplomatic/consular mission. In-country fees differ: HUF 39,000 in person or HUF 26,000 via Enter Hungary. State scholarship holders may be exempt under the OIF fee rules. The VFS Sri Lanka study page currently displays a visa fee of LKR 39,560 and a separate VFS service charge of LKR 29,490 without biometrics or LKR 33,180 with biometrics; these amounts are subject to change and should be rechecked at payment. OIF lists a 15-day procedural-administration period, but says the merits decision is made within 60 days; missing-document periods and further checks can extend the practical timeline.

5. D visa, entry and permit validity.

After approval, the consulate issues a single-entry Type D visa valid for 3 months and authorising a stay of up to 30 days to enter and collect the residence permit. Enter Hungary within 3 months of approval; otherwise the approval can become invalid. The study residence permit is normally valid for at least 1 year (or the shorter course duration), with a maximum validity of 3 years per permit; an extension may be granted for 1–3 years. The permit cannot outlast the passport. The approved permit is sent to the educational institution's address.

6. Study progress and renewal.

For an extension, apply from inside Hungary through Enter Hungary while the current permit is still valid, no earlier than 90 days and no later than 30 days before expiry; the applicant must have resided in Hungary on the permit for at least 90 days in any 180-day period. The institution's academic-progress certificate is required. OIF may refuse extension or revoke the permit if the student fails to obtain the degree within one and a half times the programme's prescribed study period. A student changing educational institution must apply for an extension with the new admission certificate.

7. Work during studies.

A student residence-permit holder may work up to 30 hours per week during the study period, and full-time outside the study period for up to 90 days per year. This is the legal maximum, not a job guarantee or a reliable plan to fund tuition and initial living costs.

8. After graduation — job-seeking or business route.

A graduate may apply for a separate residence permit to seek a job or start a business only after successfully completing the studies, while still holding a valid study-purpose residence permit, and where the intended job or business is appropriate to the level of the completed studies. The permit is valid for a maximum of 9 months and cannot be extended. The job-seeking route requires prescribed evidence (for example, registration with the competent employment agency or a Hungarian business's signed statement that employment is scheduled within three months). Apply through Enter Hungary from inside Hungary at least 15 days before the study permit expires; the applicant must have successfully completed the studies and the intended job/business must match the level of the completed studies. It is not automatic and does not guarantee a job.

9. Family members — major restriction.

Under the current OIF factsheet, a third-country national holding or applying for a study-purpose residence permit is generally not eligible for a family-reunification residence permit for accompanying family members. Do not market this route as allowing a spouse or dependants to join automatically. Seek case-specific advice if family relocation is essential.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before taking a client deposit, verify the institution/course eligibility, tuition payment, language evidence, realistic funds and sponsor proof, accommodation, health cover, Colombo appointment instructions, current government and VFS charges, processing timeline, study/work restrictions, permit expiry and post-study application timing. Explain clearly that part-time work is not guaranteed and family reunification is generally unavailable under the student route.

  • NDGAP/OIF — Current procedural fees
  • Enter Hungary — Job-seeking or entrepreneurship residence application documents
  • VFS Global / Embassy of Hungary — Sri Lanka long-stay study application, appointment and Colombo submission guidance
  • VFS Global — Hungary Visa Application Centre, Colombo and current service-charge notice

No universal monthly euro minimum is stated in the OIF student factsheet. The Colombo VFS page currently displays LKR 39,560 visa fee plus LKR 29,490 (without biometrics) or LKR 33,180 (with biometrics) VFS service charge; recheck before payment. Embassy and VFS official pages show different D-visa appointment email addresses, so obtain written confirmation of the currently accepted address before filing.

ITALY

Student visa — Colombo checklist, Universitaly, financial evidence, work and residence permit

Scope: Non-EU applicants applying from Sri Lanka for higher-education study. This section reflects the Italian Embassy Colombo Study Visa Checklist version 3/2026 dated 3 September 2026 and the national 2026/27–2027/28 procedures. Other study-visa categories may have different financial calculations and documents.

1. Confirm course and pre-enrolment.

For relevant higher-education courses, the applicant must complete pre-enrolment through Universitaly and obtain the university's validation. For academic years 2026/27 and 2027/28, this covers bachelor's, master's, single-cycle, AFAM, PhD, postgraduate/master, single-course and foundation-course categories listed by the Ministry. The national deadline for ordinary Laurea/Laurea Magistrale courses is 30 November 2026 for 2026/27 and 31 October 2027 for 2027/28. Other listed categories do not have the same fixed national deadline, but the visa application cannot be later than course commencement. Embassy decision remains independent; pre-enrolment or admission does not guarantee a visa.

2. Financial requirement — 2026/27 and 2027/28 (check the Colombo-specific rule).

For a university-enrolment study visa, the national MAECI FAQ sets the minimum at €10,179.85 per year for academic years 2026/27 and 2027/28 and says that, for multi-year university courses, the national FAQ requires this amount for the first year. However, the Italian Embassy Colombo checklist version 3/2026 (03 September 2026) is stricter: it asks applicants for bachelor's, master's, single-cycle and PhD courses to demonstrate full and total availability of funds for the entire degree duration, with lawful origin and traceability. For a Sri Lankan applicant, follow the current Colombo checklist and obtain written clarification from the Embassy if the total-duration wording is unclear; do not advise that first-year funds alone will necessarily satisfy Colombo. The checklist requests 12 months of applicant and/or parent/relative sponsor bank statements, tax returns where applicable, sponsor employment/business evidence, salary slips and socio-economic/asset evidence. MAECI says a bank guarantee or surety insurance is not suitable proof. Other study-visa categories use a different daily/fixed calculation; do not reuse €10,179.85 automatically.

3. Colombo checklist and documents.

Prepare signed application, ICAO photograph, passport with at least two blank pages and validity at least three months beyond return to Sri Lanka, applicant cover letter/study plan, admission/enrolment proof, Universitaly pre-enrolment, Declaration of Value or CIMEA certificate where applicable, tuition-payment receipt, accommodation evidence, insurance covering at least 30 days before joining the designated scheme in Italy, foreign-exchange bank letter, confirmed flight reservation and prior travel evidence if applicable. For bachelor's, master's and single-cycle courses, the Colombo checklist asks for at least B2 in the language of instruction even if the university did not expressly request it. Sinhala/Tamil documents need official English translation and certification by Sri Lanka Ministry of Foreign Affairs.

4. Processing time and deadline.

The Colombo Embassy checklist warns study visa processing may take up to three months and cannot be expedited. Plan the application well ahead of course start and check the live appointment channel and checklist before booking. The 2026/27 national deadline of 30 November 2026 applies to ordinary degree courses; verify whether the applicant's exact programme falls within this category.

4A. Where Sri Lankan applicants submit the visa application and current fees.

Book the appointment personally through the official VFS Italy Sri Lanka portal; the Embassy states that applications are accepted at the Colombo VFS centre only with a prior appointment, and applicants must choose the correct study category. The Embassy/VFS location is 675, Dr. Danister De Silva Mawatha, Colombo 05. The VFS Sri Lanka fee table currently lists the Type D long-term student visa fee as LKR 18,200 plus a separate Colombo application-centre service fee of LKR 6,370; confirm the live fee table and any optional charges at the time of booking/payment. Do not confuse visa fees with proof-of-funds or post-arrival residence-permit costs.

Application fee — 2026.

The OeAD checklist and Austrian Embassy New Delhi list a €218 application fee for a temporary residence-permit card from 1 January 2026. The fee is payable at application and is not refunded if refused or withdrawn. Other document/legalisation/translation costs and the later Visa D fee may be separate; verify payment method and current consular charges with the Embassy.

Application fee — 2026.

The OeAD checklist and Austrian Embassy New Delhi list a €218 application fee for a temporary residence-permit card from 1 January 2026. The fee is payable at application and is not refunded if refused or withdrawn. Other document/legalisation/translation costs and the later Visa D fee may be separate; verify payment method and current consular charges with the Embassy.

5. After arrival.

Within 8 working days of arrival, apply for the study residence permit (permesso di soggiorno) through the prescribed post-office kit/competent Questura process. Keep the postal receipt, attend the Questura appointment for identification/fingerprints and maintain health cover and enrolment. A visa is the entry authorisation; the residence permit is the ongoing residence document.

6. Student work.

The study residence permit allows subordinate/employee work up to 20 hours per week and no more than 1,040 hours annually. Do not promise a job or assume earnings can fund the visa application. Study must remain the primary purpose. Work beyond the permitted cap requires the appropriate conversion/authorisation.

7. After-study options.

A study permit may be converted to a work permit before expiry if the legal requirements and employer contract/proposal are met. Graduates of qualifying Italian higher-education programmes may have a job-search/self-employment preparation residence route; verify the exact qualification, application timing and current law with the Questura/Prefettura before advising. Do not promise automatic work residence or a guaranteed job.

Counsellor stop-check.

Record exact course category, Universitaly validation, Embassy checklist version, language level, first-year funds and source traceability, tuition/return travel/accommodation/insurance, sponsor relationship and 12-month bank history, application deadline, and post-arrival permit plan. Keep status Needs review until a reviewer confirms the applicant-specific checklist and course category.

FINLAND

Student residence permit — funds, insurance, work and post-study options

Scope: Non-EU/EEA applicant applying for a first residence permit for degree/vocational studies in Finland. A student visa is not the normal route for degree-length study: the applicant generally needs a residence permit for studies before travelling.

1. Study place and application route.

Obtain an acceptance from a Finnish university, university of applied sciences, upper-secondary school or vocational institution for a degree, vocational qualification or qualifying exchange. Apply for the first residence permit from abroad through Enter Finland (paper application is also possible), then book an identity-verification appointment and present your passport/biometrics. For applicants in Sri Lanka, Finland has no mission in Sri Lanka: the official Finland Abroad page directs residence-permit applicants to the Finland Residence Permit Application Centre in New Delhi; check current VFS appointment and travel-entry requirements before booking. The applicant must submit the application personally. Mainly online/distance studies that do not require residence in Finland do not qualify. Admission alone does not guarantee a permit.

2. Living funds — first application.

For studies lasting at least one year, show €9,600 for the first year (€800/month). For shorter studies, show €800 per month. This is separate from tuition. If tuition is unpaid at application time, the applicant must also have enough funds for the first-year tuition in addition to living funds. Living-cost money cannot also be counted as tuition. An approved grant/scholarship for living costs can reduce the cash amount. Institution-provided free accommodation may reduce the requirement to €400/month; free accommodation and meals may reduce it to €270/month, with written proof.

3. Bank and sponsor evidence.

Attach a bank statement covering the previous six months, showing account holder, bank and currency. Funds must be in the applicant's own personal-use account and accessible in Finland. A relative's sponsorship promise/agreement or sponsor bank statement alone is not accepted as proof of the student's funds. If family transferred funds to the student, Migri may request evidence of the money's origin, such as sponsor tax records or asset/bank evidence. Do not advise artificial last-minute deposits or assume a family sponsorship letter substitutes for the applicant's own funds.

4. Health insurance.

Insurance must be valid on arrival and throughout the stay, with excess no more than €300. For studies lasting less than two years, coverage for medical expenses must be at least €120,000. For studies lasting two years or more, pharmaceutical expenses must be covered up to at least €40,000. Ordinary travel insurance limited to 90-day trips is not sufficient. Check the actual policy wording, coverage period and deductible.

5. Application fee and documents.

Migri's study-permit page currently lists €600 for an electronic first application and €750 for a paper first application (minor applicants have separate fees). Core documents include passport, acceptance certificate, six-month bank statement, insurance certificate, proof of paid tuition or scholarship and any requested education/employment records. Fees can change; check the live Migri fee table before payment.

6. Student work rights.

With a valid study residence permit, paid employment in any field is limited to an average of 30 hours per week, equivalent to an average of 120 hours/month or 1,560 hours/year. Some weeks (including holidays) may exceed 30 hours if the year-end average remains within the limit. Practical training or diploma work that is part of the degree is outside this limit only where it is genuinely included in the degree and agreed as such; registering ordinary work as an internship afterwards does not remove the limit. Work must not prevent normal study progress. For the first residence permit, the applicant cannot rely on future work to meet the initial income requirement.

7. Extensions and post-study route.

For a study-permit extension, Migri checks study progress; higher-education students are generally expected to complete approximately 45 credits per academic year, or explain lower progress. Work income may be considered for an extension if evidenced. After completing a Finnish degree, an eligible graduate may apply for a residence permit to look for work or start a business for a maximum of two years. Migri says the application must be made within five years from the date the study/research residence permit expired; the applicant must have completed the degree and show sufficient funds (normally €800/month). The permit gives unrestricted work rights. A separate permit based on a completed Finnish degree may be available where the graduate already has an employment contract or binding job offer. This is an application route, not an automatic post-study visa or job guarantee.

8. Optional D visa — travel after approval.

A D visa is available to applicants for a residence permit for studies. It is an optional entry facilitation route linked to the study residence-permit application/positive decision; it does not replace the residence permit or guarantee approval. Confirm the current application sequence, eligibility and fee with Migri/the Finnish mission before advising the client.

Counsellor stop-check.

Record the exact qualification and institution, tuition amount/payment status, applicant-owned accessible funds and six-month history, origin of family transfers, insurance coverage/deductible, Enter Finland and identity-verification steps, work-hour limit and post-study eligibility. Keep status Needs review until a reviewer checks the applicant's documents and live Migri instructions.

  • Migri — Income requirement for students and bank evidence
  • Migri — Students must have insurance
  • Migri — Students’ right to work
  • Migri — Residence permit to look for work or start a business: maximum two years, five-year application window, funds and fees
  • Finland Abroad — Sri Lanka: Enter Finland, identity appointment and New Delhi application centre
  • Embassy of Finland, New Delhi — residence permit submission appointment and contact details

Migri’s study-permit page and general D-visa page are not fully aligned on student D-visa eligibility; verify directly before advising. Recheck live income, insurance and fee requirements at application. This is a counsellor reference, not a legal decision.

SWEDEN

Higher-education residence permit — admission, funds, work and post-study route

Scope: Non-EU/EEA applicant seeking a first residence permit for on-site higher education in Sweden. For stays under three months, a residence permit is generally not the route; check entry-visa requirements separately. This section does not cover doctoral employment permits or every vocational/other-study category.

1. Admission and application.

Applicant needs a valid passport and final admission to full-time, on-site studies. At a university/university college, 30 credits per semester counts as full-time. Distance learning does not qualify for this higher-education student residence permit. Any tuition fee must be paid before applying; the institution notifies the Swedish Migration Agency (Migrationsverket). First-time application is generally made while outside Sweden, normally through the Swedish Migration Agency online service; the permit must be issued before travel. For applicants resident in Sri Lanka, the Sweden Abroad Sri Lanka page directs residence-permit applicants to submit biometrics at the Embassy of Sweden in New Delhi. The Colombo honorary consulate is not the biometric submission point; it may handle collection of residence-permit cards by appointment. Applicants should select the correct mission in the application and confirm appointment instructions with the Embassy migration section before travel.

2. Financial maintenance — applications in 2026.

Minimum SEK 10,656 per month for the period requested. For 12 months this is SEK 127,872. This is a living-cost threshold, separate from tuition. Documented free food can reduce the monthly requirement by SEK 2,960; documented free accommodation by SEK 4,736. If both are genuinely provided, the combined reduction may apply under Migrationsverket's conditions. Family members accompanying the student increase the required amount.

3. Bank funds and family support.

Personal bank assets must be in an account belonging to the applicant (and accessible to the applicant); the bank statement must be issued no more than four months before the requested permit start date. Private individuals such as parents are not accepted as formal sponsors. Family money must be transferred to an account in the student's name and accessible to them; Migrationsverket may require evidence of the source. A private sponsor letter or parent bank statement alone is not enough. Institutional/organisation sponsorship, scholarships, eligible grants or loans, and evidenced employment income can be assessed under Migrationsverket's specified documentation rules. A job offer alone is insufficient; employment certificate and payslips are required when relying on work income.

4. Health insurance.

For a stay under one year, comprehensive health insurance is required for the full stay, covering emergency and other medical care, hospitalisation, emergency dental care and medical repatriation. For a stay of at least one year, the student should register in the Swedish Population Register and then access the national healthcare arrangements. Verify policy terms and dates, not just the insurance certificate title.

5. Student work rights — major 2026 change.

For bachelor's/master's-level higher-education students who receive a residence-permit decision on or after 11 June 2026, paid work is generally limited to 15 hours per week during semesters. Work may be unrestricted in June, July and August. Work involving education, student representation, administration, traineeship, research or artistic research is exempt only when it takes place at the admitted university/university college or is connected to the student’s education programme. A student who works beyond the permitted limit risks permit revocation or refusal of an extension. If the decision was made before 11 June 2026, the new 15-hour cap does not apply until an extension is granted; an application submitted before 11 June but decided on/after that date is covered by the new rules. Check the decision date and actual permit status; never promise unrestricted part-time work.

6. Study progress and address notification.

For first-/second-cycle higher-education permits, the tightened acceptable progress rule from 11 June 2026 is 37.5 credits during the first year and 45 credits per year from the second year onwards. Insufficient progress can affect extension or lead to revocation. Students must notify Migrationsverket of their address within 30 days after receiving a permit and after moving, unless population registration applies. The address-notification change generally does not apply to permits granted before 11 June 2026 until extension.

7. Application fee and post-study option.

Current adult application fee shown by Migrationsverket: SEK 1,500 (children under 18: SEK 750); fees are non-refundable if refused. After completing an eligible higher-education programme lasting at least two semesters and passing all courses, a graduate may apply before the current permit expires for a permit to seek work or explore starting a business. This is up to one year after bachelor's/master's studies and one to 18 months after doctoral studies, subject to funds, insurance and other requirements. It is not automatic and does not guarantee a job.

Counsellor stop-check.

Confirm the exact programme, full-time/on-site status, tuition payment, length of requested permit, 2026 maintenance total, account ownership and family-transfer trail, insurance cover, permit grant date for work-cap rules, study-progress plan and address notification. Keep all financial and legal eligibility checks separate from the student's expectation of finding part-time work.

  • Migrationsverket — New higher-education student permit rules from 11 June 2026
  • Migrationsverket — Permit to look for work after studies
  • Migrationsverket — Extension and study progress
  • Sweden Abroad — Sri Lanka: residence-permit biometrics at Embassy of Sweden in New Delhi; Colombo honorary consulate details
  • Embassy of Sweden in New Delhi — migration section contact and biometric appointment hours

Source owner: Swedish Migration Agency (Migrationsverket). Checked 18 November 2026 against the live higher-education permit page, 2026 rule-change notice, work-rights, study-progress and post-study pages. SEK 10,656/month and SEK 1,500 adult fee are the published 2026 figures at time of review. New 15-hour semester work cap, study-progress thresholds and address notification apply based on decision date from 11 June 2026; always check the individual decision date and any exceptions. Family-member rules changed again from 1 October 2026 and require a separate review if dependants accompany the student. Recheck live requirements on filing. This counsellor reference is not a visa decision.

GERMANY

Student national visa — admission, blocked account, work rights and post-study route

Scope: Sri Lankan / other non-EU applicant planning full-time higher education in Germany. For a degree-length stay, the usual route is a national visa (Category D) for study, followed by a German residence permit after arrival. A Schengen short-stay visa is not a substitute.

1. Admission and academic eligibility.

Obtain a confirmed or eligible conditional admission from a recognised German higher-education institution. The Embassy Colombo checklist accepts an admission letter or conditional acceptance letter, but the exact conditions and whether enrolment is possible must be checked with the university. Check whether the Sri Lankan O/L and A/L qualification gives direct university access or requires a preparatory route (for example Studienkolleg); do not assume A/L completion alone guarantees direct entry. Match the language evidence to the actual language of instruction and the programme's stated requirement. Save the institution, programme, start date, language and admission conditions.

2. Financial proof — 2026 study route.

The German Embassy Colombo currently lists a blocked account of €11,904 per year (€992/month) for student visa applications. For the Colombo student checklist, the stated alternatives are a scholarship/sponsorship by a German institution or a formal declaration of commitment (Verpflichtungserklärung) by a sponsor in Germany. Federal Foreign Office general guidance describes additional evidence routes in some cases, but do not assume a Sri Lankan parent's bank statement or ordinary sponsor letter will be accepted under the Colombo checklist unless the Embassy confirms this in writing. Check the exact current checklist and Consular Services Portal for the applicant's case. The blocked account normally releases only the approved monthly amount; provider fees are additional.

3. Tuition and study costs.

Most public universities do not charge general tuition fees in many cases, but students usually pay a semester contribution (the official federal portal gives about €300 per semester on average). This is not a universal tuition promise: some states, institutions, non-consecutive programmes and private universities charge tuition. Obtain the university's current fee schedule and payment deadline. Keep tuition, semester contribution, blocked-account living funds, travel, accommodation deposit and insurance as separate budget lines.

4. Embassy Colombo application and documents.

The current Embassy Colombo student checklist asks for a valid passport (at least six months), national visa application and declaration, three recent biometric photos, copies of used passport pages, motivation letter and CV, authenticated O/L and A/L certificates (and bachelor's degree for master's applicants), admission/conditional acceptance, proof of language skills, financial proof and tuition-payment evidence if applicable. It asks for two identical application sets and original certificates. The listed visa fee is €75, payable in Sri Lankan rupees at the Embassy's stated exchange rate; refusal does not trigger a refund. The Embassy may request extra documents or an interview. Incomplete documents can lead to refusal; a complete file does not guarantee approval.

5. Processing and application route.

For student visa applications, follow the Embassy Colombo instruction to register through the Federal Foreign Office Online/Consular Services Portal; the Embassy identifies students among the categories using the online portal. Complete the online application and upload the required documents. The portal performs a preliminary digital check and may request corrections or missing documents before the in-person appointment. The applicant must still attend the Embassy in person for biometrics, presentation/verification of original documents and payment of the visa fee. Colombo advises that long-term visa processing is generally about three months after submission, but individual cases can take longer and this does not include any wait for an appointment or completion of portal pre-checks. Apply well before the course start date and do not buy non-refundable travel before the visa is issued. Appointment booking is free; the Embassy explicitly states there is no need for a visa agent and agents cannot influence or fast-track decisions.

6. Health insurance and arrival formalities.

Confirm with the university which insurance is required for enrolment and the period between arrival and activation of statutory student health insurance. Do not assume a short-trip travel policy will satisfy long-term student requirements. After arrival, register the residential address with the local registration office (Einwohnermeldeamt/Bürgeramt) within the applicable local deadline, complete university enrolment and apply to the local foreigners authority (Ausländerbehörde) for the residence permit before the entry visa expires. Local appointment/document requirements vary by city.

7. Work rights during studies.

Third-country students with a study residence permit may generally work up to 140 full working days or 280 half working days per calendar year. Under Section 16b(3) Residence Act, the working-day account also has a weekly calculation option: during the lecture period, work of up to 20 hours per calendar week may be counted under that method; the legally more favourable calculation applies. Student jobs at higher-education institutions are treated separately and are not counted against this working-day account. Do not simplify this into “20 hours plus 140 days” as two cumulative allowances. Check the current legal text and exact residence-title conditions. Part-time work is not guaranteed and must not be used as a substitute for the initial financial proof.

8. Post-study option.

After successfully completing a German degree, an eligible third-country graduate may apply for a residence permit for up to 18 months to seek qualified employment. During this job-search period, any type of work is generally permitted. This is a separate application subject to the relevant conditions; it is not automatic and does not guarantee a job or permanent residence.

9. Separate route — seeking a university place.

Do not confuse the student visa for an admitted student with the visa/residence route for seeking a place in higher education. The federal portal describes a separate route of up to nine months, with a 2026 living-funds requirement of €1,091 per month and different eligibility conditions. This amount is not the standard admitted-student blocked-account figure. Refer the case to a reviewer before recommending this route.

Counsellor stop-check.

Record the exact university and programme, recognition/admission status, whether A/L allows direct access or a preparatory route is needed, language of instruction and proof, tuition/semester charges, 2026 financial-proof route and source of funds, Colombo checklist version, appointment and processing plan, insurance, arrival registration, work-condition wording and post-study eligibility. Mark Needs review until the documents and current Embassy/Migri-equivalent authority instructions are checked. Never promise admission, visa approval, employment or free tuition.

Source owner: Federal Foreign Office / German Embassy Colombo and Federal Government “Make it in Germany” portal. Checked 18 November 2026. Embassy Colombo currently publishes €11,904/year (€992/month) and a €75 student visa fee. Students must still attend in person after portal pre-check for biometrics, original-document verification and payment. Under Section 16b(3), the 140/280 working-day account and the 20-hour weekly method are alternative counting methods, not cumulative entitlements. Financial, work and post-study figures are route-specific; recheck the Embassy checklist, Consular Services Portal and current law at filing. This is a counsellor reference, not a visa decision.

AUSTRIA

Residence Permit — Student: admission, funds, work and post-study route

Scope: Third-country national (including Sri Lankan) planning to study in Austria for more than six months. For a stay of up to six months, assess the relevant Visa C or Visa D route instead; a short-stay visa is not a substitute for a student residence permit.

1. Admission and academic eligibility.

Obtain an admission decision from an eligible Austrian higher-education institution. For public-university degree admission, the applicant generally needs an A-level/high-school qualification equivalent to the Austrian Matura and sufficient for admission to a comparable course in the country where it was earned. The university decides equivalence and may impose supplementary examinations. German-language proof may be required depending on the programme; check the institution's language and admission rules. Do not promise direct entry based only on Sri Lankan A/L passes.

2. Living funds — 2026 official reference.

For students under 24: €722.58/month (€8,670.96 for 12 months). For students aged 24 or over: €1,308.39/month (€15,700.68 for 12 months). Funds must be evidenced for 12 months in advance and normally be in an account or savings book in the applicant's name, accessible from Austria. Parent support can be combined with the student's own funds, but document the sponsor's employment/income and the transfer or support arrangement. If accommodation costs exceed €386.43/month, additional funds must be shown for the excess; also provide sufficient funds for health insurance. These are 2026 figures: recalculate at filing and assess regular financial commitments.

3. Documents and insurance.

Prepare the signed application, valid passport and copies of all passport pages, recent ICAO-compliant photo, university admission letter, proof of funds, declaration of regular expenses, proof of accommodation for at least three months, all-risk health insurance valid in Austria, and a police clearance certificate issued within the required recent period (OeAD says not older than three months at application; legalisation and translation may be required). The authority may request additional documents. Confirm exact legalisation, translation and document format with the competent Austrian representation before submission.

4. Where and when to apply.

For Sri Lankan residents, the competent mission is the Austrian Embassy in New Delhi (Austria is represented in Sri Lanka by that Embassy, supported by an Honorary Consulate in Colombo). The initial residence-permit application for a stay over six months must be submitted personally at the Embassy in New Delhi; contact NEW-DELHI-KA@bmeia.gv.at for the residence-permit appointment instructions. Do not assume the Colombo Honorary Consulate or an ordinary VFS visa counter can accept the initial residence-permit application. Submit originals plus one photocopy set; foreign public documents must be legalised as required and non-German documents may need certified German translation (English documents may be accepted in some cases—confirm before filing). OeAD advises applying at least three months before intended entry and, if possible, six months in advance. The decision period is generally 90 days, extendable by a further 90 days if additional documents are needed. After approval, apply for Visa D within three months of notification to enter Austria; collect the residence card within six months of notification and within the visa validity. Do not travel on a tourist visa expecting to convert it automatically.

5. After arrival.

Register the residential address within three working days where the registration requirement applies. Complete the required health-insurance arrangements and collect the residence permit from the competent authority with the requested documents. Keep the permit valid and renew it before expiry; maintain satisfactory study progress and respond promptly to authority requests.

6. Student work rights.

A third-country student may be granted an employment permit for work of up to 20 hours per week without a labour-market test. The prospective employer—not the student—must apply to the Austrian Public Employment Service (AMS). Work must not jeopardise the primary purpose of residence, which is study. Do not describe this as an automatic unrestricted right to work, and do not rely on future wages to meet initial proof-of-funds requirements.

7. After graduation.

Eligible graduates who successfully complete a qualifying Austrian public university, university of applied sciences or accredited private-university degree may apply for a one-time 12-month renewal of the Student residence permit to seek employment or start a business, subject to general requirements. During that job-search period, employment of up to 20 hours/week may be permitted with an AMS employment permit. A Red-White-Red Card is a separate application and is not automatic.

Counsellor stop-check: Confirm age on the relevant application date, admission/equivalence and language conditions, 12-month funds calculation, accommodation cost adjustment, sponsor evidence, police clearance legalisation/translation, insurance, consular jurisdiction, appointment lead time and the current AMS work-permit process. Never guarantee approval or a job.

Checked 18 November 2026 against OeAD's 2026 checklist, Austrian Government residence rules and Austrian Embassy New Delhi guidance for Sri Lanka. 2026 maintenance thresholds: under 24 €722.58/month; age 24+ €1,308.39/month; accommodation costs above €386.43/month trigger additional funds, plus insurance costs. 2026 application fee €218. Sri Lankan residents submit the initial over-six-month residence-permit application in person at the Austrian Embassy New Delhi; confirm appointment and document legalisation/translation instructions directly. This is a counsellor reference, not a visa decision.

SPAIN

Long-stay study authorisation — 2026 funds, insurance, work and filing

Scope: Non-EU applicant planning an eligible study or training activity in Spain for more than 90 days. The Immigration Regulation RD 1155/2024 took effect on 20 May 2025 and changed the procedure. Do not reuse pre-May-2025 checklists without checking the current rules.

1. First identify the correct route.

Eligible activities include full-time higher education at an authorised higher-education centre, qualifying post-compulsory secondary education, certain student mobility, volunteering and specified training activities. Higher-education applicants must generally be at least 17; post-compulsory secondary applicants at least 18. Confirm the programme is authorised and meets the precise legal category; a private course or language course does not automatically qualify. A stay of 90 days or less is normally handled under the short-stay Schengen rules, not a long-stay study authorisation.

2. Admission and application timing.

Obtain an official acceptance document stating the programme, start/end dates, full-time status where required and total tuition/enrolment cost. For the normal overseas route, apply through the competent Spanish consular office at least two months before studies start (unless a recognised exception applies). The long-stay visa application incorporates the initial study-stay authorisation request; the visa and authorisation have separate fees. Sri Lanka falls within the consular jurisdiction of the Embassy of Spain in New Delhi; the Colombo Honorary Consulate is not the visa decision-maker. The New Delhi Embassy's official study-visa page directs applicants to BLS Visa Application Centres, and the official BLS Spain site lists a Colombo VAC. For Sri Lankan applicants, use the Colombo BLS channel only after checking the live study-visa appointment category and checklist; the consular authority remains the Embassy of Spain in New Delhi.

3. Living funds — 2026 official IPREM reference.

For the student: 100% IPREM = €600/month in 2026 (12 months = €7,200). Add €450/month (75% IPREM) for the first accompanying family member and €300/month (50% IPREM) for each additional accompanying family member. If accommodation for the entire stay has been paid in advance and properly evidenced, the required living-funds amount may be reduced accordingly. Tuition/study fees are separate and cannot be counted as living funds. Calculate for the actual authorised stay length, not automatically 12 months.

4. Financial evidence and sponsor checks.

Evidence may be the student's own funds, qualifying family support, scholarship or other accepted resources. Consular checklists may request original bank statements (for example, six months), bank stamps/signatures and a formal notarised parental sponsorship declaration, plus sponsor identity, employment/income evidence and proof of relationship. These are examples from a consular checklist, not a universal rule for every mission. For Sri Lankan cases, obtain the competent mission's exact requirements for bank statements, translations, notarisation and legalisation before collecting documents. A sponsor letter alone does not prove funds are genuinely available.

5. Insurance, criminal record and other documents.

Provide health insurance from an insurer authorised to operate in Spain, covering the full stay with benefits comparable to Spain's public healthcare system. For stays over six months, adult applicants generally need criminal-record certificates for Spain and countries of residence during the previous five years, as required by the applicable checklist; legalisation and sworn Spanish translation may be required. Prepare the application form, passport, photo, admission letter, tuition/enrolment payment evidence, financial proof and any additional documents requested by the competent consular office. Medical certificate requirements must be checked against the current regulation and the individual route; do not add an old generic medical-certificate requirement automatically.

6. Student work rights.

For eligible higher-education study-stay authorisations, work on an employed or self-employed basis may be authorised automatically, without a separate procedure, provided the work is compatible with studies and does not exceed 30 hours per week. Other study categories may have different rules and may require separate work authorisation. Do not promise a job or treat the work allowance as guaranteed income for visa funds.

7. Family members and PhD warning.

Eligible family members of students in higher education or specialised healthcare training may apply under the student-family route, but their visas do not grant work permission. Important 2026 route change: the Spanish Consular Section in New Delhi publishes that, from August 2026, doctoral/PhD applicants are to use the researcher residence/visa route rather than the study visa route. Confirm this with the competent authority and university before advising a PhD applicant.

8. After arrival and renewals.

If the stay exceeds six months, apply personally for the Foreigner Identity Card (TIE) at the competent police station within one month of entering Spain. The New Delhi Embassy states that for stays not exceeding six months, the visa covers the full stay and no TIE is required; for stays over six months, the visa is issued for one year and the student must complete the TIE process. The Embassy's published decision period is one month from the day after submission, but it may be extended if extra documents or an interview are requested. Track study progress, passport validity, insurance and renewal deadlines.

9. Post-study option — not automatic.

A qualifying graduate who completes higher education at an authorised Spanish institution and reaches at least European Qualifications Framework Level 6 (degree level) may apply for a separate residence authorisation for job search or a business project for up to 24 months, non-extendable. This authorisation does not permit employment while it is valid; if a suitable job or business project is secured, the applicant must apply for the appropriate work/residence authorisation. It is not a guaranteed graduate visa for every course or qualification.

\n

Counsellor stop-check: Verify the exact programme category and recognition, age threshold, 2026 IPREM calculation, tuition separately, sponsor documents, insurance wording, criminal-record period, medical certificate requirements for the New Delhi checklist, translations/legalisation, Sri Lanka jurisdiction and Colombo BLS appointment category, fees and whether the 30-hour work rule applies to this category. Never guarantee approval, employment or a post-study route.

PORTUGAL

Residence visa for study — 2026 funds, AIMA residence permit, work and post-study route

Scope: Non-EU applicant (including Sri Lankan) admitted to an eligible Portuguese institution for study. Separate the entry residence visa from the residence permit issued after arrival. Higher education, secondary education, vocational training and exchange routes can have different conditions. For Sri Lankan applicants, long-stay national study visas are handled by the Embassy of Portugal in New Delhi; the Embassy's VFS partner lists VACs in India, not Colombo. Confirm the current appointment and travel arrangements with VFS/Embassy before booking travel to India. Sri Lankan public documents must first be authenticated by Sri Lanka's Ministry of Foreign Affairs and then legalised by the competent Embassy/High Commission in New Delhi as directed by the checklist.

1. Admission and correct visa route.

Obtain an admission/acceptance document from a recognised Portuguese institution, with course level, dates and tuition details. For a long-term course, apply for the appropriate residence visa through the Portuguese consular authority responsible for the applicant's place of residence. The residence visa allows entry for the purpose of applying for a residence permit; it is not the residence card itself. Confirm the current Sri Lanka filing channel, appointment process and checklist directly with the competent Portuguese mission before paying a service provider.

2. Means of subsistence — 2026 calculation.

For applications under the Embassy of Portugal in New Delhi, the current study/residence-visa checklist dated 29 September 2026 asks for bank statements for the last three months showing an available balance of at least €920 for each month of the intended stay, or an eligible term of responsibility from the receiving entity with the sponsor's ID and three months of bank statements. Employment income, grants and scholarships may be considered, but the checklist still requires the stated available bank balance. The €920 is the 2026 mainland minimum wage (RMMG), effective 1 January 2026 under Decree-Law 139/2025. This is the current New Delhi checklist instruction, not a promise that every study category or consular mission uses identical evidence; confirm the exact period, accommodation reductions/exemptions and sponsor acceptability for the applicant's course before advising. Tuition and living costs must be budgeted separately.

3. Entry visa documents and application timing.

The national-visa checklist dated 29 September 2026 includes the signed application, two photos, passport valid at least 90 days beyond the 120-day residence-visa period, 120-day travel insurance including urgent medical care/repatriation, criminal-record certificate (age 16+) issued within the previous 90 days and legalised, personal statement with Portuguese accommodation address and intended-stay details, financial evidence, and study admission/enrolment documents. Documents must be in Portuguese or English, otherwise provide an appropriate translation. The government service page lists a 60-working-day deadline and €90, while the New Delhi VFS page currently displays a national-visa fee of INR 11,900 (equivalent shown as €110) plus VFS service charge INR 3,384; these published fee figures differ, so confirm the amount payable for the applicant's category directly at booking and do not quote one total as universal. Apply early and avoid non-refundable travel before visa issuance.

4. After arrival — AIMA residence permit.

For higher-education students under Article 91, AIMA requires a valid passport, valid residence visa, signed accommodation/address declaration and supporting evidence, means of subsistence, criminal-record certificate from the country of nationality or the country where the applicant lived for more than one year before Portugal, proof of enrolment, tuition payment where applicable, and health insurance or proof of National Health Service coverage. AIMA says the higher-education student residence permit is valid for three years, renewable for equal periods, or for the study programme duration if shorter. Appointment/platform availability and current AIMA procedure must be checked; keep evidence of attempts and all appointment correspondence.

5. Health cover and accommodation.

For the visa stage, provide the travel medical insurance required by the consular checklist, including urgent medical assistance and repatriation where specified. For the AIMA residence-permit stage, provide health insurance or proof of coverage by Portugal's National Health Service. AIMA has specific accommodation evidence rules, including student-residence certificates or qualifying landlord/host declarations; a generic address letter may not be sufficient. Check the current AIMA accommodation guidance and the competent mission's document legalisation/translation rules for Sri Lankan records.

6. Student work rights.

Under Article 97 of the Portuguese immigration law, holders of a residence permit for study may carry out employed or self-employed professional activity complementary to the studies that gave rise to the visa. The AIMA Article 91 page confirms this right. The official material reviewed does not state a universal 20-hour or 30-hour weekly cap for all higher-education student-permit holders; do not insert an invented weekly limit. Secondary-school students, unpaid trainees and volunteers have different restrictions. Work is not guaranteed and expected earnings must not be counted as initial visa funds.

7. After graduation — limited one-year route.

AIMA provides a route under Article 122(1)(p) for graduates who held a student residence permit for second- or third-cycle higher education (typically master's or doctoral studies) and completed the studies: they may apply for up to one year to seek work or create a company compatible with their qualifications. This is a separate application with its own evidence, including proof of completed studies and means of subsistence. Do not promise this route to every bachelor's, language-course or vocational student.

Counsellor stop-check: Verify institution/course eligibility, consular jurisdiction for Sri Lanka, current visa checklist and fees, 2026 RMMG and the mission's actual student-funds calculation, sponsor evidence, accommodation, insurance, police-clearance authentication/translation, AIMA appointment and Article 91 eligibility, and whether Article 97 work rights apply to the exact permit category. Never promise approval, a job or a post-study permit.

The New Delhi checklist dated 29 September 2026 specifies €920 available for each month of intended stay; verify the latest checklist and accepted sponsor evidence before every application. Sri Lankan long-stay applicants are routed through the Embassy of Portugal in New Delhi and its VFS network, not a Colombo long-stay visa counter. The government service and VFS fee listings differ; confirm the payable fee at booking. AIMA procedures and fee tables can change. This is a counsellor reference, not a visa decision.

POLAND

National D visa and temporary residence for full-time studies — 2026 funds, e-MOS and work

Scope: Non-EU applicant (including Sri Lankan) admitted to eligible full-time higher education or doctoral studies in Poland. Keep the national D visa application and the later in-country temporary residence permit as two separate legal stages. Part-time, postgraduate/MBA and other non-standard study formats may not qualify for the student permit under Article 144.

1. Admission and eligible institution.

Obtain a formal admission/continuation certificate, course dates, tuition information and proof of payment where applicable. The Article 144 study residence permit is for full-time first- or second-cycle studies, long-cycle master's studies, doctoral school or specified preparatory routes. The institution must meet the approval requirements for admitting foreign students and must not be subject to a prohibition. Verify the institution against the current Ministry of Interior approved list; do not rely only on an agent's claim that the college is recognised. For first-cycle or long-cycle master's admission based on a foreign secondary-school certificate, the New Delhi checklist dated 14 May 2026 requires a NAWA Director recognition statement or a decision of the competent Polish education superintendent confirming that the certificate gives the right to apply for higher education. A Sri Lankan A/L certificate alone should not be assumed to satisfy this requirement.

2. National D visa — entry stage.

For Sri Lankan citizens/residents, the Polish Embassy in New Delhi is the competent mission; its official study-visa page explicitly lists Sri Lanka in its consular jurisdiction. That page currently describes personal submission at a VFS Visa Application Centre, registration through e-Konsulat and an additional VFS study-visa form. However, it does not clearly identify which VAC location a Sri Lanka-based applicant must attend. Do not assume there is a Polish student-visa VAC in Colombo or book travel to India without written confirmation from the Embassy/VFS of the designated submission location and current procedure. The page says applications may be lodged up to six months before travel and normally no later than 15 calendar days before travel; the current 14 May 2026 checklist says the decision may take up to 30 calendar days, so apply substantially earlier. General D-visa documents include a valid passport, completed and signed application, biometric photograph, covering letter, admission certificate, tuition-payment evidence or fee-waiver confirmation, education records, B2 language evidence, flight reservation, accommodation proof, sufficient funds and health/travel medical insurance (at least €30,000 cover unless qualifying Polish public health insurance is provided). The exact supporting-document list and appointment route must be confirmed with the Embassy; do not assume Indian document-authentication rules apply to Sri Lankan documents.

2A. Language and entry requirements — rules effective from 2025.

The New Delhi student-visa checklist dated 14 May 2026 requires evidence of proficiency in the language of instruction at B2 or higher. For English-taught programmes, IELTS 6.5, TOEFL iBT 79 and CAE 176 are described as highly recommended examples; these are not a universal replacement for the official accepted-certificate list or the university's own admission conditions. For applicants using a foreign secondary-school certificate from a non-EU/non-EFTA/non-OECD country for first-cycle or long-cycle master's studies, a positive entrance examination may also be required unless an applicable exception or international agreement applies. Confirm the exact certificate and exam requirements with the university and current Polish rules before collecting fees.

3. Financial means — temporary residence permit.

The Office for Foreigners' current MOS guidance states that, after deducting actual accommodation costs, the applicant must have more than PLN 1,010/month if there are no dependants, or PLN 823/month for the applicant and each dependant. Add the actual accommodation costs and provide funds for tuition/study fees and return travel separately. For a Sri Lankan applicant, the MOS Q&A lists PLN 2,500 as the return-travel amount for countries outside the neighbouring-country and specified EU/EEA group. Funds may be held in PLN or foreign currency, converted using the National Bank of Poland exchange rate on the last working day before filing. Do not present PLN 1,010 as an all-inclusive monthly budget: housing is added separately.

4. Residence permit application — major 2026 digital change.

Since 27 April 2026, study-purpose temporary residence applications must be submitted electronically through the new MOS portal. Apply while legally staying in Poland and no later than the last day of lawful stay. Create a new MOS account (old-system accounts do not transfer as a substitute), complete the online form, upload scans of all passport pages and supporting evidence, and enter the university's designated email. The university must complete and electronically sign its online annex before the student can submit. The applicant must electronically sign using a trusted, qualified or personal electronic signature and download the official UPO acknowledgement. The voivodeship office may later summon the applicant for fingerprints, original passport inspection and additional documents.

5. Permit period and fees.

The first study residence permit is normally granted for 15 months or the academic year/study period extended by three months, subject to the specific case; EU mobility programmes and doctoral schools have different periods. Subsequent permits may cover the remaining studies plus three months, up to the statutory maximum. The MOS Q&A lists a PLN 340 stamp duty and a standard PLN 100 residence-card fee; eligible students may qualify for a reduced card fee of PLN 50. Pay the separate accounts specified by the competent authority and confirm the live fees before payment.

6. Student work rights.

A non-EU student staying on a Polish national visa issued for full-time studies, or on a study temporary residence permit, may work without a separate work permit while the relevant legal conditions are met. The Office for Foreigners also confirms this can apply while waiting for the study residence-permit decision if the student applied lawfully, correctly and on time (or corrected formal gaps within the deadline). This exemption is not a blanket right for every course or visa: part-time studies and other residence categories need separate assessment. Maintain full-time student status and do not treat work as guaranteed or as a substitute for initial funds.

7. Dropping out and after graduation.

If the student ceases studying, they must notify the competent voivode in writing within 15 working days; the permit's basis may then cease. Graduates of full-time studies at a Polish higher-education institution may work without a work permit, but must still have a valid legal basis to stay. Eligible graduates may apply once for a nine-month temporary residence permit to seek employment or start a business. This is a separate application and is not automatic; confirm eligibility and current application procedure at graduation.

Counsellor stop-check: Verify full-time study category, approved institution, NAWA/education-superintendent recognition where applicable, B2 language proof and any entrance exam, New Delhi consular jurisdiction for Sri Lanka, exact VFS submission location in writing, current e-Konsulat/VFS process, 30-day prudent processing allowance, tuition paid, €30,000 insurance wording, visa-stage funds (PLN 1,010/month plus full accommodation cost and PLN 2,500 return travel under the New Delhi checklist), residence-permit funds calculation, translations/legalisation, university MOS annex, electronic signature/UPO, lawful-stay deadline and work exemption category. Never promise visa approval, a job or a residence card before the official decision.

Sri Lanka is under New Delhi consular jurisdiction, but the public page does not name the exact VFS centre Sri Lankan applicants must attend; obtain written confirmation before travel. The 14 May 2026 checklist gives a 30-calendar-day decision period while the general page says 15 days (extendable to 30); use 30 days for planning. The checklist's B2 language and NAWA recognition requirements are material screening points. PLN 1,010/PLN 823 residence-permit maintenance amounts exclude actual accommodation; return travel and tuition are separate. This is a counsellor reference, not a visa decision.

BELGIUM

Student D visa and A-card residence — 2026/27 funds, Sri Lanka filing, work and post-study

Scope: Non-EEA applicant (including Sri Lankan) admitted to full-time higher education at a Belgian-recognised institution or an eligible preparatory year. For the ordinary higher-education route, full-time study generally means at least 54 ECTS credits; limited exceptions exist for final-year students and documented circumstances. A D visa is the entry visa; after arrival the student must register locally and obtain the limited-duration A-card. Private/non-recognised institutions and non-degree courses need separate eligibility assessment.

1. Admission and institution check.

Obtain an official admission/registration certificate from a Belgian higher-education institution recognised, organised or subsidised by the public authorities, for a full-time course or qualifying preparatory year. The certificate should identify the programme and status. Check the institution's recognition and the exact study category with the competent community/authority; admission alone does not guarantee the visa. Applicants for a non-recognised private institution face extra scrutiny and additional documents, including definitive enrolment and tuition payment, prior academic results, a detailed study/career project and language evidence.

2. Living funds — academic year 2026/27.

The Belgian Immigration Office sets the student means-of-subsistence minimum at €1,062 net/month for 2026/27. For a 12-month blocked-account route this equals €12,744. Accepted evidence includes a blocked account with the institution or accepted financial-services provider, an eligible scholarship/loan certificate, or a qualifying Annex 32 formal undertaking. The Immigration Office currently says it accepts irrevocable-transfer certificates from Studely and Ready Study Go International for the specialist financial-services route; verify the live list before paying a provider. Funds assessment is individual. Tuition, travel and actual living costs remain separate budget items.

3. Annex 32 sponsor — eligibility and evidence.

A sponsor letter or bank balance alone is not enough. The guarantor must be at least 18 and meet one of the permitted status/relationship tests: Belgian citizen; qualifying EU citizen with residence rights; third-country national with unlimited residence in Belgium/another EU Member State; or a family member up to the third degree of the student. The sponsor must demonstrate regular, sufficient and stable income; irregular income is not accepted. The sponsor's own household and other supported students affect the required income. For 2026/27, the Immigration Office publishes a minimum sponsor income reference of €3,235.88 net/month for one supported student before applicable household/dependant adjustments. This replaces the older €3,279.47 figure in the previous draft; the 2026 indexed reference shown on the official mission page is €2,173.88 plus €1,062.00. Use the current Annex 32 form and obtain mission-specific instructions for signing, legalisation, relationship proof and income documents.

4. Sri Lanka — where to lodge the D visa.

The Belgian Embassy confirms there is no Belgian visa section in Sri Lanka. Switzerland represents Belgium in Colombo only for specified short-stay C visas; this does not cover long-stay D visas. All other long-stay applications, including student D visas, must be lodged through the VFS visa application centre in New Delhi. Contact the Belgian Embassy in New Delhi at newdelhi.visa@diplobel.fed.be to confirm current appointment, submission and payment instructions before travel. Do not send a student D visa application to the Swiss Embassy in Colombo or assume the Honorary Consulate can decide it.

5. Sri Lanka D-visa documents and fees.

The Sri Lanka-specific Belgian page links a dedicated student D-visa checklist. Core items include the completed/signed visa form, passport and copies of visa/stamp pages, recent photographs, proof of admission/registration and access qualification, proof of funds, health insurance covering the stay, medical certificate from an accepted doctor, and national police-clearance certificate for applicants aged 18 or over. Official Sri Lankan documents (including education and police records) require the stated legalisation process; documents not in German, English, French or Dutch require a sworn translation into one of those languages. The published long-stay D-visa handling fee is €250 from 1 July 2026, non-refundable. A separate federal administrative contribution may also apply to student D applications and is distinct from the visa handling fee; check the current IBZ fee table for the applicant’s age/category and retain the official payment proof before submission. Scholarship exemptions may apply to specified fees; verify the applicant's case.

6. After arrival — municipal registration and A-card.

After entering Belgium, register with the municipality of the place of residence within 8 working days and follow the local address/residence check. The municipality issues an A-card for a limited period, normally up to one academic year plus one month, renewable if conditions and study progress are met. Keep the D visa, admission, accommodation/address evidence, insurance and original documents available for the municipal process. A D visa is not a substitute for the A-card or registration.

7. Renewal and study progress.

Apply for renewal at the municipality no later than 15 days before the A-card expires. Authorities assess financial resources, enrolment and academic progress. Insufficient study progress can lead to refusal or withdrawal. Track credits and attendance with the institution and consult the current Immigration Office progress rules for the student's programme and academic year; do not rely only on a generic number of credits from an older EU summary.

8. Student work rights.

During the academic year, eligible non-EEA students with valid student residence status may work up to 20 hours per week, provided the work does not interfere with studies. During official school holidays, work may exceed 20 hours per week. Work is limited to the validity of the residence document and any applicable conditions; the residence card's labour-market annotation must be checked. Students cannot work during class or programme activities, and the Flemish guidance states they may not work in the summer before their first academic year starts. Do not promise a job or count future wages as initial visa funds.

9. After graduation — orientation year.

Eligible graduates may apply to extend their stay for a maximum of 12 months to look for work or set up a business, with the aim of moving to an employment or self-employment residence status. Apply within the applicable post-study window and prove sufficient means of subsistence and health cover. This is a separate application, not an automatic extra year or a job guarantee.

Counsellor stop-check: Confirm recognised institution and at least 54 ECTS full-time study (or documented exception), exact 2026/27 €1,062/month funds route (€12,744 for 12 months), Annex 32 sponsor eligibility and current €3,235.88/month baseline for one student before household adjustments, New Delhi VFS jurisdiction, €250 D-visa handling fee plus the separate current IBZ administrative contribution (check live fee table; do not combine the two), Sri Lankan document legalisation and sworn translations, medical/PCC validity, insurance, municipal 8-working-day registration, A-card work annotation, renewal progress and the 12-month orientation-year eligibility/deadline. Never guarantee approval or employment.
