The 2026/27 living-funds threshold is €1,062 net/month; the published Sri Lanka D-visa handling fee is €250, with a separate additional contribution potentially payable. Confirm all fees, Annex 32 sponsor calculations and VFS arrangements at filing. This is a counsellor reference, not a visa decision.

DENMARK

Higher-education student residence permit — 2026 funds, SIRI process, work and post-study

Scope: Non-EU/EEA applicant (including Sri Lankan) admitted full-time to an eligible Danish higher-education programme. Part-time study does not qualify under this route. A student residence permit is not a general work visa; the work right is limited and tied to the programme/permit conditions.

1. Admission and programme eligibility.

Obtain admission to a full-time higher-education programme at a publicly accredited/state-approved or otherwise SIRI-eligible institution. The programme must be approved by a state authority or meet the applicable advisory-statement route. The applicant must understand the language of instruction well enough to participate. A preparatory course qualifies only if it is an approved prerequisite for a specific higher-education programme, lasts no more than one year and the applicant has also been admitted to the linked programme. Check the institution and exact programme status on SIRI before accepting a deposit.

2. Financial self-support — 2026.

For higher-education students, SIRI's 2026 self-support amount is DKK 7,426 per month for the intended study period, up to a maximum of 12 months: DKK 89,112. Evidence must be liquid funds available to the applicant. The bank statement must be no more than 30 days old when the application is submitted and show the applicant's full name, statement date, balance and currency. A bank statement in a parent/other person's name or a simple third-party support letter is not accepted as the applicant's own funds. A granted scholarship or student loan may qualify. If tuition is payable and the first semester's tuition has already been paid, SIRI accepts that as evidence of sufficient funds under its published rule; keep the official tuition receipt. Tuition and actual living costs should still be budgeted separately.

3. SIRI fee, application form and documents.

The 2026 SIRI study-case processing fee is DKK 3,060. Create a case order ID and pay before submitting; attach the payment receipt. The normal processing time is 2 months, but may reach 3 months if further information is needed. For the usual higher-education route, use ST1: the Danish institution completes its part first and sends the applicant the link/details; the student then completes their part. Prepare copies of all passport pages, including blank pages and covers, admission/enrolment evidence, funds evidence where required, and any required translations. Guest/exchange students also provide home-institution enrolment proof. PhD applicants use the separate PHD1 form.

4. Sri Lanka submission and biometrics.

For Sri Lankan residents, Denmark's official mission information says the Norwegian Visa Application Centre (VFS Global) in Colombo accepts Danish residence-permit applications; the Embassy of Norway in New Delhi represents Denmark for residence-permit processing. Follow the current VFS checklist and appointment instructions for the long-stay residence application, not a short-stay Schengen tourist-visa checklist. After an online application, biometrics (facial photograph and fingerprints) must normally be recorded within 14 days at the designated mission/application centre. If no appointment is available within the deadline, notify SIRI as instructed and retain proof of the booked appointment.

5. Student work rights — check the programme approval.

Students in state-approved higher-education programmes receive a limited work permit: up to 90 hours per month from September to May, and full-time during June, July and August. The work permit is generally also valid during an eligible job-seeking period. A non-state-approved higher-education student who applied on or after 2 May 2025 is generally not granted a student work permit and has no right to work under this student route (limited exceptions apply, including specified Turkish Association Agreement cases). The residence card states the work right. Illegal work or exceeding the permitted hours can lead to fines and revocation/refusal of the residence permit. Do not promise a job or use expected wages as initial visa funds.

6. Major rule change from 1 October 2026 — family and post-study.

The application submission date, not the decision date, determines the transition rule. For third-country students in state-approved professional bachelor, bachelor, part-time master and master programmes (including specified Erasmus joint programmes): applications submitted before 1 October 2026 may retain up to 3 years of post-study job-seeking permission; applications submitted on or after 1 October 2026 generally receive up to 1 year. For new student applications on/after that date, a spouse/cohabiting/registered partner and children generally cannot receive an accompanying-family permit under this student route. For students who applied before 1 October, children under 18 may still apply after that date; spouses/partners must also have applied before 1 October. PhD students are exempt from these changes: up to 3 years' job seeking and accompanying family may remain available. Specified Turkish Association Agreement cases have separate protections. Do not use the old 3-year / family entitlement as a general promise.

7. After graduation — 2026 transition and exact job-seeking period.

Do not quote a single universal duration. For eligible state-approved Danish degree programmes, SIRI's current higher-education page says qualifying applications submitted before 1 October 2026 generally receive a 6-month or 3-year job-seeking permit, depending on the programme/category; applications submitted on or after 1 October 2026 generally receive a 6-month or 1-year permit. The 1 October change applies to third-country professional bachelor, bachelor, part-time master and master programmes (including specified joint programmes); PhD graduates generally retain up to 3 years. Non-state-approved programme cases have separate transition rules, including no job-seeking permit for applications from 2 May 2025 in the general rule. SIRI may grant the job-seeking period with the original permit if eligible and passport validity allows; check the actual decision/card. During job seeking, the same limited student work rights normally continue; some eligible degree graduates can apply for an unrestricted work permit after completion.

9. Study-period extensions and passport validity.

The study permit normally covers the programme's standard/nominal duration, provided the student actively participates. If studies run late, an extension may be granted for up to 1 additional year in ordinary cases; pregnancy or parental leave can support a further extension, up to 2 years total. Apply no earlier than 4 months before expiry and no later than the permit expiry date. The permit may be shortened to end 3 months before passport expiry, so check passport validity before filing.

8. Residence card and travel.

After approval and biometrics, follow SIRI's instructions for registering a Danish address/CPR and receiving the residence card. A Danish residence permit allows short visits in the other Schengen states within the usual 90-days-in-180-days limit, but does not authorise employment in another Schengen country. Giving up the Danish address or staying abroad for more than six consecutive months can cause the permit to lapse, subject to specific exceptions and dispensation rules.

Counsellor stop-check: Confirm state approval/full-time status and language readiness; calculate DKK 7,426/month and check applicant-owned liquid funds/statement age or first-semester tuition receipt; use the correct SIRI fee and ST1/PHD1 form; confirm the Norwegian VFS Colombo route and Embassy of Norway New Delhi representation; classify work rights by programme and application date; apply the 1 October 2026 family/job-seeking transition rules (including 6-month vs 1-year vs 3-year categories); check standard programme duration, extension eligibility, passport expiry and actual permit wording. Never guarantee approval, employment or a fixed post-study period.

Family and post-study rules changed on 1 October 2026; application submission date controls. Confirm live VFS instructions and exact programme classification before filing.

NORWAY

Study residence permit — 2026/27 funds, Sri Lanka filing, work and post-study route

Scope: Non-EU/EEA applicant (including Sri Lankan) admitted full-time to an eligible Norwegian university/university college or accredited vocational school for studies lasting more than three months. Different rules apply to folk high schools, Bible schools, exchange visits and other categories. Check the correct UDI category before quoting funds or family rights.

1. Admission and study requirements.

For the university/university-college route, obtain full-time admission to an accredited institution and programme. The student must have somewhere to live and meet UDI's assessment that return to the home country after study is likely. For vocational school, the school must be accredited/approved and the programme cannot be online. Part-time admission does not qualify for the ordinary student permit. A study permit is required for studies lasting more than three months.

2. Living funds — academic year 2026/27.

For university/university college and eligible vocational-school students, UDI's published 2026/27 living-funds amount is NOK 15,488 per month, or NOK 170,368 for the academic year. One-semester figures are NOK 77,440 for autumn 2026 and NOK 92,928 for spring 2027. Funds can consist of student loans, grants, the applicant's own funds in a Norwegian bank account or the institution's deposit account, or a combination. A part-time job offer already received may count under UDI's conditions. Contact the institution about its deposit-account process and transfer deadline. Tuition fees, if charged, must be covered separately; do not treat the living-funds figure as including tuition.

3. Tuition, semester fee and total budget.

Tuition for non-EU/EEA students is institution- and programme-specific; Norway's public institutions are no longer subject to a universal statutory tuition-fee requirement, and some institutions/programmes charge tuition while others have exemptions (for example eligible exchange/scholarship/doctoral categories). Obtain the exact written tuition invoice, payment deadline and refund terms from the institution. Students normally also pay a semester fee to the student welfare organisation, approximately NOK 1,000 per semester. Build a separate budget for tuition, living costs, travel, insurance and application/VFS fees.

4. Application fee and process.

For a first-time study permit applicant aged 18 or over, UDI's published fee is NOK 5,400; for applicants under 18 it is NOK 2,700. Register the application online in UDI's Application Portal, select the correct study category, pay the fee and follow the personalised document checklist. Prepare passport, admission, funds and tuition evidence, accommodation details, and any required translations. A VFS service fee and courier fee are additional where applicable. The Norwegian Embassy in New Delhi currently publishes VFS Colombo service fees of €17 service fee plus €5.50 courier (July 2026 reference); confirm the current LKR amount and optional charges with VFS before booking.

5. Sri Lanka — submission and processing.

Sri Lankan citizens/residents submit Norway residence-permit applications through the Norway Visa Application Centre (VFS Global), Colombo, by appointment. Applications are forwarded to the Norwegian Embassy in New Delhi and UDI for processing. Register and pay online before attending; print and sign the application cover letter and bring the passport and all checklist documents. Do not buy non-refundable travel tickets or travel before the permit/entry visa is issued. Processing times vary by case and category; check UDI's live waiting-time page rather than promising a fixed duration.

6. After arrival — residence card appointment.

If the student needs an entry visa, wait for the visa sticker before travelling. After arrival, book a police appointment to obtain the residence card; the appointment should normally be within the first seven days in Norway or the first available appointment. Keep the passport, admission and decision letter. Follow the local registration/identity-number instructions and maintain the Norwegian address required by the permit.

7. Student work rights and restrictions.

Once the study permit is granted, the student is automatically allowed to work up to 20 hours per week during the semester, including remote work, and full-time during holidays. Full-time work during the academic year may be permitted only where it is part of the education plan and UDI approves it. The student cannot be self-employed or run a business under the student permit. Do not start work before the permit is granted, exceed the allowed hours or assume work is guaranteed. Work breaches can lead to revocation and affect future applications.

8. Family and permanent residence.

The spouse/cohabitant and children may usually apply for family immigration when the principal student holds a university/university-college study permit; UDI warns it may not be able to process their applications at the same time. Family rights are not available for every study category (for example, folk high-school students cannot bring family under that route). Time spent on a study permit does not count towards the residence period for permanent residence. Do not promise permanent settlement from study alone.

9. After graduation — skilled job-seeker permit.

A student who completes their education in Norway may apply for a job-seeker residence permit to look for skilled work, but must apply before the current study permit expires; UDI advises applying at least one month before expiry. The applicant must have completed the qualifying Norwegian education, intend to seek skilled employment and show their own funds of at least NOK 28,448 per month (NOK 341,373 per year reference). The permit can be granted for a maximum of one year. It allows full-time or part-time work while searching for relevant skilled employment, including jobs not requiring skilled qualifications, but not self-employment. It is a separate application and not an automatic extension or job guarantee.

10. Maximum time on a student permit.

UDI states that a person can hold a study permit for a total maximum of two years under the ordinary study-permit category. Do not assume repeated course changes or renewals allow indefinite residence on student status. Confirm the student's previous study-permit history and exact permit category with UDI before planning a longer pathway.

Counsellor stop-check: Confirm study category and accreditation, full-time admission, 2026/27 NOK 170,368 (or correct semester amount), source/accessibility of funds and institution deposit deadline, tuition invoice and semester fee, NOK 5,400 adult application fee plus VFS charges, Colombo appointment and New Delhi processing, accommodation, seven-day police appointment, 20-hour work cap, family eligibility by category, two-year maximum study-permit period, and the one-month-before-expiry job-seeker deadline/funds. Never promise employment, permanent residence or permit approval.

Austria — Residence Permit: Student

Who may qualify: Third-country nationals admitted to an eligible Austrian university, university of applied sciences, accredited private university or teacher-training institution. Some non-degree routes qualify only when they meet the statutory programme/ECTS conditions; a language-only course is not enough.

  • 2026 funds, per month: under age 24 — €722.58; age 24 or older — €1,308.39. Couples — €2,064.12; each child — €201.88. Evidence is normally required for 12 months and funds must be accessible from Austria.
  • Accommodation adjustment: if monthly accommodation costs exceed €386.43, additional funds must be shown. Adequate health insurance and accommodation are separate requirements.
  • Funds evidence: applicant's account/savings, scholarship, qualifying guarantee or documented sponsor support may be combined. Authorities may request six months of bank statements and evidence of the funds' origin.
  • Work: employer must obtain an AMS employment permit before work starts. Student employment up to 20 hours/week can be permitted without a labour-market test; this is not an automatic unrestricted work right.
  • After graduation: eligible graduates may apply once to extend the student residence permit for 12 months to seek work or establish a business, subject to conditions.

OeAD — Residence Permit Student and 2026 funds · Austrian Migration Portal — study and work rules

Source owner: OeAD / Austrian Federal Government. Checked 13 November 2026. Recheck the applicant's exact age, accommodation cost, insurance and programme eligibility before calculating funds.

Switzerland — Non-EU/EFTA student residence

Important: Switzerland's student residence authorisation is decided by the competent canton. There is no single nationwide student bank-balance figure or automatic work entitlement for every student. Treat the canton's written requirements as decisive.

  • Who needs which visa: Sri Lankan nationals need a national visa (type D) for a study stay over 90 days. A Schengen type C visa is for short stays up to 90 days and is not the correct long-term student route. The D visa is issued only after the competent cantonal authority authorises the stay.
  • Where Sri Lankan applicants apply: submit the national-visa application to the Embassy of Switzerland in Colombo. The Embassy's 2026 student checklist requires an appointment and personal appearance; prepare three D-visa forms, four photos, current/previous passports, academic certificates, CV, a signed motivation/future-plan letter, university acceptance and tuition-payment evidence, six months' sponsor/bank evidence, sponsor ID and relationship proof, and language evidence (IELTS/TOEFL or the relevant German/French/Italian certificate). Sinhala/Tamil documents require official translation into English, German, French or Italian. The Embassy may request additional documents.
  • Funds: the Sri Lanka student checklist asks for proof that the student/sponsor can cover study and living costs, including six months of bank transactions and a bank balance letter; it does not publish one fixed CHF amount. The SEM's CHF 30/day figure is an entry/short-stay reference for valid student-permit holders, not a universal long-term student-residence budget. Obtain the exact amount, accepted evidence and any canton-specific rules in writing from the intended canton before advising a client.
  • Processing: the Embassy's current national-visa page estimates approximately 8–12 weeks, varying by canton and case. The older February 2026 student checklist says approximately three months; counsel clients to apply early and not promise a fixed decision date.
  • Visa fee — verification required: the February 2026 student checklist says “visa processing fee: free of charge”, while the Embassy's fee schedule valid from 26 May 2026 lists an adult visa at CHF 82 / LKR 34,030. This conflict must be confirmed directly with the Embassy before quoting or collecting a fee; do not hardcode either amount as the guaranteed student fee.
  • After arrival: register with the residents' registration office / competent cantonal authority within 14 days of arrival and apply for the appropriate residence permit. Students must hold admission from a state-recognised institution and prove sufficient means. Permits may be issued for the study period or one year and renewed subject to continuing eligibility; apply for extension at least 14 days before expiry. For stays over three months, Swiss health insurance is generally required.
  • Work during study: for third-country students at a university or university of applied sciences, the canton may approve work no earlier than six months after the course starts, normally up to 15 hours per week outside holidays. The university must confirm the work will not delay studies and the employer must submit the application. This is not an automatic right; check the canton and permit conditions.
  • After graduation: graduates of recognised/accredited Swiss higher-education institutions may obtain up to six months to seek qualified employment, subject to sufficient funds, suitable accommodation and the applicable conditions. Employment admission remains subject to the rules for third-country nationals; this is not a guaranteed job or automatic work permit.

Swiss Embassy — Sri Lanka national-visa jurisdiction · Swiss Embassy — current student checklist and processing · 2026 Sri Lanka student D-visa checklist (PDF) · Swiss Embassy — national visa fees · SEM — student work and post-study job search · SEM — entry and financial means

Source owner: Swiss Federal Department of Foreign Affairs (FDFA), State Secretariat for Migration (SEM), and cantonal migration authorities. Checked 18 November 2026. Financial amount and fee must be confirmed with the relevant canton / Embassy; do not use the CHF 30/day entry reference as the long-term visa funds requirement.

Czech Republic — Long-term residence for studies

Who may qualify: Third-country nationals enrolled in a study category recognised under Czech law, commonly an accredited university study programme. Not every private course or language course automatically qualifies as “studies” for this permit.

  • Core documents: passport, application, confirmation of studies/enrolment, accommodation proof, proof of funds, photograph and comprehensive medical insurance; further documents may be requested. The official portal lists a standard 60-day processing time, subject to suspension and legal exceptions.
  • Funds: the Interior Ministry calculates the required amount using the statutory subsistence minimum and duration of stay. Its published example still uses CZK 3,130/month, a figure dated from 1 January 2023. Do not reuse that example as a verified 2026 amount: recalculate against the current statutory figure and the applicant's exact stay dates. Certain school or public-institution commitments/grants can substitute for some evidence under the stated conditions.
  • Work: a valid study residence permit can provide free access to the labour market for qualifying study categories. For a full-time accredited university programme, a separate work permit for a specific job is generally not required; employer reporting and other employment rules still apply. Check the exact course and permit category.
  • After graduation: eligible successful university graduates may apply for a residence permit to seek employment or start a business, for up to nine months, before their current permit expires.

Czech Interior Ministry — Study residence permit · Czech Interior Ministry — Proof of funds · Study in Czechia — Work during studies

Source owner: Czech Ministry of the Interior / official Study in Czechia portal. Checked 13 November 2026. Financial amount requires a fresh statutory check before client quotation.

Latvia — Student Long-Stay D Visa and Temporary Residence Permit

Route: for a study stay exceeding 90 days, a non-EU student admitted to an accredited Latvian education institution needs the institution's OCMA-approved invitation, a national long-stay (D) visa for entry, and then the study residence permit formalities with the Office of Citizenship and Migration Affairs (OCMA). The D visa is normally valid for 90 days in this student route; it is not the long-term residence permit itself.

  • Sri Lankan applicants — mission and verification: Sri Lankan nationals apply for the study D visa through the Embassy of Latvia in New Delhi, with submission via VFS Global Sri Lanka in Colombo. Before booking, complete the mandatory visa-support document verification through Hello Verify India and obtain the transaction number; the Embassy states a VFS appointment will not be granted without it. Current VFS Sri Lanka instructions say appointments are booked online only; do not use the discontinued email appointment address. Check the live VFS portal and mission instructions before every application because submission arrangements can change.
  • 2026 living funds: OCMA's official subsistence table, effective 1 April 2026, sets the general monthly minimum at €780 for cases not assigned a special category. For a student residence case use the current OCMA/mission checklist to confirm the required period and whether additional family-member amounts apply. Do not treat this as tuition, insurance or travel money; budget those separately.
  • Study D visa documents: passport (valid at least three months beyond intended departure from Latvia/Schengen and with two blank pages), completed/printed/signed national visa application, recent 35 × 45 mm photograph, OCMA-approved invitation, study/admission evidence and prior education records, proof of accommodation, sponsor evidence (if parent-funded, notarised sponsorship declaration plus sponsor employment/payslips or self-employment/company and bank evidence), proof of funds, travel medical insurance for the intended 90-day visa period with at least €42,600 cover, and Hello Verify confirmation. The Embassy may request extra documents or interview.
  • Visa fee and processing: the New Delhi Embassy student page lists a €90 national visa fee. It states 15 days after all stipulated documents are received, extendable to 60 days if additional information or scrutiny is required. VFS service, courier and video-conference charges are separate and non-refundable; confirm current LKR charges at booking.
  • After arrival / residence permit: students must apply for the study residence permit with OCMA after entering Latvia. Keep the university/OCMA invitation, study agreement, passport, proof of subsistence and health insurance ready; comply with OCMA's appointment, biometrics, medical and residence formalities. The residence permit is the legal basis for the longer study stay. Confirm the current application fee and decision-time option directly with OCMA rather than quoting an old fee schedule.
  • Health insurance: maintain valid health insurance for the planned stay. The national Study in Latvia portal states that the long-stay visa policy must cover at least €42,600 for emergency medical care, hospital treatment, transport and repatriation. Check when Latvian state health coverage begins and avoid gaps.
  • Work during studies: the national Study in Latvia portal says full-time bachelor's students may work up to 20 hours/week during studies. Full-time master's and doctoral students may work full-time during study breaks; the exact conditions and any employer/work-permit procedure must be checked against the current permit and OCMA rules. Do not promise a job or tell students to rely on wages to fund tuition.
  • After graduation: the national Study in Latvia guidance describes an additional four-month period for bachelor's graduates to apply for further studies or seek work. Master's and doctoral graduates may request an additional nine-month job-search extension, subject to OCMA approval; it is not automatic. Confirm the application deadline and current legal basis before client advice.
  • Counsellor checks: confirm the exact accredited institution and course, invitation number, Hello Verify completion, VFS online appointment, latest sponsor/bank evidence rules, current €780 subsistence threshold, insurance dates/coverage, and post-arrival OCMA procedure. Explain clearly that visa issuance, residence-permit approval, and permission to work are separate decisions/conditions.

Latvia Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lankan student visa checklist · Latvia Embassy — Student D visa / Hello Verify · VFS Global Sri Lanka — current student appointment and submission information · OCMA — Necessary subsistence (effective 1 April 2026) · Study in Latvia — Work and post-study information · European Commission — Student in Latvia · Latvian MFA — Long-stay visa processing periods

Source owner: Latvian Ministry of Foreign Affairs / Embassy in New Delhi, Office of Citizenship and Migration Affairs (OCMA), VFS Global Sri Lanka and national Study in Latvia portal. Checked 18 November 2026. €780 is the OCMA general monthly subsistence level effective 1 April 2026; confirm the exact student-period calculation and fees with OCMA/Embassy before client quotation.

Lithuania — Student National Visa and Temporary Residence Permit (third-country nationals)

Route: a non-EU student admitted to a recognised Lithuanian higher-education/research institution applies for the study-based temporary residence permit (TRP) through the Migration Department's MIGRIS system. The institution normally creates a mediation letter/invitation in MIGRIS. A national visa may be needed for entry or where the applicant's exact study route requires it; do not assume a visa alone replaces the study TRP for a long-term degree course. Confirm the correct application route with the institution and Lithuanian mission before the student travels.

  • Eligibility and admission: applicant must be enrolled in an eligible higher-education programme or doctoral studies. Confirm the institution and programme are eligible and obtain the official mediation letter number. The Migration Department's legal basis is Article 46 of the Law on the Legal Status of Foreigners.
  • 2026 financial requirement: the student funds formula is 0.5 of the Lithuanian minimum monthly wage (MMA) per month. Lithuania's official 2026 MMA is €1,153, so the indicative student living-funds baseline is €576.50 per month. Funds must cover the relevant permit period; where a permit is issued for longer than one year, the funds are required for at least one year. The institution's mediation letter should certify sufficient funds; if it does not, submit separate proof such as bank statements. Return-travel funds may also be required. Tuition, insurance and travel should be budgeted separately.
  • Documents: valid passport; institution admission and MIGRIS mediation letter; proof of sufficient legal funds and return travel where required; valid health insurance; accommodation/residence declaration; and criminal-record evidence or other supporting documents if requested. Documents may need apostille/legalisation and Lithuanian translation depending on issuing country and document type. Follow the current MIGRIS checklist and appointment instructions for the applicant's location.
  • Application and fees: submit the TRP application and supporting documents through MIGRIS, then attend the required in-person appointment for identity verification/biometrics at the designated service provider or Migration Department office. Fees and processing times depend on the application category and standard/urgent handling; check the live Migration Department fee schedule before quoting a client. Do not reuse an old fee amount.
  • Permit duration and study progress: a study TRP may be issued/renewed for the duration of studies but for no longer than two years at a time. Renewal requires continuing enrolment and study progress; the law generally requires at least 40 academic credits in the preceding study year, unless justified reasons are accepted. A cumulative maximum of eight years for study-based TRPs applies under the 2026 amendments to the relevant permit cohort.
  • Work rights — 2026 change: for study TRPs issued under the rules effective from 22 May 2026, first- and second-year bachelor's students may work no more than 20 hours/week (excluding holidays and institution-specified practical training). Third- and fourth-year bachelor's students, master's students, doctoral students and residents may work up to 40 hours/week under the Migration Department's published explanation. Confirm the permit issue date, study year and programme level before advising; do not apply a blanket 20-hour rule to every student.
  • Family members: under the 2026 amendments, master's-level students and doctoral students/residents may bring eligible family members immediately, subject to the applicable family-reunification conditions. Other students generally face a two-year lawful-residence and permit-validity requirement before family reunification. Verify the family member's category and current legal conditions.
  • After graduation: the law allows a final-year study TRP to be renewed at the student's request for a period of up to six months after the expected end of studies. Eligible graduates may also apply for a separate 12-month temporary residence permit for job search/self-employment. These are distinct routes; confirm the exact MIGRIS category, eligibility and filing deadline before advising. Neither guarantees employment.
  • Sri Lankan counsellor checks: confirm the institution's mediation letter, the correct D-visa/TRP sequence and where the applicant must lodge biometrics, the 2026 €576.50 monthly calculation and return funds, document legalisation/translation, insurance dates, the 22 May 2026 work-hour cohort, and current fee/processing time. Do not promise that part-time work will fund tuition or living costs.

Lithuanian Migration Department — official immigration guidance · MIGRIS — application portal · Migration Department — 2026 legal amendments (work, family and permit duration) · Migration Department — student work-hour rules from 22 May 2026 · Lithuanian Ministry of Social Security and Labour — 2026 minimum wage €1,153 · Law on the Legal Status of Foreigners — Article 46

Source owner: Lithuanian Migration Department, Ministry of Social Security and Labour, and e-Seimas legislation. Checked 18 November 2026. €576.50 is calculated as half the 2026 MMA (€1,153); verify the applicant's permit period and any additional return-travel/family requirements in MIGRIS. The 22 May 2026 amendments apply according to the relevant permit issue date and student category.

Estonia — Student D visa and Temporary Residence Permit (third-country nationals)

Route depends on course duration: Sri Lankan nationals need the correct Estonian long-stay route. A D visa can cover a stay of up to 365 days in a 12-month period. Students enrolled in a full-degree programme who need to stay longer must obtain a temporary residence permit (TRP) for study; the D visa is not a substitute for a long-term residence permit.

  • Admission / eligibility: secure admission and the official invitation/confirmation from an eligible Estonian educational institution. For university and professional higher-education programmes, the applicant must demonstrate the required language ability in the language of instruction. The institution should advise whether the applicant applies for a D visa first and submits the study TRP application in Estonia or whether a TRP application can be lodged at a foreign mission for the particular case.
  • Where Sri Lankan applicants apply: Germany represents Estonia in Colombo for Schengen short-stay C visas only; the German Embassy in Colombo does not accept Estonian long-stay D-visa applications. The Estonian Embassy in New Delhi handles long-stay D visa applications for Sri Lankan residents. Book the long-term visa appointment and confirm current submission arrangements directly with the Embassy before travel.
  • D visa financial means: the Estonian Ministry of Foreign Affairs lists €880 per month for studies. The applicant must also provide evidence of income for the three months immediately before application, showing amount, regularity and source, and demonstrate sufficient means for the actual purpose and period of stay. Do not treat the €880/month D-visa figure as tuition-inclusive; budget tuition, travel and insurance separately.
  • D visa fee: the Ministry of Foreign Affairs currently lists a state fee of €120 for examination of a long-stay visa application. Check the payment method and any applicable exemption with the mission where the application is lodged.
  • D visa documents: passport issued within the previous 10 years, at least two blank pages and valid at least three months after the requested visa expires; completed and signed application; recent ICAO-compliant 35 × 45 mm photo; travel medical insurance covering the requested period (or until Estonian Tervisekassa coverage begins); proof of funds/income for the preceding three months; family/biographical data; evidence of study purpose such as institution acceptance/invitation; travel booking/transport evidence; and biometrics. Foreign public documents must be legalised or apostilled and translated into Estonian or English, as required.
  • Study TRP documents: institution invitation, proof of sufficient legal income, health insurance covering medical treatment during the permit, language evidence where required, passport and state-fee payment. The law requires sufficient legal income for the relevant residence period. The Estonian Academy of Arts publishes €880/month for students in 2026; use this as current institutional guidance, not as a substitute for written PBGB confirmation for the applicant's case.
  • After arrival: students must register their place of residence in the Population Register within one month of arrival where the TRP route applies. For a study period exceeding one year, arrange the TRP appointment and submit the required documents to the Police and Border Guard Board (PBGB). Official guidance indicates a decision within two months after a complete application is accepted; missing documents can delay the process. Confirm the exact route and appointment before travel.
  • Work during studies: a student holding a study residence permit may work without a separate work permit and without a fixed statutory hours cap, provided the work does not interfere with studies. The student must maintain full-time study and satisfactory progress; national study guidance refers to completing around 30 ECTS per semester and finishing within the nominal study period. Do not market part-time work as guaranteed or assume a student can prioritise employment over study.
  • After graduation: national Study in Estonia guidance states that non-EU graduates may stay for an additional nine months to seek employment and apply for a work-based residence permit. Confirm the applicant's eligibility, start date and current PBGB procedure before advising; this is not a job guarantee.
  • Counsellor checklist: verify exact programme/institution eligibility, D visa versus TRP route, New Delhi appointment availability, apostille/legalisation and translation, three-month income evidence, current PBGB funds and state fees, insurance dates, and residence registration deadline. Do not tell clients to lodge an Estonian D visa at the German Embassy in Colombo.

Estonian MFA — Long-stay D visa, study funds and documents · Estonian Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lanka applicants and D-visa jurisdiction · Estonian MFA — visa representation by country · Study in Estonia — visa and residence permit guide · Study in Estonia — work during and after studies · Estonian Academy of Arts — 2026 funds guidance · Riigi Teataja — Aliens Act, student employment provisions

Source owner: Estonian Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Police and Border Guard Board (PBGB), Riigi Teataja and national Study in Estonia portal. Checked 18 November 2026. D-visa funds €880/month and fee €120 are from current MFA guidance; €880/month TRP guidance is corroborated by a university source and must be confirmed with PBGB for the case. Nine-month graduate period should be reconfirmed with PBGB.

Finland — Residence Permit for Studies

  • Funds: Migri requires at least €800/month. For studies lasting one year or longer, the applicant must show €9,600 in their own bank account for the first year at application. If tuition is unpaid, the tuition fee amount must also be available separately; living-cost funds cannot be used to pay tuition. A six-month bank statement is required.
  • Insurance/admission: attach the Finnish institution's acceptance/attendance certificate, proof of funds, private medical insurance and evidence of paid tuition or scholarship as applicable. Non-EU/EEA students generally pay tuition for English-taught higher-education programmes unless exempt/scholarship applies.
  • Work: with a study residence permit, paid work is allowed in any field for an average of 30 hours/week over the year (up to 1,560 hours/year). Some weeks may exceed 30 hours if the annual average stays within the limit. Degree-required internships/diploma work agreed as part of the study programme are exempt from the cap.
  • After graduation: eligible graduates may apply for a residence permit to look for work or start a business for up to two years, subject to funds and other Migri requirements. Part-time work is not guaranteed and should not be used as the initial funds plan.

Migri — Residence permit application for studies · Migri — Student income requirement · Migri — Permit to look for work/start a business · Study in Finland — Official FAQ

Source owner: Finnish Immigration Service (Migri). Cross-checked 16 November 2026 against the current Migri study-permit page: €800/month and €9,600 for the first year remain the published minimum; work is an average of 30 hours/week (1,560 hours/year). Funds are required at first application and cannot be replaced by expected future part-time earnings. Confirm the latest statement and tuition evidence rules in Enter Finland at filing.

Sweden — Residence Permit for Higher Education

  • 2026 maintenance funds: SEK 10,656/month for applications in 2026. The amount can be reduced if the student has documented free food and/or accommodation from the university/eligible organisation. Additional amounts apply for accompanying family.
  • Work — rule changed 11 June 2026: students with a permit granted on or after 11 June 2026 at bachelor's/master's (first/second-cycle) level may work up to 15 hours/week during semesters. Work may be unlimited in June, July and August, and certain study-related/academic work is exempt. Permits granted before 11 June 2026 are treated differently until extension.
  • Study progress: from 11 June 2026, stricter progress rules apply to relevant new/extended permits: 37.5 credits in the first year and 45 credits per year from the second year. The student must also notify the Migration Agency of their address within 30 days unless population registration removes that obligation.
  • After graduation: a graduate who completed at least two semesters may apply from inside Sweden, before the study permit expires, for a 12-month permit to seek work or explore starting a business. A job permit route has its own conditions.

Swedish Migration Agency — Apply for higher-education study permit · Swedish Migration Agency — New rules effective 11 June 2026 · Swedish Migration Agency — Post-study job search

Source owner: Swedish Migration Agency (Migrationsverket). Cross-checked 16 November 2026 against the current higher-education application page: maintenance is SEK 10,656/month for 2026 applications; Bachelor's/Master's students with permits granted on or after 11 June 2026 are limited to 15 hours/week during semesters, with stated exceptions and June–August summer work. Apply rules by permit decision date and study level, not just application date.

GREECE

Student visa and residence permit — Sri Lankan applicants, 2026 review

Scope and route: for a Sri Lankan/non-EU applicant admitted to a recognised Greek higher-education institution for full-time study exceeding 90 days. The applicant needs a Greek national long-stay Type D study visa (H.1 Studies) before travel, then a Greek study residence permit after arrival. A Schengen short-stay C visa is not a substitute for a full academic programme. A Greek national visa/residence permit is for Greece; it does not create a right to live or work in another EU country.

1. Sri Lanka application channel.

Greece's Embassy in New Delhi is the competent diplomatic authority accredited to Sri Lanka, and Greece also has an Honorary Consulate General in Colombo. The Greek MFA lists a Greece Visa Application Centre in Sri Lanka operated through GVCW (not VFS Global). The Embassy's published Schengen processing guidance includes courier time for applications from Sri Lanka to New Delhi; however, do not assume the Schengen processing-time page automatically applies to national D visas. Before booking, contact the Colombo GVCW centre and the Embassy Consular Office at grcon.del@mfa.gr to confirm that the centre accepts the H.1 national study visa, personal appearance/interview, appointment, current fee and original-document handling.

2. Verify admission and course.

Obtain the official acceptance/enrolment certificate from a recognised Greek higher-education institution for a specific full-time programme, the programme duration, language of instruction and tuition/enrolment fee receipt or confirmation of acceptance where fees are payable. The institution should confirm whether it participates in the accelerated student residence-permit procedure. Check that the student meets academic and language requirements before collecting visa documents.

3. Financial evidence — important source discrepancy.

The national Type D H.1 visa guidance published by the Greek visa service states a minimum of €400/month for study costs and subsistence. The Attica Decentralised Administration's current residence-permit checklist also lists €400/month. However, the University of Athens' non-EU student checklist currently refers to €650/month. These official/institutional checklists are not fully aligned. Do not present €400 as a universal guarantee or treat €650 as a nationwide statutory figure. For counsellor budgeting, use at least €650/month as a cautious planning figure until the competent consular post and the student's destination-region migration authority confirm the exact required amount, period and sponsor evidence in writing. Tuition, accommodation, insurance and return travel are separate budget items.

4. Type D visa documents.

Prepare the university acceptance/enrolment certificate and fee evidence where applicable; evidence of sufficient funds; valid passport; national visa application and photo; health/travel insurance meeting the current Greek national-visa requirements; and any additional documents requested by the Embassy, including criminal-record or medical documents where applicable. If the applicant is under 18, parental/legal-guardian consent is required. Sri Lankan public documents may need certification by Sri Lanka's Ministry of Foreign Affairs followed by verification/attestation through the Greece visa centre and Embassy in New Delhi, then Greek translation as instructed. Follow the Embassy's Sri Lanka-specific attestation procedure; do not assume an apostille alone is sufficient.

5. Arrival and study residence permit.

After entering Greece on the national study visa, submit the study residence-permit application electronically through the Ministry of Migration and Asylum platform and complete the required appointment/biometrics at the competent migration office. University of Athens guidance says third-country students should apply within 90 days of arrival. The Attica administration lists a €150 administrative fee for each year requested (fee code 2107), plus a €16 residence-card fee; scholarship holders may have fee exemptions under the applicable category. The EU Immigration Portal describes a normal one-year renewable permit; multi-year permits may be available where the institution certifies total programme duration. Multi-year permit holders must submit updated enrolment, examination participation and transcript evidence every two years as required. Confirm the competent region's live checklist and fee at filing.

6. Health insurance and academic progress.

Provide acceptable private or university insurance for the residence application, unless the scholarship or applicable public insurance covers the student. Maintain active enrolment and examination participation. Keep tuition receipts, attendance/progress records, updated transcripts, proof of funds and accommodation evidence ready for renewal. A multi-year permit can be withdrawn if required periodic study evidence is not submitted.

7. Work during studies.

Greek law permits eligible third-country students holding a study residence permit to undertake part-time employment under the applicable labour rules. The Greek visa service information states the hours should not be less than 10 hours per week or the equivalent; the University of Athens student guidance describes a 20-hour-per-week maximum. Treat 20 hours/week as the conservative upper planning limit, verify current labour-law conditions and the student's exact permit before work starts, and do not promise a job. Part-time wages should not be used as initial visa funds.

8. After graduation / change of status.

The European Commission's Student in Greece portal (dated 1 April 2025) says that under the general student route graduates must return to their home country and submit a fresh application from there; it does not describe an automatic post-study job-search permit. Do not promise a Greek graduate job-search extension. If a separate current route is available under newer legislation or a specific graduate category, verify it directly with the Ministry of Migration and Asylum before advising. A student should not assume they can simply change to another residence purpose while remaining in Greece.

Counsellor stop-check: obtain written confirmation of Sri Lanka's current H.1 application channel; verify the university/programme and national D visa category; resolve the €400 vs €650 monthly funds discrepancy with the competent authority; check insurance and document attestation/translation; diarise the 90-day residence application; explain 20h/week as a conservative work ceiling; and do not promise post-study stay or employment.

  • Greek Embassy New Delhi — visas and Greece Visa Application Centre links for Sri Lanka
  • GVCW — Greece visa application centre for Sri Lanka (confirm H.1 national D visa acceptance before booking)
  • Greek Embassy New Delhi — attestation procedure for Sri Lankan documents
  • European Commission — Student in Greece (1 April 2025)
  • Attica Decentralised Administration — study residence permit documents and €400/month evidence
  • University of Athens — non-EU student residence documents and €650/month checklist
  • University of Athens — work during studies guidance
  • University of Athens — online application within 90 days
  • Greek MFA — national Type D visa and residence legislation

Source owners: Hellenic Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Ministry of Migration and Asylum/competent Decentralised Administrations; supporting university guidance from National and Kapodistrian University of Athens. Checked 18 November 2026. The EU Immigration Portal entry is dated 1 April 2025; verify subsequent legal amendments and mission instructions before client advice.

MALTA

Study residence permit — licensed provider, MQF level, funds, work and graduate route

Scope: for third-country nationals, including Sri Lankan applicants, planning study in Malta. A student residence permit is not a generic route for every short course or private college. Confirm entry visa requirements and the current Identità process for the applicant's nationality and course duration.

1. Check provider and exact qualification before taking a deposit.

Identità's study-permit guidance requires a full-time course at an appropriately licensed institution. For the higher-education route, verify the provider's MFHEA licence and the exact qualification's recognition by MQRIC, normally at MQF Level 5 or above. Check the actual campus, programme, award, full-time status and at least 15 hours of organised study per week where required. A recruitment-agent partnership or college marketing page is not proof that a course qualifies for student residence or work rights.

2. Entry visa — obtain the correct D visa before travel.

Identità says non-EU students intending to study a course of 105 days or longer must obtain a study National Long-Stay Type D visa before travelling. The application is processed by Identità's Central Visa Unit through its VFS Extended Service; there is no ordinary application directly at a Maltese embassy. The current FAQ says submit at least 8 weeks before the course starts and no earlier than 6 months before commencement. Processing may take up to 8 weeks. The student visa checklist currently asks for a passport with 10 months' validity, institution enrolment letter, tuition payment proof, three months' bank statements and international bank-card evidence. VFS service charges are additional; the FAQ gives an approximate €150 Extended Service administration fee, but confirm the exact amount at booking. Do not assume a VFS centre in Sri Lanka is currently enabled: check the live Extended Service branch list and book in a country where it is available. Identità says applicants need not be legal residents of the country where they lodge.

3. After arrival — apply for the study residence permit.

For the standard higher-education route (MQF Level 5+), submit the study residence application online through the Identità Expatriates Portal and complete the required in-person steps/biometrics in Malta. Identità lists a €50 fee for study/research/training applications. The travel document must have at least 6 months' validity at residence-permit submission. The residence permit is a separate step from the D visa; do not describe the D visa itself as the residence card. Follow the current Form N.01/portal checklist and instructions from the Expatriates Unit.

4. Financial means — distinguish the D-visa and residence-permit tests.

Do not merge two different official checks. The current Identità student D-visa checklist asks for funds of at least 75% of the national minimum wage for every month of study. Malta's official 2026 weekly minimum-wage reference is €229.44; using 52 weeks/12 months, 75% is approximately €745.68 per month (illustrative calculation; confirm the current checklist's calculation method and any applicable sector/category before quoting). Separately, Identità's study-residence FAQ describes the residence-permit test as 60% of the annual national minimum wage or the poverty line, whichever is higher, pro-rated to study duration and household size. For the D-visa checklist, prepare the last 3 months' bank statements, a recent bank certificate and proof the student can access overseas funds in Malta. If sponsored, provide the sponsor's last 3 months' statements, signed declaration and ID/passport copy. Tuition and actual living expenses remain separate budget items; do not use future part-time earnings as the initial proof of funds.

5. Insurance, health screening and tuition evidence.

Prepare proof of tuition payment/enrolment, accommodation and private health insurance unless the institution/category is specifically exempt. Identità's guidance describes health screening requirements for certain stays over three months; follow the current instructions issued by the Maltese health authorities and institution. Confirm whether the institution is one of the stated insurance-exempt institutions rather than assuming every public or private college is exempt.

6. Work during study — limited hours and a separate licence.

Identità says full-time third-country students in a course leading to an MQRIC-recognised higher-education qualification may work up to 20 hours per week from the first year, but only after the student residence permit is issued and the employer obtains the required Jobsplus employment licence. Other students on courses exceeding 90 days may only access work after their first 3 months, subject to Jobsplus conditions and possible labour-market tests. The student cannot start work merely because a D visa was issued. Course level, permit wording and the actual licence matter. Do not promise employment or count expected wages as initial funds.

7. Post-study stay — up to nine months for eligible graduates.

Identità's study FAQ describes a post-study residence route of up to nine months for eligible non-EU graduates who successfully complete an MQF Level 5 or higher course and apply while lawfully in Malta, before their student permission expires. This is a job-search/business-preparation stay, not automatic permission to work full-time. A separate employment authorisation or single permit is needed before taking up employment. Confirm the current statutory basis, application timing and financial evidence directly with Identità before promising this route.

Counsellor stop-check: verify MFHEA provider licence and MQRIC award, MQF level and full-time study, whether course length triggers a D visa (105 days+), VFS Extended Service branch/appointment, 8-week filing lead time and passport validity, D-visa funds (75% minimum wage/month) separately from residence-permit funds (60% annual minimum wage or higher poverty line), tuition and insurance/health screening, €50 residence fee, Jobsplus licence and 20-hour cap, and eligibility/deadline for the 9-month graduate route. Do not market Malta as “study plus guaranteed part-time job”.

Official references

  • Identità — study residence permit eligibility and application
  • Identità — study FAQ, financial means and post-study stay
  • Identità — student visa employment conditions
  • Identità — study D-visa, VFS Extended Service, timing and fees FAQ
  • Identità — Student National D-visa document checklist
  • DIER — 2026 national minimum wage
  • Identità — VFS Extended Service branch list
  • Malta Further and Higher Education Authority — provider licensing and qualifications

Source owner: Identità (Expatriates Unit and Central Visa Unit), with MFHEA/MQRIC for provider and qualification recognition. Checked 18 November 2026. The D-visa checklist and residence-permit funds tests are different; the €745.68/month figure is an indicative conversion of the published 2026 weekly minimum wage, not a universal fixed visa balance. Confirm the live checklist, VFS branch and category-specific conditions before filing.

CYPRUS

Student entry permit and residence — full-time study, funds, work and post-study route

Scope: for third-country nationals, including Sri Lankan applicants, planning full-time study in the Republic of Cyprus. Cyprus is not yet part of the full Schengen area; a Cyprus visa or residence permit does not grant unrestricted entry, residence or work rights in Schengen countries.

1. Confirm the institution and full-time programme.

The EU Immigration Portal states that student permission is for full-time students admitted to a recognised higher-education institution or eligible public/private institution for a diploma or higher qualification. Verify the institution's recognition, programme, full-time status, tuition, attendance and the institution's role in submitting the entry-permit application. Do not assume a short course or language-only programme qualifies under the same route.

2. Obtain the correct entry authorisation before travel.

A third-country student must obtain a Cyprus visa or an entry permit from the Civil Registry and Migration Department before arrival. For higher-education students, the institution generally coordinates the entry-permit application. The Migration Department says an application cannot be made from inside Cyprus if the person is there only as a tourist/visitor. Do not advise a client to enter as a visitor and convert to student status in-country.

3. Documents, funds and arrival deadline.

Prepare the admission/acceptance evidence, valid passport, proof of sufficient living funds, tuition arrangements, health insurance, criminal-record certificate and any institution/Migration Department forms, medical certificates and certified translations. After arrival, register with the District Aliens and Immigration Unit, complete the required medical checks and apply for the temporary residence permit within 10 days. The EU Immigration Portal says student permits are generally issued for one year and are renewable; submit renewal about one month before expiry. No single universal euro bank-balance figure is published in the cited general student guidance. Some university checklists give institution-specific amounts (for example €7,000); never present that as a national threshold. Confirm the current amount, sponsor rules and required bank-statement period directly with the chosen institution and Migration Department.

4. Fees and processing — verify the student's exact route.

Government fee schedules and university instructions may distinguish entry authorisation, first registration/ARC, residence-card issuance and renewal. University of Cyprus guidance, for example, quotes a combined €140 payment in its own process, while other published government fee schedules cover different permit categories. These are not interchangeable national student-visa prices. Obtain the current written fee breakdown and processing estimate from the institution and the competent authority before quoting a client. Complete applications may still take longer if documents or checks are outstanding.

5. Work during study — restricted occupations and prior approval.

Students may work outside study hours only in the occupations/sectors allowed by the current Ministerial Order and subject to the applicable conditions. The Migration Department's published guidance sets a maximum of 20 hours per week during classes and up to 38 hours per week during class interruptions. The student must first sign an employment contract and submit it to the District Labour Office for approval, with the study timetable. This is not unrestricted permission to take any job; check the current permitted-sector list and approved contract before work starts.

6. Post-study stay — limited to eligible higher qualifications.

After studies, a graduate may apply for a residence permit for up to 12 months to seek employment or establish a business, if legal conditions are met. The intended job/business must correspond to the completed qualification and the published minimum study level is European Qualifications Framework level 7 (master's) or higher. This is an application-based route, not an automatic extension and not a general promise for bachelor's or diploma graduates.

7. Sri Lankan application channel.

Cyprus has no resident diplomatic mission in Sri Lanka; Sri Lanka is covered by the High Commission of Cyprus in India, and an Honorary Consulate is listed in Colombo. The High Commission's published notice says its VFS arrangement effective 21 July 2026 also serves applicants from parallel-accredited countries including Sri Lanka. Confirm with the High Commission/VFS whether the specific national student visa or entry-permit case can be lodged from Sri Lanka, the correct centre, appointment route and original-document requirements. Do not assume the Colombo Honorary Consulate itself issues visas.

Counsellor stop-check: obtain written confirmation of institution/programme eligibility, the institution-led entry process, current funds and sponsor evidence, insurance/medical/criminal-record requirements, 10-day arrival registration deadline, exact fees, permitted work sector and approved contract. Do not promise part-time work, a fixed bank balance or post-study eligibility without checking the student's exact course and current rules.

  • Cyprus Migration Department — Students: entry and re-entry
  • Cyprus Migration Department — Economic activities by students (20/38-hour limits and contract approval)
  • Cyprus Migration Department — Post-study job-search/entrepreneurship permit
  • Cyprus Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Sri Lanka consular coverage
  • Cyprus High Commission, New Delhi — visa information and VFS arrangement

The national financial threshold and exact student fee breakdown are not stated as one universal amount in the cited general sources; confirm with the institution/authority for each case. Work rights depend on the current permitted-sector order and prior contract approval.

CZECHIA

Czech Republic — long-term student visa / study residence permit

Scope: third-country nationals, including Sri Lankan applicants, intending to study in Czechia for more than 90 days. The correct route depends on the legal definition and type of study; accredited university study commonly qualifies for a long-term residence permit for study, while certain secondary-school, non-exchange or language courses may require a long-term study visa instead.

1. Confirm programme eligibility and choose the correct route.

For a long-term residence permit for study, the programme must fall within Section 64 of the Foreigners Act; common cases include an accredited university study programme. A permit is normally valid for up to one year, and in cases specified by law up to two years; it can be renewed if study continues and conditions are met. Do not assume every private college, short course or language course qualifies for the same permit. Ask the institution to confirm the legal study category in writing.

2. Apply from Sri Lanka through the Czech Embassy in New Delhi.

The Embassy of the Czech Republic in New Delhi has territorial competence for Sri Lankan citizens and accepts long-term visa and long-term residence applications in person. The Embassy's study-appointment notice updated 23 May 2026 instructs Sri Lankan applicants to use the dedicated booking email newdelhi.visa@mzv.gov.cz during the registration window stated on that page. The email subject must contain only the applicant's passport number; the body must give the applicant's full name, visa type and purpose; attach only PDF scans of the passport biodata page and admission/purpose document. One applicant may send only one email. The Embassy states that 360 applications for these study/educational categories are accepted in 2026 and appointment allocation is limited; the separate booking guidance says allocation includes a random element and no agent can guarantee or prioritise an appointment. Always recheck the live registration dates and instructions before sending. Applications are lodged personally; interview/fingerprints may be required. Do not confuse this long-stay route with short-stay Schengen C-visa representation arrangements.

3. Prepare documents and translations.

Typical documents include the correct application form, passport, photographs, original university acceptance/enrolment confirmation, accommodation evidence, proof of funds or scholarship/sponsor coverage, and criminal-record extract. Additional medical report or other police certificates may be requested. Documents generally must be no older than 180 days (except passport/photo), translated officially into Czech and foreign public documents must be apostilled or superlegalised as applicable. New Delhi's published 2026 guidance specifically notes superlegalisation for Sri Lankan public documents; confirm the exact chain for each document before submission. Insurance evidence is required before visa issuance after approval, subject to the applicable insurance rules.

4. Funds — use the official formula and check the current statutory base.

The Ministry of the Interior's published student-funds guidance gives a long-term visa/long-term residence calculation of 15 × the existential minimum, plus 2 × that amount for every full month after the first. The existential-minimum base remains CZK 3,130; the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs page updated 1 October 2026 confirms that figure (the separate living minimum for a single adult is CZK 5,500, so do not confuse the two). Using the existential-minimum base, a six-month example is CZK 78,250 and a 12-month example is CZK 115,810. This is a statutory evidence calculation, not a realistic living-cost budget; the European Education Area suggests budgeting roughly €500–750/month depending on location and lifestyle. The school or a legal entity may cover some/all costs, and a grant or scholarship can be accepted under the rules. Recheck the formula and required period with the embassy/IPC for the student's exact route before quoting.

5. Fees, processing and arrival.

The published consular fee for a long-term study residence-permit application is CZK 2,500; the long-term study visa route also lists CZK 2,500. The legal processing period for a study long-term visa or study long-term residence permit is generally 60 days, but missing documents or suspended proceedings can extend the real timeline. If the residence-permit application is approved abroad, the embassy issues a single-entry visa (typically 60 days) to collect the residence card in Czechia; follow the embassy/MOI instructions. A long-term visa holder must register with the Foreign Police within 3 business days of arrival, unless the accommodation provider fulfils the reporting duty.

6. Work during study — no fixed 20-hour cap for eligible full-time university students.

A third-country national holding a valid study long-term visa or study residence permit for a full-time accredited university programme has free access to the Czech labour market and does not need a separate work permit/Employee Card for employment during the validity of that study status. This is not a blanket rule for every course or every residence category. Confirm that the student's programme and permit qualify, and ensure the student continues to meet study and residence obligations. Do not advertise a guaranteed job or guaranteed earnings.

7. After graduation — up to 9 months for eligible graduates.

A graduate who successfully completes a university-accredited study programme in Czechia may apply before the current residence status expires for a long-term residence permit to seek employment or start a business. It is valid for up to 9 months and cannot be extended. Eligible graduates have free access to the labour market under the applicable rules. This is an application-based route, not an automatic extension; non-university or non-accredited programme graduates may not qualify.

Counsellor stop-check: verify the institution's accreditation and Section 64 study classification, exact route (long-term visa vs study residence permit), New Delhi appointment using the current 2026 registration email/window (newdelhi.visa@mzv.gov.cz; one email and PDF-only attachments), Czech translations and Sri Lankan document superlegalisation, current existential-minimum funds formula (CZK 3,130, not the separate CZK 5,500 living minimum), insurance, processing time and eligibility for free labour-market access. Do not promise a 20-hour limit, job placement or post-study permit to every course applicant.

  • Czech Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Study long-term visa/residence application procedure
  • Czech Ministry of the Interior — Proof of funds and student calculation

Source owner: Czech Ministry of the Interior, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs, Embassy of the Czech Republic in New Delhi and European Education Area. Checked 18 November 2026. The 2026 New Delhi study appointment notice uses newdelhi.visa@mzv.gov.cz (not the older visa_newdelhi@mzv.cz address) and specifies one email per applicant, PDF-only attachments and limited annual capacity. Funds use the CZK 3,130 existential-minimum base confirmed by MPSV on 1 October 2026; do not confuse it with the CZK 5,500 living minimum for a single adult. Confirm live registration dates and exact amount for the application route at filing. Work access depends on qualifying full-time study under the law.

Luxembourg — Student residence (third-country nationals)

  • Eligibility: third-country applicant must be admitted to a recognised Luxembourg higher-education establishment for full-time studies leading to a higher-education degree/diploma. Doctoral students are included. Check the exact institution and programme recognition before taking a deposit or promising visa eligibility.
  • Important procedure change from 1 October 2026: new applicants use the long-stay Visa D route directly through the competent Luxembourg/Belgian diplomatic or consular post. The old separate temporary authorisation-to-stay process applies only to applications lodged on or before 30 September 2026 under transitional rules. Do not use the old process for a new case.
  • Sri Lanka — where to lodge: long-stay D visa applications for Sri Lankan residents are processed by the Luxembourg Embassy in New Delhi. The Embassy's 6 October 2026 instructions say the application must be couriered through the Luxembourg Honorary Consulate in Sri Lanka at 103//14 Dharmapala Mawatha, Colombo 7 (email: colombo@consul-hon.lu; phone: +94 11 243 6271). This is the Embassy's specified courier address for visa files; do not confuse it with the Honorary Consul's separately listed general office address. Do not assume the Swiss Embassy's short-stay representation or the India VFS centre handles a Sri Lankan long-stay student visa. Confirm courier acceptance, payment method and any current checklist directly before dispatch.
  • Documents: full copy of valid passport; criminal-record extract from current country of residence dated within the last 3 months (or justified affidavit); official admission; proof of sufficient resources for living costs and return travel; and travel health insurance valid across Schengen for medical treatment/hospitalisation, medical repatriation and funeral costs for the visa period. Parent-funded applicants need bank evidence showing parents can cover living, healthcare and education costs for at least 12 months plus return travel. A Luxembourg-resident/Luxembourg-national guarantor may use the official financial-support undertaking. Documents not in English, French or German need certified translation. Under-18 applicants need parental authorisation.
  • Financial proof: monthly resources must be at least 80% of the social inclusion income (REVIS) currently in force. The benchmark is dynamic; do not quote an old annual euro figure as a fixed 2026/27 rule. Accepted evidence includes grant/loan, student's bank certificate, qualifying parent support, formal Luxembourg guarantor undertaking, material benefits such as free accommodation, or a qualifying work contract up to 15 hours/week. Tuition and return travel should be budgeted separately where relevant.
  • Processing / Visa D: the official guide says the decision is in principle within 60 days for a complete application; missing documents suspend the period. If approved, the D visa is issued for 6 months, during which the applicant must enter Luxembourg and complete the post-arrival formalities before it expires. An application receipt or appointment is not a visa approval.
  • After arrival: declare arrival with the commune of residence, undergo the required medical check, and apply for the student residence permit before the D visa expires. The older EU Immigration Portal gives a 3-day arrival declaration reference, but because the national procedure changed on 1 October 2026, verify the exact local deadline and current steps with the commune/Guichet before advising. Student permits are renewable; submit renewal at least 2 months before expiry and provide continued enrolment, funds and other required evidence.
  • Work during studies: student residence-permit holders may work up to an average of 15 hours per week over a one-month period outside study time. This cap does not apply to paid work during school holidays, specified doctoral research, or University of Luxembourg assistant contracts. The employer must make the required written declaration to Immigration and notify the start of employment within 3 working days. Do not describe this as unrestricted work or guaranteed employment.
  • After graduation: eligible students who completed their final year in Luxembourg and obtained a Master's degree, and qualifying doctoral graduates, may apply for a non-renewable 12-month job-search/business-creation residence permit. Apply no later than 30 days before the current student permit expires; prove comprehensive health insurance and resources of at least 80% of the current REVIS. Bachelor-only graduates are not covered by this specific route unless another legal pathway applies.
  • Fees (checked 18 November 2026): the Luxembourg Embassy New Delhi lists the long-stay Visa D fee as €50 (its displayed INR equivalent can change with exchange rates). After the first residence-permit application is accepted, the official Guichet procedure requires an €80 residence-card issuance fee and a biometric appointment. Budget medical examination/TB screening, courier, translation and document costs separately. Confirm payment instructions with the Embassy before sending funds.
  • Post-arrival deadlines and card issue: within 3 days of arrival, declare arrival at the commune using the valid D visa and accommodation evidence. Complete the Luxembourg medical examination and TB screening as soon as possible. Apply for the first student residence card through MyGuichet.lu or by post before the D visa expires; provide the full passport copy, arrival declaration and suitable accommodation proof. Once approved, pay €80, attend biometrics, then collect the card (the official page says it is normally available one week after biometrics). The card is not issued automatically just because the D visa was granted.
  • Fees / counsellor check: confirm institution recognition, the current REVIS-linked amount, Sri Lankan courier procedure, translation/legalisation, the 3-day commune declaration, medical/TB steps, the first-card application deadline and the €80 card fee before client advice.
