Checked 18 November 2026. New direct Visa D procedure applies from 1 October 2026. The student financial threshold remains 80% of the current REVIS and is dynamic; the FNS table depends on household composition, so this page deliberately does not convert it into a single fixed student balance. The Embassy lists €50 for Visa D; Guichet lists €80 for the residence card. Reconfirm both fees, current REVIS interpretation and the Sri Lankan courier process at filing.

SLOVENIA

Slovenia — study temporary residence, D visa, Sri Lankan route, funds and work (2026/27)

Scope: non-EU/EEA/Swiss nationals, including Sri Lankan students, enrolling in publicly recognised education or study programmes in Slovenia. For stays over 90 days, the usual route is a temporary residence permit for study. A national D visa is a separate entry document and is not automatically required in every filing route.

1. Eligible programme and permit duration.

The study residence permit applies to third-country nationals enrolled in publicly recognised education or study programmes, including accredited higher-education institutions and specified exchange/training programmes. It is generally issued for the programme duration, but for no longer than one year at a time; it may be extended while the student continues to meet the conditions. The permit is linked to enrolment/admission. Notify the Administrative Unit if studies are interrupted, completed or withdrawn.

2. Sri Lankan jurisdiction — distinguish C visa from study residence/D visa.

The Slovenian Embassy in New Delhi lists Sri Lankan citizens among nationals requiring a visa. Its country information says Sri Lankan applicants for short-stay C visas are represented by the Embassy of Switzerland in Colombo. This does not confirm that Switzerland accepts Slovenian national D visas or study-residence applications. The first Slovenian temporary residence permit is generally obtained before entering Slovenia; official Study in Slovenia guidance says students who need a visa should request the study residence permit through the competent Slovenian diplomatic mission. Before accepting a case, email the Embassy in New Delhi and the receiving university to confirm the competent mission, whether the student must lodge the residence application abroad, and whether a D visa is needed after approval. Do not book a Switzerland Colombo appointment for a national study permit without written confirmation.

3. Core documents.

Prepare the prescribed application form, valid passport, biometric photograph, proof of enrolment/admission, proof of valid health insurance in Slovenia, evidence of sufficient means of subsistence and a criminal-record certificate from the home country where required. The University of Ljubljana's current 2026/27 guidance says the home-country police certificate should be no older than three months, officially translated into Slovene and apostilled/certified where applicable. Additional documents may be requested by the competent Administrative Unit or diplomatic mission.

4. Financial evidence — 2026/27.

The University of Ljubljana's international-student guidance currently states sufficient means of €507.43 per month for the 2026/27 example. Evidence may include a legally obligated parent's/legal guardian's written support declaration for the academic year, funds in a bank account in Slovenia or abroad, scholarship/grant or other qualifying income. Another Study in Slovenia FAQ still displays an older €494.09 figure. Because these education/government pages are not aligned, use €507.43 only as the current 2026/27 planning reference and obtain the exact amount and calculation period from the competent Administrative Unit before submission. This is a subsistence test, not a complete tuition-and-living-cost budget.

5. Application location and timing.

If the applicant is lawfully residing in Slovenia, the application may be submitted to the competent Administrative Unit before lawful stay expires. If applying from abroad, follow the competent Slovenian diplomatic mission's process; a student who requires a visa should start as soon as enrolment is confirmed. The University of Ljubljana recommends applying preferably 3–6 months before planned arrival because processing can take a few months. Do not assume that a pending in-country application permits a student to remain after the existing visa/90-day stay expires unless the competent authority confirms the legal effect.

6. Fees — filing channel matters.

The University of Ljubljana publishes Administrative Unit charges of €50 for issuing/renewing a student temporary residence permit, €4.50 application fee and €15.47 card printing. The Slovenian Embassy New Delhi fee page lists €102 for a first temporary residence permit application, €15 for the residence card, €13 for effective service of the card collected outside Europe and €77 for a D visa. These are different services/filing routes; do not add them together as one standard student fee. Confirm the exact fees payable for the student's route with the mission or Administrative Unit.

7. After arrival and address registration.

After receiving the residence permit card, the student must register their temporary residence/address. University of Ljubljana guidance says students in dormitories are generally registered by the landlord; students in private accommodation must register within 8 days of receiving the permit, using the rental contract/landlord statement or other address evidence. Follow the local Administrative Unit's instructions and report address changes.

8. Work during studies — no blanket weekly-hours promise.

A student with a valid study temporary residence permit may work under Slovenian student-work and employment law. Temporary/occasional student work is arranged through a licensed student-work intermediary and requires a valid referral form (napotnica) before work begins; eligibility depends on recognised student status and other conditions. Official guidance does not state a universal 20-hour or 40-hour weekly entitlement for all foreign students. Do not promise a job, fixed hours or guaranteed income. Employment outside the student-work system must comply with the relevant employment/work-permit rules.

9. After graduation — up to nine months to seek work.

A third-country national who successfully completed tertiary studies at a Slovenian tertiary institution within the previous two years and obtained at least a tertiary qualification may apply for a temporary residence permit to seek employment or self-employment. It may be granted for up to nine months and cannot be extended. This permit allows the graduate to seek work; it does not itself authorise employment. If a job or self-employment opportunity is secured, the graduate must obtain the appropriate new residence/work authorisation before starting.

10. Schengen travel distinction.

A valid Slovenian residence permit allows short visits to other Schengen states under the usual 90 days in any 180-day period rule, but does not grant long-term residence, study or employment rights in another Schengen country.

Counsellor stop-check.

Verify the publicly recognised programme, exact competent mission for a Sri Lankan study-residence application, whether a D visa is required, application timing, current 2026/27 financial threshold, parent-support declaration wording, police certificate age/legalisation/translation, health insurance, channel-specific fees, address-registration deadline and student-work eligibility. Never confuse Swiss representation for Slovenian short-stay C visas with acceptance of national D/study-residence cases.

  • Slovenian Embassy New Delhi — Visa information, Sri Lanka C-visa representation, D-visa and residence fees
  • Study in Slovenia — Temporary residence permit for studies and application location
  • University of Ljubljana — 2026/27 student residence checklist, €507.43 monthly funds, fees and address registration
  • European Commission — Student in Slovenia (work and change-of-status overview)

  • Government of Slovenia — Temporary and occasional student work / referral form

Re-audited 18 November 2026. Explicitly flagged: Sri Lanka's Swiss representation is for C visas; it is not evidence of D-visa or study-residence acceptance. 2026/27 funds pages show €507.43 versus older €494.09; current figure requires Administrative Unit confirmation. No universal weekly work-hours figure or automatic job-search work authorisation is asserted.

Slovakia — Temporary Residence for Study (third-country nationals)

Eligibility / route: non-EU applicants need admission or official confirmation from an eligible Slovak educational institution/programme. The study temporary-residence category under Section 24 generally covers full-time university students; secondary and other study categories have separate conditions and age limits. A study residence permit may be granted for the expected study period, up to a maximum of six years. For a stay over 90 days, the applicant must follow the national temporary-residence process; a Schengen C visa is not a substitute.

  • Sri Lankan application channel: check the Slovak Embassy in New Delhi's current consular jurisdiction and appointment instructions for residents of Sri Lanka before lodging. The Slovak Foreign Ministry's mission page lists New Delhi for visa and residence matters; do not assume an honorary consulate can accept a study residence application. In-person submission and biometrics are generally required unless an explicit legal exception applies.
  • Documents: completed application on the prescribed form; valid passport; recent colour photograph; admission/confirmation of study; criminal-record extract from the country of citizenship and any country where the applicant lived for more than 90 days in the last three consecutive years during the relevant period (normally no older than 90 days); proof of financial security; and any additional documents requested by the Foreign Police. Foreign public documents generally need apostille/superlegalisation and official Slovak translation, unless an exemption applies. Documents should normally be issued within 90 days of filing.
  • 2026 financial proof: the general benchmark is the Slovak subsistence minimum per month of residence. From 1 July 2026 the adult subsistence minimum is €295.22/month; the general residence-check benchmark for a stay exceeding one year is 12 times the monthly amount (€3,542.64) rather than multiplying by every month of a multi-year course. The student must show the required funds in an accepted form, such as a bank statement covering the prescribed period, scholarship/institution funding confirmation, or a sponsor affidavit with the sponsor's bank evidence. Confirm the applicable calculation and accepted evidence with the competent Foreign Police/mission for the exact permit category before quoting a client.
  • Accommodation: university students are generally exempt from the accommodation-proof requirement under the study category; secondary-school and other categories may need a confirmed accommodation document. Confirm the rule for the precise programme.
  • Application / decision: submit the application to the competent Slovak diplomatic mission or Foreign Police department as legally permitted. After approval, complete the required health-insurance and medical-report formalities within the prescribed deadline. A residence card is issued after biometric enrolment and the applicable conditions are met. Check current state fees and processing time on the official mission/Ministry page; do not quote historic euro fees without a fresh check.
  • Work during study: university students may work up to 80 hours/month (approximately 20 hours/week) without a separate work permit under the official guidance. High-school students generally have a lower limit of 40 hours/month. Other student categories and internships may be treated differently. Work beyond the applicable student limit requires the appropriate employment route; a student residence permit is not unrestricted work permission.
  • After study: successful university graduates may apply to renew/extend the temporary residence for up to nine months to seek employment or start a business under the applicable Slovak route. This is not automatic and does not itself grant unrestricted work rights; the graduate must obtain the required employment/business residence status before taking up work beyond the student allowance. Do not confuse the separate 30-day protective period mentioned in EU guidance with the nine-month graduate route.
  • Counsellor checks: verify that the school and programme qualify under Section 24; whether the applicant is in a university, secondary or other category; current subsistence-minimum figure; exact financial evidence period; New Delhi mission jurisdiction; apostille and Slovak translation; medical/insurance deadline; and the correct student work limit. Do not promise a job or assume part-time earnings will cover tuition.

Slovak Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs — Residence of foreign nationals · Slovak Embassy New Delhi — consular information · IOM Slovakia — 2026 legislative update and revised subsistence minimum · IOM Slovakia — Temporary residence for study · European Commission — Student in Slovakia

Source owner: Slovak Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs, Ministry of Interior and Foreign Police, and IOM Slovakia; supporting operational guidance from IOM Slovakia and European Commission. Checked 18 November 2026. €295.22 is the general adult subsistence minimum effective 1 July 2026, replacing €284.13. It is a general residence-check benchmark; the initial study-permit document checklist and any student-specific financial evidence must be confirmed separately with the mission/Foreign Police.

Romania — Student visa and residence (non-EU/EEA)

Entry and admission: Sri Lankan nationals require a long-stay study visa (D/SD) for a full-time course in Romania. The applicant needs acceptance to an accredited/state-recognised higher-education institution and a Ministry of Education acceptance letter. Romania's D/SD is a national long-stay visa, not a Schengen short-stay C visa; after arrival the student must obtain a Romanian residence permit from the General Inspectorate for Immigration (IGI). A Romanian long-stay visa does not itself authorise residence in other Schengen states.

Visa application documents: Ministry of Education acceptance letter showing full-time study; proof of payment of tuition for at least one academic year (unless exempt, e.g. eligible Romanian state scholarship holders); proof of means of support; proof of language knowledge for the programme (except the Romanian-language preparatory year); criminal-record certificate; travel medical insurance for the visa validity period with at least €30,000 cover; valid passport and completed eVisa application. Minors also need parental/guardian consent. Confirm the current original/copy, legalisation and translation requirements with the competent Romanian mission.

2026 financial proof for D/SD visa: IGI's current study guidance requires at least the national guaranteed gross minimum basic salary per month for the entire period stated in the visa. Government Decision No. 146/2026 sets this at RON 4,325/month from 1 July 2026. The indicative calculation is RON 4,325 multiplied by the number of months stated in the visa. As the D/SD long-stay visa is normally valid for 90 days, a three-month illustration is RON 12,975; calculate against the actual visa period. Do not substitute the net wage or a generic one-year total. Eligible Romanian state scholarship holders are exempt from the tuition-payment and means-of-support evidence under the stated rules.

Application channel / timing: complete the online application at the Romanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs eVisa portal, upload the supporting documents, and attend the interview in person at the competent Romanian diplomatic mission/consular post after validation and appointment. The Ministry advises lodging long-stay applications ideally at least 45 days and no more than 90 days before intended travel; this is a planning recommendation, not a guaranteed decision time. IGI's favourable opinion is normally issued within 30 days, with a possible 15-day extension. The D/SD visa is normally issued for 90 days. Sri Lankan applicants must use the Romanian mission competent for their legal residence; do not assume the French Embassy/Schengen representation in Colombo handles Romanian national D/SD visas. Verify the competent mission and appointment route with Romania's MFA before booking.

After arrival — residence permit: apply personally or through the IGI online portal to the territorial IGI office for the county of residence, at least 30 days before the visa/right of stay expires. Provide passport, first-time acceptance letter if required, current enrolment certificate for full-time study, proof of means at least equal to the national guaranteed minimum gross salary for at least six months (2026 illustration: RON 25,950), legal accommodation evidence, medical certificate, valid Romanian-territory health insurance and fees. The permit is generally extended annually while the student remains eligible. Romanian state scholarship beneficiaries may be exempt from permit fees.

Work during studies: IGI's current “Studies” guidance and the amended legal framework allow a student holding a study residence right to work without a separate work authorisation under an individual part-time employment contract of no more than six hours per day. Older IGI/EU pages still show four hours/day; the newer current IGI page is the source to follow, but the counsellor should verify the exact legal text and permit category before advising. This is not a job or income guarantee.

After graduation: eligible graduates may request an extension of temporary stay for up to nine months to seek employment, complete employment formalities or open a business, subject to IGI conditions and timely application. This is not automatic and does not by itself grant unrestricted work rights.

Counsellor checks: verify institution accreditation and full-time course; acceptance letter; one-year tuition payment or scholarship exemption; gross minimum wage calculation (RON 4,325/month from 1 July 2026); language evidence; criminal record; €30,000 travel medical cover; correct Romanian mission for a Sri Lankan resident; residence-permit six-month funds, accommodation and insurance; six-hour daily part-time work limit; and graduate-extension eligibility. Never promise part-time employment or that wages will cover tuition/living costs.

Primary sources: Romanian IGI — Studies (current D/SD, residence and work rules); Romanian MFA — eVisa supporting documents; Romanian MFA — eVisa FAQ and application process; Romanian Government Decision No. 146/2026 — gross minimum wage from 1 July 2026; Study in Romania — visa FAQ and Schengen distinction; Study in Romania — residence permit procedure. Checked 18 November 2026. Financial examples use the RON 4,325 gross monthly minimum effective 1 July 2026; exchange-rate conversion deliberately omitted. Mission jurisdiction and individual requirements must be confirmed with Romania's MFA/IGI before client advice.

Bulgaria — Student visa and residence (non-EU)

Eligible study and route: the ordinary student route covers non-EU nationals admitted as full-time students at a Bulgarian higher-education institution. Specified upper-secondary exchange pupils have a separate route. Sri Lankan nationals require a national long-stay Type D visa before travel and then a prolonged-residence permit from the Ministry of Interior Migration Directorate. The standard student residence permit is up to one year and renewable; under the amended Foreigners Act, a permit for the final year of study may be issued for up to 18 months. A Bulgarian Type D visa/residence permit is not a work visa for unrestricted employment and does not authorise settlement in another EU state.

Sri Lankan application channel: Bulgaria has no functioning resident Embassy in Sri Lanka. The Bulgarian Embassy in New Delhi is accredited to Sri Lanka; the MFA also lists an Honorary Consulate in Colombo. The New Delhi Embassy's consular section accepts visa applications by appointment, and since 1 November 2025 Bulgarian visa applications in India can also be lodged through VFS Global centres in India. The public MFA information does not clearly confirm that a Sri Lankan resident may lodge at a Colombo honorary consulate or VFS Colombo; do not promise either route. Obtain written confirmation from consular.delhi@mfa.bg about the correct submission location, personal appearance, appointment and whether the applicant must travel to India.

2026 financial evidence: the amended Foreigners Act requires stable, regular, predictable and sufficient means at least equal to the national minimum monthly wage, minimum scholarship or minimum pension for the residence period, subject to statutory exceptions. The 2026 national minimum monthly wage is €620.20 from 1 January 2026 (Decree No. 243/13 November 2025; originally stated as BGN 1,213). For planning, a 12-month calculation at the wage benchmark is €7,442.40. This is a legal minimum benchmark, not a complete living-cost estimate; confirm whether scholarship or other statutory alternative applies and the exact evidence accepted by the Embassy/Migration Directorate.

Type D visa fee and evidence: the MFA's published Type D fee is generally €100, unless a statutory/treaty exemption applies (the MFA lists an exemption for certain students admitted under Council of Ministers Decree 228). The visa checklist includes passport, application/photo, travel medical insurance covering the planned stay with at least €30,000 cover, secured accommodation, sufficient means or scholarship evidence, and a criminal-record certificate for first application. Student-specific documents include the Ministry of Education and Science admission certificate and university confirmation of full-time study. Tuition/enrolment payment evidence may be required after entry as specified by the Act. Documents from Sri Lanka must be legalised/apostilled as applicable and translated into Bulgarian by an accepted translator. Confirm the exact checklist with the competent mission; the general Type D list and university-residence list are not identical.

Application and arrival: submit the Type D application in person at the competent Bulgarian diplomatic/consular mission; appointments are required at New Delhi. The standard Type D visa under Article 15(1) is generally for up to six months and permits up to 180 days; visa category and actual validity are set by the consular decision. After arrival, apply in person to the Migration Directorate or regional Migration Department for the student residence permit with passport/visa/entry stamp, accommodation proof, Bulgarian-territory health insurance (unless covered through the university/national system), financial evidence, initial criminal-record certificate, Ministry admission certificate and current-year university enrolment confirmation. The Act provides that the tuition/enrolment fee evidence is presented after entry. Register the residential address with the competent authorities within five days of arrival, as stated in the European Commission student guidance; complete the Migration Directorate/card process promptly and keep proof of filing.

Work during studies: full-time higher-education students may work up to 20 hours per week during the academic year and during official university holidays without a separate work permit. The employer must register the employment with the Employment Agency within seven days after the work starts. This exemption is not a promise of a job, and earnings should not be used as proof of funds before employment exists.

After graduation: eligible graduates may, within nine months, seek and start work and use employment brokerage services, provided they apply for registration with the Employment Agency within seven business days under the applicable route. This is a conditional post-study route, not an automatic unrestricted work permit; the graduate and employer must complete the required registration/status steps. Confirm current Migration Directorate practice before advising.

Changing course / renewal: the EU Immigration Portal states that a student is not permitted simply to change studies under the existing student authorisation and may need a new application. Apply for renewal before expiry and maintain full-time enrolment, funds, accommodation and insurance. The residence card's exact validity and final-year 18-month provision should be checked against the student's study calendar and current legal text.

Counsellor checklist: verify recognised university and full-time admission; Ministry acceptance certificate; Sri Lanka's New Delhi consular jurisdiction and actual visa submission location; €620.20/month benchmark and scholarship exceptions; €30,000 insurance; accommodation; police clearance and Bulgarian translation/legalisation; Type D category and €100 fee/exemption; five-day address notification; student work 20h/week plus employer 7-day registration; and nine-month graduate route. Never guarantee employment or that part-time wages will cover tuition/living costs.

Primary sources: Bulgarian MFA — Sri Lanka: New Delhi Embassy jurisdiction and Colombo Honorary Consulate; Bulgarian Embassy New Delhi — consular contacts and appointments; Bulgarian MFA — Type D visa and VFS locations; Bulgarian MFA — Type D supporting documents; Bulgarian MFA — visa fees (€100 Type D); State Gazette — Decree No. 243/2025, 2026 minimum wage; Foreigners in the Republic of Bulgaria Act — Article 24c; Labour Migration and Labour Mobility Act — Article 38; European Commission — Student in Bulgaria. Checked 18 November 2026. Confirm mission submission route and individual case requirements before client advice.

Croatia — Student temporary stay (third-country nationals)

Eligible study and residence: third-country nationals enrolled at a Croatian higher-education institution must regulate temporary stay for university studies. The normal permit is valid for up to one year (or the shorter study period). Students covered by eligible EU/multilateral mobility programmes or inter-university agreements may receive up to two years. The residence card is biometric. Do not confuse the temporary-stay approval with the separate D visa used for entry by visa-required nationals.

Sri Lankan application channel: the Croatian Embassy in New Delhi covers Sri Lanka. The Embassy's published visa instructions state that Sri Lankan applicants must appear personally at the Embassy in New Delhi; as an exception, they may first attend the Croatian Honorary Consulate in Colombo for personal verification and then submit through a VFS centre in India via a representative/travel agent. The application is processed only by the Embassy. Confirm the current process and appointment instructions with the Consular Section before booking travel.

Financial evidence — 2026 benchmark: MUP's current calculation page uses the official previous-year average monthly net salary of €1,449 (2025 figure, published 4 March 2026). A university student may show a scholarship or monthly maintenance funds of at least 25% = €362.25/month. The student must also prove return-travel funds of at least 60% = €869.40. These are statutory maintenance/return benchmarks, not tuition or accommodation budgets. Re-check the official MUP figure when applying in a later calendar year.

Documents: valid passport (valid at least three months beyond the intended temporary-stay validity), completed application, 30 × 35 mm colour photograph, proof of enrolment/admission at a Croatian higher-education institution, financial evidence/scholarship, health-insurance evidence and any required criminal-record certificate duly legalised/apostilled and translated. The general MUP conditions include valid travel document, purpose of stay, sufficient funds and health insurance. Confirm the exact criminal-record requirement and document-validity period with the Embassy/competent police administration; the legal exception for certain students arriving under EEA mobility should not be assumed for an ordinary Sri Lankan applicant.

Where to apply / D visa: temporary-stay applications are generally lodged in person at a Croatian diplomatic mission/consular post. MUP expressly allows visa-required third-country nationals coming for undergraduate, graduate or postgraduate university study to apply at the competent Croatian police administration/station while lawfully present in Croatia. If the applicant needs a D visa to enter after temporary-stay approval, the D visa is an entry authorisation, normally valid for up to six months with an approved stay of up to 30 days; apply in person at the competent Embassy/consulate or designated visa centre within the prescribed window. It is not the residence permit itself.

Arrival / registration: MUP's current temporary-stay page requires the temporary address to be reported to the competent police administration/station no later than three days after entry. If the application is approved, attend the competent police administration for biometric enrolment/card issuance as instructed. Students should contact the Croatian Health Insurance Fund (HZZO) and complete any applicable insurance registration promptly; confirm the exact deadline and coverage category for the individual case.

Official fees shown by MUP: temporary-stay administrative fee €46.45 (where application is filed at a police administration/station); biometric card production €31.85 standard or €59.73 accelerated, plus the stated €9.29 card issuance fee. Visa D fee is separate; verify the current consular fee and payment method with the Embassy.

Work during studies: university students with temporary stay for university studies may be employed or self-employed for up to 20 hours per week, unless the internship is an integral part of the study programme. This is a statutory ceiling, not a promise of a job or enough income to fund tuition/living costs. Work outside the student exemption requires the appropriate stay-and-work permission.

After graduation: the European Commission's Student in Croatia guidance describes a possible one-year stay to seek employment or set up a business, renewable for one further year. It is not automatic and is not unrestricted work authorisation. The graduate must apply for the correct status before the student permission expires and confirm the current MUP procedure and eligibility.

Renewal: submit the extension application to the competent police administration/station before the current permission expires; the European Commission portal states no later than 60 days before expiry. If filed before expiry, the student may remain while the decision is pending under the conditions described by the portal. Confirm the deadline with MUP/police station for the current case.

Counsellor checklist: verify the higher-education institution and full-time enrolment; calculate the 2026 student funds (€362.25/month plus €869.40 return funds) using the official annual salary figure; confirm New Delhi/Colombo verification and VFS process; check health insurance, police certificate/legalisation/translation; distinguish temporary-stay approval from D visa; diarise the three-day address registration; explain the 20-hour work limit and graduate route without job guarantees.

Primary sources: Croatian MUP — calculation and amount of maintenance funds for third-country nationals (2026 update); Croatian MUP — temporary stay and address registration; Croatian MUP — work rights for third-country nationals; Croatian Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lanka consular jurisdiction; Embassy — Sri Lankan applicant submission instructions; Croatian MFA — long-stay D visa; European Commission — Student in Croatia; Official Gazette — 2026 amendment to maintenance-funds regulation. Checked 18 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required before client advice.

SOURCE RE-CHECK · 14 NOVEMBER 2026

New Zealand and UAE — latest official-source verification note

New Zealand — student visa changes published and effective 27 October 2026.

Immigration New Zealand confirms four changes: (1) new master's-by-research student visa applications made on or after 27 October 2026 no longer receive unlimited in-study work rights; new master's students have up to 25 hours/week during study and full-time during holidays, subject to visa conditions; (2) English-language ability is expressly a key factor when assessing whether a student can meet visa conditions (the announcement does not set a new test score); (3) INZ can suspend processing in broader circumstances, including while provider non-compliance is being determined; and (4) for visas whose expiry is tied to course completion, the post-course period changes from one month to two months, while the 31 March expiry date remains unchanged.

Application-date control: the master's work-right change expressly applies to new student visa applications made on or after 27 October 2026. Do not retrospectively apply it to an older visa without checking that visa's actual conditions. Check the student's eVisa/visa letter before advising on work.

Publication date: 27 October 2026. Effective date: 27 October 2026. Source owner: Immigration New Zealand (INZ). Review date: 14 November 2026. Status: Official source checked — counsellor approval still required.

INZ — Changes to student visas (official announcement) · INZ — Working on a student visa

UAE / Dubai — GDRFA student residence service re-checked.

GDRFA Dubai's official service page states that an eligible foreign student enrolled at an accredited university, college, educational institution or research institute may receive a student residence permit, with the educational institution acting as host and guarantor. Listed requirements include a recent colour photo with white background, passport copy, approved medical examination, Emirates ID application receipt and health insurance. The institution must be licensed and have approval from the Ministry of Education or the competent emirate education authority.

The live GDRFA page displays AED 200 residence-permit fee, AED 10 knowledge fee, AED 10 innovation fee, AED 500 inside-country fee where applicable and AED 20 delivery fee; it says an extra AED 100 per year applies when residency exceeds two years. Displayed fees and applicability must be reconfirmed in the live service for the applicant's case. The page gives an expected completion time of 48 hours and a 60-day grace period after expiry or cancellation.

Important scope: the 60-day statement is the GDRFA Dubai service-page note after expiry/cancellation. Do not replace it with the separate ICP guidance that may apply to a student after completing studies; confirm the exact emirate, permit category and triggering event with the responsible authority. Student residence is not itself a work permit or a promise of part-time employment.

Publication date: not stated on the live service page. Effective date: not stated. Source owner: General Directorate of Residency and Foreigners Affairs — Dubai (GDRFA). Review date: 14 November 2026. Status: Official service page checked — fees and case-specific applicability require final human verification.

GDRFA Dubai — Issuing a residency permit to a student · Federal ICP — Issuing residency permit

It says the student visa application requires a certificate from the university/institute specifying the study duration. The portal page displays an update date of 18 February 2026. Its current summary does not state a universal bank-balance threshold. For a Dubai student case, reconcile this general federal overview with the specific GDRFA Dubai student-residence service and obtain written sponsor confirmation. The page-text alert alone does not establish a new sponsor rule or a change in eligibility.

The 2026 blocked-account amount shown is EUR 11,904. Student work is described as up to 140 full days or 280 half days per year, or up to 20 hours per week; eligible graduates may seek a job for up to 18 months after graduation. These are the current published summary points, not proof that the detected page-text difference changed the law. IRELAND · OFFICIAL RE-CHECK · 14 NOVEMBER 2026

Student permission — Stamp 2, finances, work and post-study route

Visa-required applicants: for academic courses beginning after 1 July 2023, show immediate access to at least €10,000 for one academic year, plus ready access to €10,000 for each subsequent year, in addition to course fees. For a course resulting in a stay of 8 months or less, show €833 per month or €6,665, whichever is lower. These are immigration financial-evidence thresholds, not a complete realistic living budget.

Financial evidence and sponsor review.

Provide six months of bank statements whether self-funded or sponsored. Explain large or irregular deposits. If using a sponsor, show the relationship, support amount and sponsor's ability to support themselves and their family; provide sponsor employment/business evidence and six months of bank statements. Credit cards are not accepted. An education bond of at least €10,000 is an alternative only for eligible degree-programme students under the ISD pilot; check the approved provider and current terms.

Course eligibility and Stamp 2.

Check the exact full-time programme on the official Interim List of Eligible Programmes (ILEP) or other applicable approved-list route before advising. English-language students generally need a 25-week programme, at least 15 classroom hours weekly, 85% attendance and an end-of-course exam. Stamp 2A is different: it does not allow employment. The permission actually granted on the IRP / immigration letter controls.

Work rights — only if the student has valid Stamp 2.

Up to 20 hours/week during term time. Up to 40 hours/week only during June, July, August and September, and 15 December to 15 January. A qualifying course-integral internship/placement may have a separate exception subject to programme level, credit and duration limits. Do not promise a job or include expected part-time income as proof of initial funds.

After graduation.

Eligible graduates may qualify for the Third Level Graduate Programme (Stamp 1G): generally 12 months after an eligible award, with a further 12 months possible for eligible master's graduates subject to conditions. This is not automatic for every course or student. To remain employed after Stamp 1G, the graduate generally needs an employment permit or another valid immigration permission.

Source owner: Immigration Service Delivery, Department of Justice, Home Affairs and Migration, Ireland. Publication/effective date: the current service pages do not state a fresh 2026 commencement date for these figures; the financial increase took effect 30 June 2025. Review date: 16 November 2026. Status: official guidance re-checked; human reviewer sign-off still required.

Official references: ISD — Third Level Graduate Programme / Stamp 1G · ISD — Information on Student Finances · ISD — Planning to Study / working as a student · ISD — Immigration permission stamps · ISD — Stamp 2 registration documents.

Before marking any country rule “verified”

  1. Open the primary official authority page, not only a university or agent summary.
  2. Record the source owner, exact rule/topic, jurisdiction and applicant category.
  3. Record publication date and effective date separately; if absent, write “not stated”.
  4. Record date checked, reviewer and a short evidence excerpt or saved reference.
  5. Keep status as Needs review until a qualified human reviewer confirms applicability and exceptions.

Prepared from official pages linked above. Content review date: 13 November 2026. Added Canada, Germany and New Zealand reference notes; human review remains required. Figures and procedures are subject to change; confirm directly before client use.

CANADA + AUSTRALIA · REVIEW CONTROL

Cross-check completed — 16 November 2026

Canada: the IRCC funds table states CAD 23,448 for one applicant outside Quebec applying on or after 1 September 2026, excluding tuition and transportation. Eligible students may work up to 24 hours/week off campus during regular terms and unlimited hours during scheduled breaks. Study permit eligibility, DLI status, PAL/TAL or CAQ, and PGWP eligibility must be checked for the exact institution and programme.

Australia: the current subclass 500 page states AUD 29,710 annual student living costs and 48 hours/fortnight during course sessions, with no work-hour limit for Masters by Research and doctoral students after commencement. The 2 October 2026 application-location/family changes and subclass 485 age, qualification and duration rules are now highlighted above. These checks do not constitute an individual visa assessment.

2026 COUNSELLOR COMPARISON · FINANCIAL EVIDENCE + STUDENT WORK

International student financial requirements and work rights — verified country snapshot

Use this as a screening comparison, not a promise of employment or a complete visa assessment. Figures below are official published immigration maintenance/funds benchmarks where available. Tuition, travel, insurance, dependants, exchange rates and local living costs may be additional. Always check the exact visa route, course, application date and visa conditions before advising.

United Kingdom — applications checked 14 November 2026.

Maintenance: £1,529 per month in London or £1,171 outside London, up to 9 months; tuition for one academic year is additional. Funds normally need to be held for 28 consecutive days, with the 28-day period ending within 31 days before application. Sri Lankan applicants should not assume the differential-evidence exemption applies. Work: eligible degree-level students at qualifying higher-education providers may work up to 20 hours/week in term time; below-degree students at qualifying providers may have a 10-hour limit; other cases may have no work permission. Check the CAS, provider and Appendix Student conditions. Official: UKVI — money · Student visa.

Canada — applications on or after 1 September 2026.

For one applicant outside Quebec, living expenses: CAD 23,448 for 12 months, excluding tuition and transportation; amounts rise with family size. Quebec uses its own financial rules. Applicants must show first-year funds and a credible plan for the remaining programme. Work: eligible full-time DLI students in qualifying programmes may work up to 24 hours/week off campus during regular academic sessions and unlimited hours during scheduled breaks. ESL/FSL-only and some other programmes do not qualify. Official: IRCC — proof of financial support · IRCC — work off campus.

Australia — Student visa subclass 500 (checked 16 November 2026).

Living-cost evidence: AUD 29,710 for the student for 12 months; add AUD 10,394 for an eligible accompanying partner and AUD 4,449 per eligible child, plus first 12 months of tuition, travel (Home Affairs guide: AUD 2,000 from Sri Lanka) and any school-age child costs (AUD 13,502/year). These are visa evidence minimums, not a realistic city budget. Financial evidence is required when the ImmiAccount checklist or Department requests it; students must not rely on future part-time earnings. Work: up to 48 hours per fortnight while the course is in session; unlimited during scheduled breaks; Masters by Research and doctoral students have no work-hour cap after commencement, subject to visa conditions. From 2 October 2026 most applicants must lodge offshore and most new applicants cannot include family unless an exemption applies. The base Student visa charge is now from AUD 2,500; check the live pricing estimator and any concessions. Official: Department of Home Affairs — subclass 500 · Federal Register — 2026 application-location and family changes.

Australia — post-study Temporary Graduate visa (subclass 485), not automatic.

For the Post-Higher Education Work stream, the applicant generally must be aged 35 or under (exceptions apply), apply in Australia, have held a Student visa in the last 6 months, complete an eligible recent degree from a CRICOS-registered course, and meet English, health-insurance, police-check and study requirements. Current stay periods: Bachelor degree — 2 years; Masters coursework/extended — 2 years; Masters research — 3 years; Doctoral degree — 3 years. The Post-Vocational Education Work stream is for eligible associate degree, diploma or trade graduates whose qualification is relevant to an occupation on the skilled occupation list; stay is generally up to 18 months. Employment is not arranged or guaranteed. From 13 November 2026 new family-member rules apply to subclass 485 applications; check eligibility before including dependants. Official: Department of Home Affairs — Temporary Graduate visa subclass 485 · Post-Higher Education Work stream.

New Zealand — tertiary / English-language student (reviewed 16 November 2026).

Living funds: NZD 20,000 per year, or NZD 1,667 per month for study shorter than one year; compulsory-school students have a separate NZD 17,000/year figure. Tuition and outward travel are additional; prepaid accommodation may be deducted in eligible cases. Work: eligible student visas may allow up to 25 hours/week and full-time work during specified scheduled holidays; the eVisa conditions control. Post-study: PSWV may allow up to 3 years depending on qualification and study duration, with NZD 5,000 funds and strict application deadlines. Important: INZ postponed the new Short-Term Graduate Work Visa and Level 7 Graduate Diploma PSWV expansion from 16 to 23 November 2026; these are not open on 16 November. Official: INZ — student fund requirements · INZ — working on a student visa · INZ — Post-Study Work Visa · INZ — postponement notice.

Ireland — visa-required non-EEA student applicants (reviewed 16 November 2026).

For academic courses longer than 8 months, show immediate access to at least €10,000 for one academic year and ready access to a further €10,000 for each subsequent year, in addition to tuition. For courses under 8 months, show €833 per month or €6,665, whichever is less. Provide six months of bank statements, explain large/irregular deposits and evidence the source; sponsors need their own financial and income evidence. Work: eligible Stamp 2 students may work up to 20 hours/week in term and 40 hours/week during June–September and 15 December–15 January; Stamp 2A does not allow work. Post-study: eligible NFQ Level 8 graduates may receive 12 months of Stamp 1G; eligible Level 9+ graduates may receive 12 months initially and a further 12 months subject to conditions. Stamp 1G allows full-time employment but not self-employment; it is not an employment permit or job guarantee. Official: ISD — student finances · ISD — Stamp 2 / Stamp 1G conditions · ISD — Third Level Graduate Programme.

Germany — admitted higher-education student, 2026 benchmark.

Blocked-account benchmark: €11,904/year (€992/month) for the ordinary admitted-student route; accepted alternatives and the current mission checklist must be checked. Do not confuse this with the separate study-place-seeking visa route. Work: up to 140 full days or 280 half days/year, or the applicable 20-hour weekly option under current rules. Post-study job search is a separate permit route, not automatic. Official: Federal Government — visa for studying · German Embassy Colombo.

Finland — student residence permit.

Funds: the current official study guidance and Migri income rules must be checked for the application date; the commonly published baseline is €800/month (€9,600/year), with tuition and insurance additional. Work: average 30 hours/week; degree-related practical training and thesis work may have different treatment. Do not budget around uncertain part-time employment. Official: Migri — studies · Migri — right to work.

Sweden — higher-education study residence permit, 2026.

Maintenance: SEK 10,656/month for applications in 2026; tuition is additional. Work: for bachelor's/master's students whose permit was granted on or after 11 June 2026, maximum 15 hours/week, subject to the listed exceptions (for example, work integral to education and June–August). Verify permit grant date and exceptions carefully. Official: Swedish Migration Agency — higher education.

Netherlands — university / HBO student residence permit, 2026.

Study norm: €1,130.77/month for university/HBO students in 2026, excluding tuition; the separate MBO norm is €928.58/month. Funds must cover 12 months and are checked annually. Work as an employee: up to 16 hours/week OR full-time in June, July and August; employer must obtain a TWV work permit. Self-employment has separate rules. Official: IND — required amounts · IND — student residence and work rules.

Cross-checked 16 November 2026 against the IND's current required-amounts table (2026 study norms) and its student residence guidance. The university/HBO and MBO amounts are distinct; work as an employee requires the employer's TWV. Do not treat the study norm as tuition-inclusive.

UAE / Dubai — no single nationwide student bank-balance figure identified in the published service pages reviewed.

Student residence is not an employment permit. Confirm the exact sponsor/host, emirate, institution approval, document checklist and any institution-specific financial evidence in writing. Do not invent a fixed AED maintenance minimum or promise part-time work. Official: GDRFA Dubai · UAE ICP.

France — long-stay student visa (2026 update).

Financial evidence: from 1 August 2026, Article R.422-2 sets the minimum at 47% of the gross monthly SMIC in force on the application filing date. The official French government service currently displays €877.50/month, based on the 1 June 2026 SMIC increase. The amount is indexed and may change; recalculate using the SMIC effective on the actual filing date rather than hard-coding €877.50 indefinitely. The threshold applies to applications filed from 1 August 2026 under the decree. Work: a qualifying long-stay student visa allows employee work up to 964 hours/year (60% of the legal annual working time); check the visa/residence conditions and employer notification requirements. Do not convert this annual cap into a guaranteed weekly schedule. Official references: Légifrance — Article R.422-2 (47% of gross SMIC; effective 1 August 2026) · French Government — Student residence and current €877.50 amount · France-Visas — student work up to 964 hours/year (Algerian nationals: separate limit).

Italy — university enrolment visa, academic years 2026–27 and 2027–28.

Financial evidence: €10,179.85/year for a university enrolment visa; for multi-year studies, the official FAQ says proof is needed for the first year. Other study-visa types use a different calculation. The Italian Ministry says a bank guarantee or surety insurance is not an accepted substitute for suitable financial resources; personal/family or accepted institutional guarantees may be assessed. Work: up to 20 hours/week and 1,040 hours/year. Official: Italian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Study in Italy FAQ.

Romania — long-stay study visa.

Financial evidence: the European Commission's Romania student guidance requires means of support at least equal to the minimum monthly wage for the entire visa period, except for students/pupils awarded a Romanian State scholarship. Tuition payment for at least one year, the Ministry of Education acceptance letter, criminal-record evidence and medical insurance are also listed. For a stay over 90 days, a residence permit is required after arrival. Work: part-time, without a separate work authorisation, up to 4 hours/day under the cited student route. Recheck the applicable minimum-wage amount on the actual filing date; do not use a stale annual figure. Official: European Commission — Student in Romania · Study in Romania — admission procedures.

Bulgaria — full-time higher-education student.

Financial evidence: show sufficient funds for subsistence and return travel, alongside admission, accommodation, health insurance and medical evidence. The official EU Immigration Portal page reviewed does not publish a single fixed 2026 student amount; obtain the current amount and acceptable evidence from the Bulgarian diplomatic mission before quoting a figure. Route: visa type D followed by a residence permit after arrival. Work: up to 20 hours/week during the academic year and official holidays without a separate work permit under the published student guidance. Official: European Commission — Student in Bulgaria.

Croatia — temporary stay for university studies.

Financial evidence: official Croatian guidance requires sufficient resources for subsistence and return travel. EURAXESS Croatia describes the study-route benchmark as a scholarship or access to funds of at least 25% of the average monthly net paid salary in the previous year; confirm the current calculation and accepted evidence with the Croatian Ministry of the Interior (MUP) / competent mission before client use. Work: third-country students may work or be self-employed up to 20 hours/week, unless an internship is an integral part of the study programme. Official: European Commission — Student in Croatia · EURAXESS Croatia — proof of means.

Greece — Student national visa and residence permit (third-country nationals)

Eligible study and entry: A non-EU applicant must be accepted by a recognised Greek higher-education institution for a specific course, have sufficient resources for living costs and pay enrolment fees where required. Apply for the national study visa (Type D) through the Greek Embassy/Consulate responsible for the applicant's lawful residence. After arrival, apply electronically for the study residence permit; the University of Athens guidance tells incoming Type D students to apply within 90 days of arrival. Confirm the exact deadline and process with the competent Greek migration office for the applicant's case.

Documents: Prepare passport, valid Type D visa and entry evidence, university acceptance/enrolment certificate, tuition/enrolment fee receipt where applicable, evidence of sufficient funds, private health insurance (unless covered by scholarship or another accepted scheme), and any consular-specific criminal-record, medical, translation or legalisation documents. The official Greek Ministry of Migration portal lists the study residence category and supporting-document requirements; the receiving consulate may require additional visa-stage documents.

Financial evidence: The European Commission Student in Greece page requires proof of income/resources sufficient for the duration of study but does not publish one universal national euro amount. The University of Athens International Students Office currently gives €650/month as its practical minimum for its own incoming non-EU students. Treat €650 as institution-specific guidance, not a nationwide statutory threshold; obtain the chosen university's written requirement and the Greek consulate's current accepted evidence list before quoting a figure.

Residence permit duration and fee: The European Commission portal describes a one-year renewable permit and a fee of €150 per year. Greek Law 5275/2026 (Government Gazette A' 17, 6 February 2026) amended the duration framework to allow a permit aligned with the maximum duration of the study programme where the statutory conditions are met. A multi-year permit may require additional institution certification and periodic enrolment/progress evidence. Recheck current fee and eligibility with the migration portal; do not assume every student receives a full-course permit automatically.

Work during studies — 20 hours/week maximum: Article 118 of the Greek Migration Code, as amended by Law 5275/2026, permits holders of the study residence permit (H.1) to work part-time outside study hours, capped at 20 hours per week. This is a legal ceiling, not guaranteed employment or sufficient income to cover tuition and living costs.

After graduation: Law 5275/2026 introduced the H.11 residence permit for up to one year after successful completion of eligible studies/research, to seek qualifying employment or establish a business. Eligibility and application requirements depend on the previous permit and successful completion; it is not automatic work authorisation during the study permit.

References: Greek Ministry of Migration and Asylum — residence permit categories/documents; Greek Government Gazette A' 17/06-02-2026 — Law 5275/2026; European Commission — Student in Greece; University of Athens — supporting documents and institution-specific funds; University of Athens — working and studying. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Spain — Long-stay study visa and authorisation (third-country nationals)

Eligible study and application route: For study longer than 90 days, a non-EU applicant generally needs a long-stay study visa and the associated authorisation for the qualifying activity. Under Royal Decree 1155/2024, in force from 20 May 2025, eligible activities include full-time higher education at an authorised Spanish institution leading to a recognised qualification, as well as specified post-compulsory secondary studies, student mobility, volunteering and training activities. For higher-education study, the applicant must generally be at least 17. The visa application submitted at the competent Spanish consular office normally includes the application for the study-stay authorisation. A person already legally in Spain may use a limited in-country route for eligible higher studies; do not assume this option is available to an applicant applying from Sri Lanka.

Documents: Prepare official admission/enrolment evidence from the authorised institution, proof of tuition/enrolment payment where required, sufficient financial resources for stay and return, health insurance from an insurer authorised to operate in Spain covering the full stay and equivalent to Spain's public-health coverage, valid passport, and any required health certificate and criminal-record certificate for stays over 180 days. Applicants under 18 need parental/legal-representative consent and accommodation/care arrangements. Consular offices may require sworn Spanish translations, apostille/legalisation and additional local documents; follow the exact checklist of the competent mission.

2026 financial benchmark — IPREM: An official Spanish consular study-visa page publishes the 2026 benchmark as 100% of monthly IPREM = €600 per month for a student applying alone; add 75% (€450/month) for the first accompanying family member and 50% (€300/month) for each additional accompanying family member. This is a 2026 consular-published IPREM benchmark, not tuition. Evidence may be the student's own funds, eligible family/legal representative support or scholarship/financial aid. Prepaid accommodation for the full stay may be deducted where the consular rules permit. The cited consular page is for Washington, not Sri Lanka: verify the IPREM amount, sponsor evidence and accepted bank-statement period with the Spanish mission competent for the applicant's residence before quoting it as the Colombo/Sri Lanka checklist.

Work during studies: A long-stay study authorisation for higher education automatically authorises employed or self-employed work, provided it is compatible with the studies and does not exceed 30 hours per week. For other study/training categories, separate conditions or authorisation may apply. Curriculum internships that form part of the programme do not require an additional work authorisation. This is a legal limit, not guaranteed employment or a reliable way to finance tuition/living costs.

Duration and renewal: The study-stay authorisation is generally linked to the duration of the qualifying course and may be renewed if the student continues to meet the requirements, remains enrolled and makes the required academic progress. Check the exact validity dates on the resolution/visa and follow the current renewal deadline; do not assume that every course receives identical duration.

After graduation: A qualifying graduate may be able to modify from study stay to residence and work after obtaining the relevant qualification/certificate and meeting the applicable employment or self-employment requirements under Article 190 of Royal Decree 1155/2024. This is not an automatic open work permit or a guaranteed post-study job-search period. Check the exact qualification, previous permit category, employer/business conditions and any scholarship/cooperation-programme restriction.

References: Spanish Ministry of Inclusion — official student visa and long-stay authorisation guide (RD 1155/2024); Spanish Ministry of Foreign Affairs — 2026 IPREM and student visa evidence (consular page; not Sri Lanka-specific); Spanish Ministry of Inclusion — work access and 30-hour limit; BOE — Royal Decree 1155/2024, including Article 190; European Commission — Student in Spain. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required for each applicant.

Portugal — Higher-education student residence visa and permit

Eligibility and route: A non-EU applicant normally applies for a national residence visa for study through the competent Portuguese consular mission, then enters Portugal and applies to AIMA for the student residence permit. Admission/acceptance by a recognised higher-education institution is required. The residence visa is an entry route, not itself the final residence permit. AIMA identifies this route under Articles 62 and 91 of Law 23/2007. Certain eligible students who have entered Portugal legally may have an in-country application route under Article 91(4); do not assume it applies to an applicant applying from Sri Lanka.

Documents: Valid passport and residence visa; official admission/enrolment confirmation; tuition payment evidence where applicable; proof of sufficient means of subsistence; accommodation/address declaration; criminal-record certificate from nationality country or country of residence for more than one year (as applicable); and health insurance or evidence of National Health Service coverage. The consular visa stage may also require application form, photographs, return-travel evidence, travel insurance, accommodation, police clearance and Portuguese criminal-record consultation. Documents may need legalisation/apostille and Portuguese translation. Use the current checklist of the Portuguese mission responsible for Sri Lanka.

Financial requirement — important caution: AIMA's higher-education Article 91 page requires proof of sufficient resources and refers to the general subsistence rules. However, AIMA's currently published general subsistence page still displays the 2025 minimum wage of €870, so it is not a reliable standalone 2026 student budget figure. The 2026 amount and the consular method of calculating funds must be confirmed with the competent Portuguese mission before quoting a fixed monthly/yearly threshold. Crucially, Article 91(5) exempts students admitted to a higher-education institution specifically approved under the relevant ministerial procedure from submitting proof of tuition payment and means of subsistence. Do not assume every recognised university is on that approved list; verify the exact institution's status. Keep tuition, accommodation and living budget separate.

Residence permit duration: Under Article 91(2), the higher-education student residence permit is valid for up to three years, renewable for equal periods; if the programme is shorter than three years, the permit is issued for the programme duration. EU/multilateral mobility or inter-institutional agreement cases have a distinct two-year/duration-of-programme rule under Article 91(3). The European Commission's Portugal page still contains older one-year descriptions, so for current higher-education duration use the current AIMA page and law.

Work during studies: AIMA states that holders of a study residence permit may undertake employed or self-employed professional activity supplementary to the study activity that formed the basis of the visa (Article 97 of Law 23/2007). The AIMA student page does not state a fixed student-specific weekly hour cap. Do not advertise a blanket 20-hour or 40-hour rule without checking the current labour-law rules, contract, study compatibility and any restrictions for the student's specific permit/category. Secondary-school students, unpaid trainees and volunteers have different conditions.

After studies: Portuguese law provides distinct routes for certain graduates to change status and work: Article 122(1)(o) refers to graduates of secondary or first-cycle higher education; Article 122(1)(p) refers to graduates of second/third-cycle higher education or researchers. Eligibility, qualification proof and application conditions must be checked before advising; this is not an automatic open work permit or guaranteed job-search period.

Family and counsellor checks: The EU Immigration Portal describes family-reunification rights for study-permit holders, subject to the applicable conditions. Confirm the current AIMA family route, proof of relationship, housing and financial conditions separately. Before advising, record exact institution and whether it has Article 91(5) approval, programme level, tuition paid, source of funds, accommodation, insurance, visa mission and current AIMA appointment process.

Official references: AIMA — Article 91 higher-education student residence permit; AIMA — means of subsistence (page still shows 2025 €870 reference); gov.pt — study residence visa documents (updated 6 Aug 2026; warns page may be outdated); Diário da República — Law 23/2007, Articles 62, 91, 97 and 122; European Commission — Student in Portugal (17 Sep 2025; some permit-duration text older than current AIMA law). Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Malta — Student D-visa and study residence permit (third-country nationals)

Course and eligibility: Identità states that a third-country national accepted into a full-time higher-education course at MQF Level 5 or above may apply for a study residence permit, provided the institution is licensed by the Malta Further and Higher Education Authority (MFHEA) and the qualification is recognised by MQRIC. Part-time courses are not eligible under this route. Identità also says primary and secondary private educational institutions may be accepted, while government primary/secondary institutions are not accepted under this framework. Courses below MQF Level 5 may require a different national visa/residence route; verify the exact course and provider before taking a deposit.

Visa before travel: Identità's current visa FAQ says non-EU students intending to study for 105 days or more must obtain a National Long-Stay Study D-visa before travelling, using the Central Visa Unit Extended service/VFS appointment process. After arrival, the student applies in person/through the relevant Identità process for the study residence permit and biometrics. Do not advise entering as a tourist and converting to study; Identità says a student must have intended to study before travel and meet the applicable visa route. Short courses have different rules.

Documents and preparation: Course acceptance and enrolment confirmation; tuition payment receipt (Identità FAQ describes at least the first semester/year as applicable); passport; completed study permit application; proof of sufficient funds and access to them in Malta; accommodation evidence; comprehensive health insurance covering the intended stay; return-travel funds/evidence; and any required health screening. Foreign public documents may need apostille/legalisation and certified English translation. Minors need parental/legal-guardian authorisation. Follow the current Identità/VFS checklist for the applicant's residence country and course category.

Financial evidence: Identità's current Study FAQ describes the required minimum as 60% of Malta's annual national minimum wage or the applicable poverty-line benchmark, whichever is higher, pro-rated to the household size and duration of studies. It asks for recent bank statements (the FAQ describes the preceding 3 months), proof that funds held abroad can be accessed in Malta, and sponsor declaration/ID/capacity evidence where relevant. Scholarships/stipends should be documented. The published FAQ does not give a single fixed euro amount for all 2026 cases; calculate using the official benchmark in force on the application date and confirm with Identità. Keep tuition separate from living funds.

Health insurance: Identità requires comprehensive health insurance for the planned stay; its FAQ describes coverage for medical assistance and hospitalisation. Check the exact policy wording, territorial validity and any exemption for the student's institution/category with Identità. Do not assume basic travel insurance is sufficient.

Work during study: The official Identità Central Visa Unit states that eligible full-time MQF Level 5+ students may work up to 20 hours per week from the first year, after a residence permit is issued and subject to a Jobsplus employment licence. Students in other qualifying courses exceeding 90 days may be allowed up to 20 hours/week only after the first 3 months of study, with a Type D visa and Jobsplus licence; labour-market/sector conditions may apply. The European Commission's portal describes 20 hours/week for MQRIC levels 1–4 after 12 weeks, subject to licence. Never tell a student that a job is guaranteed or that they may start work before the licence is issued.

After graduation: Identità provides a post-study residence route of up to 9 months for eligible non-EU students who successfully complete an MQF Level 5+ course and apply while their study permit is still valid. The purpose is to seek employment or set up a business; it is not an automatic work permit. A separate employment authorisation/single permit is required before starting work. Confirm current application form, funds, insurance and eligibility directly with Identità.

Counsellor checkpoints: Verify the institution on the MFHEA licensed-institutions register and the exact programme's MQF/MQRIC level; confirm course is full-time; check D-visa/VFS availability for Sri Lanka; calculate funds at application date; confirm insurance; and explain that part-time work is conditional, limited and not guaranteed.

Official references: Identità — Visa FAQ (105-day Study D-visa rule and student visa process); Identità — Non-EU Study FAQ (eligibility, documents, financial formula, post-study route); Identità — Student Visa Employment (20-hour work conditions); MFHEA — licensed institution register; European Commission — Student in Malta (1 April 2025; compare against newer Identità guidance). Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Cyprus — Third-country student entry permit and temporary residence

Eligibility and entry route: A non-EU student must be accepted for full-time studies by a higher-education institution registered/recognised under Cyprus law, or another eligible recognised educational institution for an eligible diploma-level programme. Obtain the required entry permit/visa before travel through the competent Cyprus authority/mission. The Migration Department states that an application cannot be lodged from inside Cyprus when the person is there as a tourist/visitor. Do not advise a Sri Lankan applicant to enter as a visitor and then convert to student status.

Arrival deadline and residence card: The current Cyprus Migration Department student page instructs the student to register in the Aliens' Register and apply for a temporary residence permit within 7 days of arrival. This is stricter/more specific than the European Commission portal's older wording (within one week/10 days); use the current national Migration Department 7-day instruction. Biometrics, passport, official translations/certification and supporting documents are required. The permit is normally issued for up to one year and renewed annually until the programme is completed, subject to satisfactory academic progress and continuing eligibility.

Documents: Admission/acceptance and full-time enrolment; passport; entry permit/visa; proof of sufficient funds for living costs and study costs; health insurance; police clearance/criminal-record certificate; medical tests/certificates required by the authorities; accommodation details and tuition evidence where requested. Documents must be officially translated and duly certified. Minors need parental authorisation. Check the mission-specific checklist for Sri Lanka and any institution-specific submission process.

Financial evidence: The Migration Department's national student page requires sufficient financial resources but does not publish one universal fixed euro amount on that page. The University of Cyprus International Students Support Office publishes an institutional requirement of €7,000 available balance/financial ability for its own student-entry process, with specified bank-letter and parent-sponsor conditions. This is not a national statutory minimum and must not be applied to every Cyprus institution. Obtain the exact current financial checklist from the chosen institution and Migration Department; include tuition, living, accommodation and return travel separately.

Fees and processing time: The Migration Department page lists €70 for entry permit plus residence permit, €140 where the entry/residence permit and ARC combination applies, €55 for residence permit issue/renewal or change of status, and €70 first Aliens Register registration (some combined routes list €125). It indicates up to one month for a complete student entry-permit application and up to three months for a complete residence-permit application; incomplete files can take longer. Confirm the correct fee combination before collecting payment.

Work during studies: Students may work outside study hours only in occupations/sectors specified by the current Minister of Labour order and subject to the conditions for the activity. The maximum is 20 hours per week during teaching periods and up to 38 hours per week during class breaks. The student must first sign an employment contract and submit it to the District Labour Office for approval; the study timetable must accompany the contract. The law's sector list and labour-market conditions matter—do not describe this as unrestricted work permission or guaranteed employment.

After graduation: Cyprus allows an eligible graduate to apply for a separate residence permit for up to 12 months to seek employment or establish a business, provided the intended job/business corresponds to the level of completed studies. The minimum qualification level is EQF Level 7 (master's) or higher. This is not an automatic permit for every diploma/bachelor graduate and is not permission to start working without the appropriate authorisation.

Counsellor checkpoints: Verify the exact institution's recognition/registration, course level and full-time status; get the institution's current funds and document checklist; confirm the Sri Lanka competent visa channel; plan the 7-day arrival registration; check the current permitted occupations and labour-office contract approval; and assess post-study eligibility only for master's/doctoral-level graduates.

Official references: Cyprus Migration Department — Students (national legislation, 7-day registration, fees, processing, work and post-study rules); University of Cyprus — Non-EU student entry permit and its institution-specific €7,000 funds evidence; European Commission — Student in Cyprus (cross-check; older timing details differ from current national page). Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Poland — National D study visa and temporary residence permit

Who qualifies: A non-EU applicant normally needs a Polish national D visa for a stay over 90 days, issued for full-time first- or second-cycle higher education, long-cycle master's studies, doctoral school or a legally qualifying preparatory course. For the study-based temporary residence permit, the institution must be approved by the Polish Minister of the Interior for admitting foreign students, or fall within a statutory exemption, and must not be subject to a ban on admitting foreign nationals. Part-time, postgraduate and MBA programmes are not automatically treated as "studies" for this permit; a different residence basis may apply. Check the exact institution and course before counselling.

Sri Lankan application route: The Polish Embassy in New Delhi's official jurisdiction page explicitly lists Sri Lanka under its consular jurisdiction. It directs study-visa applicants to register through e-Konsulat and submit personally at the designated Visa Application Centre; the current checklist and appointment process must be checked immediately before booking. Applications may be lodged up to 6 months before travel and, as a rule, no later than 15 calendar days before intended travel. The Embassy's current page lists a separate mandatory study-visa form in addition to e-Konsulat. Do not assume the same VFS centre, appointment slots or fee as India-based applicants without checking the current Sri Lanka arrangements.

Documents: Visa application and passport; admission/enrolment certificate from the approved institution; evidence of tuition/semester payment where applicable; evidence of accommodation; sufficient funds for living costs, accommodation and return travel; health/travel medical insurance; education certificates and recognition/legalisation documents; and any additional documents requested by the Consul. The national visa insurance requirement is generally at least €30,000 medical cover for the intended stay, including urgent medical treatment, hospitalisation and medical return, with the insurer paying the provider directly. The Embassy may request an interview or extra evidence. Follow its latest checklist, not a generic agent checklist.

2026 financial evidence — Sri Lanka jurisdiction checklist: The Polish Embassy in New Delhi's current study checklist (14 May 2026) requires proof of living funds of PLN 1,010 per month for the intended stay, accommodation costs separately, and PLN 2,500 for return travel to a non-neighbouring country such as Sri Lanka. It accepts specified evidence such as travel cheques, bank credit-card-limit certificate, qualifying bank certificate in Poland/EU/EFTA/Switzerland, scholarship award, or employment/salary evidence; sponsor support requires a notarised sponsorship letter covering travel, living and accommodation costs, with supporting evidence. Check the PDF itself and whether the Embassy has issued a newer checklist before each application. This is a mission checklist benchmark; it is not a universal tuition-inclusive budget.

Residence permit after arrival: If the D visa will not cover the full intended stay, apply for a study temporary residence permit while legally in Poland and no later than the final day of legal stay. Since 27 April 2026, study residence applications must be submitted electronically through the MOS portal.
