The university must complete and digitally sign its online annex before the student can submit. The applicant must electronically sign, retain the official submission acknowledgement (UPO), and later attend in person for fingerprints/original passport checks. The first permit is normally 15 months (or the shorter justified study period plus 3 months); subsequent permits may cover the remaining study period plus 3 months, up to 3 years. Official fee: PLN 340 stamp duty plus residence card fee (standard PLN 100; eligible students may pay PLN 50). Confirm current payment accounts and reduced-fee eligibility.

Work during studies: A non-EU student enrolled in full-time studies and holding a Polish national visa issued for study or a study temporary residence permit may work without a separate work permit. This exemption does not automatically cover part-time students or people staying under another visa/permit. Studies must remain the main purpose of stay and academic progress must be maintained. There is no fixed weekly-hour limit stated in the official Polish student work guidance for this exemption; do not invent a 20-hour cap. Employment is not guaranteed and should not be used as the sole proof of funds.

After graduation: A graduate of full-time studies at a Polish higher-education institution may work without a work permit, but must still have a valid legal basis to remain in Poland. A qualifying graduate may apply once for a temporary residence permit of up to 9 months to seek work or establish a business. It is not automatic and the application must meet the current Office for Foreigners' conditions.

Admission and recognition checkpoint: For applicants with Sri Lankan secondary-school qualifications, check whether the certificate is recognised for entry to Polish higher education and whether a NAWA recognition statement is required. The official Study in Poland guidance notes B2 language proficiency requirements in specified foreign-certificate cases, and possible entrance examinations. Do not promise admission or visa approval based only on A/L results; obtain the institution's written assessment and confirm legalisation/recognition steps.

Official references: Embassy of Poland in New Delhi — D-type study visa (Sri Lanka jurisdiction, appointment and insurance); Embassy study-visa checklist dated 14 May 2026 (funds and sponsor documents); Study.gov.pl — visa and MOS residence procedure (27 April 2026 electronic application); Office for Foreigners — study temporary residence conditions and financial rules; Study.gov.pl — work during studies and 9-month graduate permit; Study in Poland — admissions, NAWA recognition and B2 guidance. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Czech Republic (Czechia) — Long-term study visa / study residence permit

Who qualifies: A third-country national may apply for a long-term visa for studies where the activity meets Section 64 of Act No. 326/1999. The common cases are a university-accredited study programme; qualifying language/professional preparation for an accredited university programme organised by a public university, EU programme or international agreement; and secondary/higher vocational studies within an exchange programme. Not every private course or language-school course qualifies. Check the programme's legal classification and institution before accepting a student.

Visa or residence permit: A long-term study visa is for stays over 90 days and is valid for up to 1 year. If the course/stay is longer than a year, the student needs a long-term residence permit for studies. A direct application for a study residence permit may be possible at a Czech diplomatic mission. The official Information Portal lists a 60-day processing time and a consular fee of CZK 2,500 for the long-term study visa; processing can take longer if the application is suspended or documents are requested. A visa holder must register with the Foreign Police within 3 business days after arrival, unless an applicable accommodation-provider registration exception applies.

Sri Lankan applicants: The Czech Embassy in New Delhi has territorial jurisdiction over Sri Lanka. Its current appointment page (updated 16 October 2026) specifically includes citizens of Sri Lanka applying for long-term visas/residence permits for study and educational activities. Applicants must follow the current appointment booking instructions and quotas on that page. Do not use an expired appointment announcement or assume walk-in submission is possible.

Documents: Application form, passport, proof of accommodation, proof of sufficient funds or official evidence that costs are covered, photograph, original confirmation of studies/enrolment, and a criminal-record-equivalent certificate from the applicant's country of citizenship (not required under age 15). Under-18 applicants need parental consent. The authority may request medical reports or police certificates from other countries where the applicant lived for a total of 6 months in the preceding 3 years. Before visa collection, provide qualifying travel medical insurance for the first 90 days plus comprehensive insurance for the remainder, or comprehensive insurance for the full permitted stay. Document legalisation, translation and format requirements must be checked with the Embassy.

Financial proof — do not use an outdated fixed total: The Czech Ministry of the Interior's public finance page currently displays a subsistence minimum of CZK 3,130/month, stated as effective from 1 January 2023. It gives the formula for a long-term visa: 15 times the subsistence minimum plus twice the minimum for each full month after the first. For a long-term residence permit, it gives the same formula. The page's date is old, so do not present CZK 3,130 or sample totals as a confirmed 2026 figure without checking the current subsistence minimum and the Embassy's latest checklist on the filing date. For qualifying study cases, funds may be evidenced by an undertaking from a public authority/legal entity to cover the monthly subsistence minimum, proof the school covers all study-related costs, or an eligible scholarship; any shortfall may need the applicant's own evidence. Tuition, accommodation and realistic living budget should be recorded separately.

Work during study: A third-country national studying a university-accredited programme on a full-time basis has free access to the Czech labour market. This means a separate work permit is not required, but the student must still hold a valid residence status and remain enrolled/meet study conditions. The official guidance does not state a general weekly student-hours cap for this free-access category; do not invent a 20-hour limit. Part-time or non-accredited courses do not automatically qualify.

After graduation: A graduate who held a study long-term residence permit and completed a university-accredited study programme in the Czech Republic may apply before the current permit expires for a long-term residence permit to seek employment or start a business. It is valid for up to 9 months, cannot be extended, and gives free access to the labour market once granted. It is not available automatically to every course attendee or a student who has not completed an eligible accredited programme.

Counsellor checkpoints: Confirm Section 64 eligibility and accreditation in writing; use the New Delhi Embassy's Sri Lanka appointment instructions; calculate financial proof using the current subsistence-minimum amount rather than copying an old web-page total; confirm insurance and translation/legalisation; explain that work rights depend on full-time accredited study; and record the student's arrival-registration deadline.

Official references: Czech Ministry of Interior — Long-term Visa for Studies (eligibility, fee, documents, 60-day processing, arrival registration); Czech Ministry of Interior — Study Residence Permit; Czech Ministry of Interior — Proof of Funds (note 2023 subsistence-minimum date); Czech Ministry of Interior — Free Access to Labour Market; Czech Ministry of Interior — Graduate job-seeking/business permit (up to 9 months); Czech Embassy New Delhi — Study appointment instructions for Sri Lankan citizens (updated 16 October 2026); Embassy territorial jurisdiction — Sri Lanka. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Austria — Student residence permit (2026)

Who qualifies / correct route

Non-EU applicants generally need an Austrian Residence Permit – Student (Aufenthaltsbewilligung – Student) for eligible regular degree programmes at recognised universities, universities of applied sciences, accredited private universities or recognised teacher-training institutions. Some non-degree programmes qualify only under specific conditions (including at least 40 ECTS in the listed university-course routes); a language-only course is not enough. Obtain the exact admission letter before advising on eligibility.

Application route and duration

Usually apply in person through the competent Austrian embassy/consulate in the country of residence and wait for the residence-authority decision. After approval, a Visa D may be needed to enter Austria and collect the residence card. The student residence permit is normally issued for 12 months and must be renewed with evidence of academic progress. Do not describe this as a simple short-stay Schengen student visa.

2026 financial evidence

OeAD lists monthly maintenance amounts of €722.58 for applicants under 24 and €1,308.39 for applicants aged 24 or older. Evidence is generally required in advance for up to 12 months in an account in the applicant's name that can be accessed from Austria. If accommodation costs exceed €386.43/month, the excess must be covered by additional funds; health-insurance costs must also be covered. These are living-cost benchmarks, not the complete tuition-and-travel budget. Confirm the applicant's exact age on the relevant date and the current application checklist.

Documents and sponsor funds

Prepare passport, recent ICAO photo, admission evidence, proof of funds and their source, accommodation evidence (normally at least 3 months), comprehensive health insurance covering all risks in Austria, and a declaration of regular financial commitments. For first applications, a recent police clearance may also be requested and may need legalisation and translation. Parent/sponsor support can be used, but keep the sponsor's six-month bank statements, income evidence and a signed declaration that funds are freely available to the student. The authority may request proof of the origin of funds.

Work during study

A student residence permit does not itself authorise employment. The employer must obtain an AMS employment permit before work starts. Up to 20 hours/week can be permitted without a labour-market test; above 20 hours requires a labour-market test. The student's primary purpose must remain study. Never promise a job or that part-time income will cover tuition or living costs.

After graduation

Eligible graduates may apply before the student permit expires for a one-time 12-month extension to seek work or start a business. If a suitable job is found and the conditions are met, a graduate may switch to the Red-White-Red Card route. This is not an automatic permanent-residence or work entitlement.

Official sources: OeAD — Residence Permit Student (2026 checklist, funds, documents and work); Austrian Government — Student checklist (updated 5 November 2026); Migration.gv.at — Study in Austria and labour-market access; Federal Ministry of the Interior — Residence Permit Student and graduate extension. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required; confirm the competent Austrian mission's Sri Lanka-specific checklist before lodgement.

Luxembourg — Student Visa D and Residence Permit (2026)

Who qualifies / eligible study

A non-EU applicant planning to stay over 90 days must be admitted to a Luxembourg higher-education establishment for a full-time study cycle leading to a higher-education degree or diploma. Doctoral researchers are included. The official list includes the University of Luxembourg, establishments awarding the BTS advanced technician's certificate, and approved private higher-education establishments. Do not assume every private college, short course or part-time programme qualifies.

Before travel: two-step procedure

First obtain temporary authorisation to stay and apply for a long-stay Visa D through the competent Luxembourg diplomatic/consular representation in the country of origin or residence, or a neighbouring country if Luxembourg has no long-stay visa representation there. The visa is normally valid for 6 months; the stated decision period is in principle up to 60 days, subject to complete documents. Long-stay visas can only be issued by Luxembourg or Belgian embassies. Confirm which mission accepts applications from Sri Lanka before booking.

After arrival

Declare arrival with the commune where the student lives, undergo the required medical check-up, and apply for the student residence permit before the Visa D expires. The student must complete these steps in Luxembourg; a Visa D alone is not the final residence card.

2026 financial evidence

The official student guidance sets the minimum monthly resources at 80% of the social inclusion income (REVIS) currently in force, plus enough to cover the return trip. The amount is indexed and can change, so VisaWatch deliberately does not hard-code a euro figure: calculate it using the REVIS rate in force on the application date. Acceptable evidence may include a scholarship or student-loan certificate, a bank certificate in the student's name, qualifying parental support with proof of the parents' solvency and ability to cover at least 12 months of living, healthcare and education costs plus return travel, or a legally compliant Luxembourg-based guarantor undertaking. Free accommodation/material benefits may be considered. Budget tuition and actual living costs separately.

Documents

Prepare full passport copy, recent criminal-record extract from the current country of residence (normally issued within the last 3 months for adults), admission proof, financial evidence, return-travel funds, and Schengen travel health insurance for the Visa D stage covering medical treatment/hospitalisation, repatriation and funeral costs. Under-18 applicants need parental authorisation. Documents not in English, French or German require a certified translation. The authority may request further documents.

Work during study

Students may undertake paid work for an average of up to 15 hours per week, outside study time. This limit does not apply to work during school holidays, qualifying doctoral research work, or University of Luxembourg assistant contracts. The employer must submit the required written declaration to Immigration within 3 working days after the student's first workday and provide the contract and residence-permit copy. Do not tell clients that a job is guaranteed or that earnings will cover tuition or living costs.

After graduation

The official post-study job-search/entrepreneurship route is limited to eligible graduates who successfully complete a final higher-education diploma leading to a Master's degree, or successfully defend a doctoral thesis in Luxembourg. The application must be made in time before the student permit expires. If approved, the residence permit is valid for 12 months and is non-renewable; any later salaried or self-employed activity must relate to the academic training/research and requires the appropriate status. Do not promise this route to every bachelor's graduate.

Official sources: Luxembourg Guichet.lu — Visa D for student residence permit (last update 1 October 2026); Guichet.lu — Post-study job-search/entrepreneurship residence permit (last update 1 October 2026). Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required; confirm current REVIS amount, competent mission for Sri Lankan applicants and institution/course eligibility before client advice.

Slovakia — Temporary Residence for Study (2026)

Who qualifies

Third-country applicants whose main purpose is study generally need temporary residence for study for stays over 90 days. Eligible categories include full-time university students, certain full-time secondary students under 20, post-secondary/vocational/specialisation students under 23, and university-organised language or professional preparation for university study. A private course or language school is not automatically eligible; check the statutory category and institution.

Application route and duration

Apply in person at the Slovak diplomatic mission competent for the applicant's citizenship or residence, or at the Foreign Police only where the applicant already has a qualifying lawful status in Slovakia/another EU Member State. University study residence may be granted for the expected programme duration, up to a maximum of six years. The Ministry of Foreign Affairs guidance states a decision within 30 days after a complete application is received. This is a temporary residence permit, not simply a Schengen C visa.

2026 financial evidence

Slovak authorities require financial security for the stay. The general residence guidance uses the subsistence minimum for each month of stay, and twelve times the monthly amount if the stay exceeds one year. The subsistence minimum changed to €295.22/month from 1 July 2026; the 12-month benchmark is therefore €3,542.64. Use the exact duration and rule applicable to the student's case; re-check the indexed amount on the filing date. Student guidance accepts, among other evidence, the student's last three months' bank statements, scholarship confirmation, confirmation from a legal entity providing funds, or a sponsor affidavit supported by the sponsor's last three months' bank statements. Do not present the amount as a tuition-inclusive budget.

Documents and accommodation

Prepare valid passport, recent 3 × 3.5 cm colour photograph, university/school admission or other proof of eligible study, criminal-record extracts for the applicant's nationality and relevant countries of residence during the previous three years, and financial evidence. Documents generally must be no older than 90 days unless a specific exception applies. The general residence checklist requires accommodation evidence for at least six months (or the entire shorter permit period); confirm the exact study-specific exemption and checklist with the receiving mission/Foreign Police. Foreign documents may require apostille/superlegalisation and official Slovak translation.

Work during study

A university student with temporary residence for study may work without a separate employment permit up to 80 hours per month. Working more requires the appropriate change of residence purpose to employment. Other study categories may have different rules; do not apply the university-student 80-hour rule to every student.

After arrival

After the residence is granted, enter Slovakia within 180 days. Report the start of stay to the Foreign Police within 3 working days of arrival. Arrange health insurance within 3 working days after receiving the residence card and submit the required medical certificate within 30 days of receiving the residence permit. Check the current authority instructions for the precise certificate and accepted medical facility.

After graduation

A university graduate who successfully completes studies in Slovakia may apply to renew the temporary residence for up to nine months to seek employment or start a business. Apply in person at the competent Foreign Police Department no later than the last day of the current permit's validity, with the diploma/completion confirmation, passport, financial and other required evidence. This is not an automatic work permit or guaranteed employment.

Sri Lankan applicant — important consular distinction

The Slovak Embassy in New Delhi has territorial competence for Sri Lanka for national visa/residence matters. Italy's Embassy in Colombo represents Slovakia for certain short-stay Schengen C visa matters only; that representation does not extend to Slovak national visas. Do not send a long-stay student residence application to the Italian Embassy in Colombo on the basis of short-stay representation. Confirm appointment and submission instructions with the Slovak Embassy in New Delhi before booking travel.

Official sources: Slovak Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs — Foreign students in Slovakia (documents, work and arrival duties); IOM Migration Information Centre — Temporary residence for study (updated 22 July 2026); IOM — Subsistence minimum €295.22 from 1 July 2026; Slovak MFA — Residence financial means and accommodation; Slovak MFA — Visa representation agreements (national visas excluded); Slovak MFA — Diplomatic mission territorial competence list (Sri Lanka: New Delhi). Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Slovenia — Temporary Residence Permit for Study (2026)

Who qualifies

Third-country nationals (including Sri Lankan citizens) staying more than 90 days for education may qualify if enrolled in a publicly recognised education or study programme leading to a publicly recognised qualification, at an accredited higher-education institution, or in another recognised exchange/training category. A private language course or unrecognised provider is not automatically eligible. Verify the exact institution, programme and legal basis before accepting a case.

Correct permit and application route

The main route is a Temporary Residence Permit for Study under Article 44 of the Foreigners Act, not an ordinary short-stay Schengen C visa. It is normally issued for the duration of the study programme but for no longer than one year at a time; renew before expiry while the student remains eligible. Sri Lankan citizens need a visa to enter Slovenia. The first residence application is generally lodged in person at the competent Slovenian diplomatic/consular mission abroad; where the law allows an in-country application, it must be lodged before the lawful stay expires. A national D visa is a separate entry document and may be required depending on the route and decision. Do not assume a short-stay visa representation in Colombo can accept a national residence application.

2026 financial evidence

The basic monthly amount of minimum income (OZMD) is €507.43 from 1 March 2026. The University of Ljubljana's 2026/27 international-student guidance applies this as the current monthly means benchmark and says it must be evidenced for the academic year. For a 12-month planning example, this is €6,089.16; it is a subsistence evidence benchmark, not tuition or a full realistic living budget. Recalculate if the application period or statutory amount changes. Evidence may include funds in a bank account in Slovenia or abroad, scholarship/grant, qualifying income, or a support declaration from parents/legal guardians who are legally obliged to maintain the student under their home-country law. The official government page describes the categories of acceptable resources; check the receiving administrative unit's exact evidence format.

Documents

Prepare application form, valid passport and copies, biometric photograph, admission/enrolment certificate, proof of health insurance covering at least emergency healthcare in Slovenia, sufficient funds, and a criminal-record certificate from the country of origin (first application). The University of Ljubljana advises the police certificate should be no older than three months and officially translated into Slovene, with apostille/certification where applicable. Further documents may be requested by the competent Administrative Unit or diplomatic mission. Confirm the current fee and translation/legalisation requirements with the receiving office.

Work during study

There is no general 20-hour weekly cap stated in the official study guidance. A student with study residence may perform temporary/occasional student work through the authorised student-work service if they meet the legal student-status conditions and have the required referral. The Slovenian Government states the minimum gross hourly rate for student work is €8.98 from 5 February 2026. For ordinary employment alongside study, the employer must follow the Employment Service information-sheet procedure and labour-market check; do not describe this as unrestricted employment. Distinguish student-work referral from regular employment authorisation and never promise a job or income sufficient to fund tuition.

After arrival

After the residence permit is issued, the student must register their temporary address. The University of Ljubljana guidance says students in private accommodation must register within 8 days of receiving the permit; dormitory accommodation is generally registered by the landlord. Follow the deadline and method given by the local Administrative Unit and accommodation provider.

After graduation

Slovenia's official student guidance does not establish a universal automatic 9- or 12-month post-study job-search visa for every graduate. If the student obtains a job, the employer may initiate the appropriate employment authorisation/information-sheet process and the graduate must apply for the corresponding further temporary residence status before the current permit expires. Do not promise a dedicated post-study stay unless the applicant qualifies under a specific current statutory route.

Sri Lankan applicant — consular check

GOV.SI confirms Sri Lankan citizens require a visa to enter Slovenia and directs applicants to the competent Slovenian diplomatic mission for long-stay visa details. A D-type long-stay visa application can only be filed at a competent Slovenian diplomatic mission or consular post, not at another Schengen state's embassy acting only as a short-stay visa representative. Before taking a deposit or booking travel, confirm which Slovenian mission currently accepts residence/study applications from Sri Lanka and its appointment/document checklist.

Official sources: Infotujci — Temporary residence permit for study; Study in Slovenia — Temporary residence permit for studies; University of Ljubljana — 2026/27 funds, documents and fees; GOV.SI — Basic amount of minimum income (€507.43 from 1 March 2026); GOV.SI — Student work and minimum gross hourly rate (€8.98 from 5 February 2026); Employment Service — Student employment information-sheet procedure; GOV.SI — Visa requirements for Sri Lankan citizens. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Estonia — Student D Visa and Temporary Residence Permit (2026)

Who qualifies / eligible programme

Third-country nationals (including Sri Lankan citizens) need a legal basis to study in Estonia. A study temporary residence permit (TRP) is available for recognised education categories under the Aliens Act. At higher-education level, the statutory route is for full-time first- or second-cycle study at an institutionally accredited institution, or doctoral study. Part-time degree students, visiting/exchange students and external learners may not qualify for a study TRP; check the exact programme with the university before making a visa promise.

Correct route by course duration

For a degree programme of up to one year, the student will generally use a long-stay national D visa; a study TRP is not normally granted for that short duration. For degree studies longer than one year, the student generally enters on a D visa and applies for the study TRP at the Police and Border Guard Board (PBGB) in Estonia after arrival. The D visa may be issued for up to 365 days within 12 consecutive months. The D-visa decision at a foreign representation may take 10–30 calendar days; TRP decisions are generally made within two months after a complete application/deficiencies are resolved, but university guidance advises allowing longer in some cases. Never plan travel on the assumption that submitting a TRP application automatically extends lawful stay.

2026 financial evidence

For study purposes, Estonia's Ministry of Foreign Affairs lists €880 per month for the applicant on a D-visa application. For a 12-month planning example this is €10,560. The D-visa file must include evidence of income for the three months immediately before applying, showing amount, regularity and source. University guidance also uses €880/month as the 2026 sufficient legal income benchmark for the study TRP. This is a maintenance benchmark, not tuition, accommodation deposits or a complete budget; verify the period and evidence format with the receiving mission/PBGB. Do not assume a sponsor letter alone is sufficient without the sponsor's financial evidence.

Documents

Prepare passport (issued within the previous 10 years, at least two blank pages, valid at least 3 months beyond visa expiry), completed form, 35 × 45 mm ICAO photo, travel medical insurance, proof of funds and preceding three months' income, evidence of the study purpose/admission, travel booking or other transport evidence, family/biographical data forms and visa fee. Foreign public documents for D-visa applications must be apostilled or legalised and translated into Estonian or English. For the TRP, the university normally sends the invitation to the PBGB; prepare the application, proof of income and health insurance. Higher-education applicants must meet the institution's language-of-instruction requirement.

Work during study

A student holding a study TRP may work in Estonia without a separate work permit and without a fixed statutory weekly hours cap, provided the work does not interfere with full-time studies. Full-time academic progress remains essential: university guidance refers to completing at least 75% of the study load (45 ECTS) at the end of each academic year. If work interferes with studies or academic progress is not maintained, residence status may be at risk. Do not market this as unrestricted work or guaranteed employment.

After arrival and residence obligations

After the study TRP is granted, register the place of residence in Estonia's Population Register within one month. Maintain sufficient legal income and valid health insurance throughout the permit. If studies are interrupted or the required study load is not completed, the permit may expire or be revoked; the Aliens Act provides that in specified cases the study TRP expires 30 days after interruption/failure to complete the required curriculum.

After graduation

Do not promise a universal automatic post-study job-search visa or a guaranteed work period. A graduate who obtains eligible employment must qualify for the appropriate employment-based residence status under the rules then in force. Time spent in Estonia on a study TRP counts only half when calculating the residence period for long-term resident status if the person later obtains a residence permit on another basis; study residence alone does not directly qualify the student for long-term residence.

Sri Lankan applicant — critical consular instruction

The Estonian Embassy in New Delhi's official page states that Sri Lankan residents applying for a Schengen C visa must use the German Embassy in Colombo, but that representation does not accept Estonian long-stay D-visa applications. Sri Lankan applicants for a D visa or study residence permit should contact the Estonian Embassy in New Delhi, book the appointment personally and confirm the latest document checklist and territorial competence before travel. Never direct a student to the German Embassy in Colombo for an Estonian student D visa.

Official sources: Estonian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — D visa, documents and €880/month study funds; Estonian Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lanka visa and residence applications (C-visa representation excludes D visas); Estonian Aliens Act — Study TRP eligibility, duration, employment and expiry rules; University of Tartu — 2026 visa/TRP guidance and work conditions; Study in Estonia — D visa and residence permit procedure; European Commission — Student in Estonia. Checked 16 November 2026. Human counsellor verification required.

Latvia — Student Long-Stay D Visa and Temporary Residence Permit (2026)

Who qualifies / eligible programme

For a Sri Lankan third-country national, first obtain admission to a Latvian education institution eligible to host international students and confirm the exact programme, campus, study level and full-time status. A residence permit is generally required for a stay exceeding 90 days in a six-month period. Do not assume every private college, short course or part-time programme qualifies; ask the institution to confirm its OCMA invitation route.

Correct route and application sequence for Sri Lankan applicants

The Latvian Embassy in New Delhi has territorial competence for Sri Lankan nationals' long-stay study visa applications. The Embassy's Sri Lankan-national instructions say the student applies for a national D visa through the Colombo VFS centre under the New Delhi Embassy route; after receiving the 90-day D visa and entering Latvia, the student must complete the study residence-permit process with the Office of Citizenship and Migration Affairs (OCMA). Complete the mandatory Hello Verify document verification first and obtain the transaction number — the Embassy says a VFS appointment will not be granted without it. VFS/Embassy instructions also require a video conference. Follow the current institution invitation and OCMA instructions for the exact sequence and any pre-travel residence-permit steps.

2026 financial means — benchmark, not a full budget

Latvia's PMLP page on necessary subsistence, updated 16 March 2026, lists €780 per month as the general minimum monthly amount for residence-permit cases not covered by special categories. A 12-month illustration is €9,360. However, the Embassy's Sri Lankan student checklist asks for an original private bank statement showing the last three months' transactions and a bank balance confirmation; it does not state a student-specific total in that checklist. Therefore, treat €780/month as the published general benchmark only and obtain written confirmation from the university/OCMA/Embassy of the exact amount and period required for the individual student. Tuition, accommodation, insurance, travel and other living costs must be budgeted separately.

Documents — Sri Lankan Embassy checklist

The Embassy checklist includes: passport valid at least three months beyond intended departure from Latvia/another Schengen state with at least two blank pages; printed and signed national D visa e-application; recent 35 × 45 mm photo; OCMA-approved invitation from the Latvian education institution; signed study agreement and the Academic Information Centre (AIC) certificate; institution confirmation of matriculation/need to arrive to complete the programme; original bank statement for the previous three months with stamped/signed balance confirmation; international card usable in euros; proof of accommodation; prior education originals and copies; and Hello Verify confirmation. If a parent sponsors the student, provide a notarised sponsorship declaration plus the sponsor's employment contract and last three payslips, or the applicable company registration and business bank evidence for a self-employed sponsor. Confirm whether police clearance, apostille/legalisation and certified translations are required for the specific file.

Insurance and visa fee / processing

The Embassy's Sri Lankan checklist requires travel medical insurance valid throughout the intended 90-day visit across the Schengen area, with at least €42,600 cover for urgent medical care, emergency hospital treatment, medical transport and repatriation. The same Embassy checklist lists a €90 D visa fee and a 15-day processing period after the complete file reaches the Embassy; it notes that additional checks can extend the decision period to 60 days. VFS service, courier and video-conference charges are additional and can change. Recheck all amounts and appointment instructions on the day of booking; fees are generally non-refundable if refused.

Work during studies — check the student's exact category

The national Study in Latvia guidance states that full-time Bachelor's students may work up to 20 hours per week during studies. It describes full-time Master's and doctoral students as able to work full-time during study breaks. The European Commission's Latvia student page describes a different formulation for graduate/postgraduate students, so do not promise unlimited term-time work or quote a universal 40-hour entitlement without confirming the current Immigration Law, programme level and permit conditions with OCMA. Employment is not guaranteed and must not interfere with study obligations.

After graduation

Study in Latvia guidance describes an additional four-month period for eligible Bachelor's graduates to apply for further studies or seek work. Eligible full-time Master's and doctoral graduates may request a further nine-month job-search extension, but this is not automatic and must be requested before the existing permit expires. Confirm eligibility, deadline and work authorisation directly with OCMA. Do not advertise a guaranteed post-study job or residence outcome.

Practical counsellor checks before giving advice

Verify the institution/programme eligibility, OCMA-approved invitation, Hello Verify transaction number, Colombo VFS appointment category, passport-transmission requirements, exact funds and study-period calculation, insurance wording, current fees, and whether any additional AIC, police-clearance, apostille or translation documents apply. Keep a dated copy of the official checklist in the client file. Do not tell a student to rely on part-time wages to pay tuition or living costs, and do not submit an application until the student has checked the current Embassy/VFS instructions.

Official sources: Latvian Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lankan nationals, D visa checklist, Hello Verify, €90 fee and processing time; Latvian Embassy — student D visa process; Latvia PMLP — necessary subsistence, updated 16 March 2026; Latvia PMLP — residence permit framework; Study in Latvia — work and post-study information; European Commission — Student in Latvia. Checked 18 November 2026. Financial calculation and work-hour differences remain subject to individual OCMA/Embassy confirmation.

Lithuania — Student National D Visa and Temporary Residence Permit (2026)

Who qualifies / eligible programme

A non-EU student must be admitted to a recognised Lithuanian higher education and research institution for an eligible study programme or doctoral studies. The Law on the Legal Status of Foreigners also lists separate routes for general education, formal vocational training, traineeships and professional development; do not assume that a private short course or every vocational programme qualifies. The ordinary higher-education study temporary residence permit is issued for the study period, normally up to two years at a time; the law sets an overall maximum of eight years for study-based permits issued under the new rule effective 22 May 2026.

Visa and residence route — distinguish D visa from TRP

A national D visa is a long-stay entry visa, normally for at least 91 days and up to 12 months; it is not the same as a temporary residence permit. The current VFS Lithuania Sri Lanka page publishes a full-time study D-visa checklist for students, including the institution's electronic mediation letter. For longer degree studies, confirm the exact MIGRIS/TRP sequence with the Migration Department and the competent Lithuanian mission before paying fees or booking travel. Important: the Migration Department announced that Lithuania's external-service-provider branch in Sri Lanka closed for residence-permit applications from 1 March 2024. Do not tell clients they can lodge a Lithuanian TRP application at a Colombo VFS centre merely because the VFS page lists D-visa services.

2026 financial evidence — updated calculation and stale VFS figures

The student subsistence benchmark is 0.5 of the applicable minimum monthly wage (MMA) per month, plus a separate return-travel amount of one MMA. Lithuania's Social Security and Labour Ministry confirms that from 1 January 2026 the gross MMA is €1,153. Therefore, the 2026 planning calculation is €576.50 per month for subsistence plus €1,153 for return travel. For a 12-month period, the subsistence component alone is €6,918, plus the return amount; this is not a full study budget. Important discrepancy: the VFS Sri Lanka D-visa page still displays old figures of €277/month and €555 return funds, based on the 2025 MMA (€1,038). Do not copy those outdated values into a 2026 client quotation. Confirm the exact evidence period and accepted funds with the Embassy/Migration Department and institution on the filing date. Tuition, accommodation, insurance and realistic living costs are additional.

Documents and insurance

Typical study D-visa documents on the current VFS Sri Lanka checklist include the online application, valid passport, recent 35×45 photo, medical insurance valid for the stay throughout the Schengen area with at least €30,000 coverage, confirmed flight reservation, the institution's EPIS electronic mediation-letter number, original bank statements covering the last three months, financial sponsorship evidence where applicable, and legalised education documents. The exact residence-permit document list is generated in MIGRIS and may include accommodation, proof of funds, police/character documents and certified translations. Follow the current checklist for the applicant's nationality and case.

Work during studies — major 2026 change

From 22 May 2026, first- and second-year Bachelor's students with study residence permits issued after that date may work no more than 20 hours per week during study periods; holidays and curriculum-based practical training are excluded. Third- and fourth-year Bachelor's students, Master's students, doctoral students and medical residents may work up to 40 hours per week under the Migration Department's 2026 explanation. Do not use the older blanket statement that every student may work 40 hours/week. Check the permit issue date, study year and programme level before advising.

Application procedure changes from 1 November 2026

For first-cycle Bachelor's study applications, a student already in Lithuania may submit a first TRP application directly to the Migration Department only if they hold one of the qualifying statuses listed in the 2026 amendment (for example, a valid Lithuanian TRP or Lithuanian national D visa, an EU/Schengen residence permit, or lawful visa-free stay). A student who entered on a Schengen C visa or another country's national visa cannot use that in-country route after 1 November 2026 and must apply through an authorised external service provider. This is a procedure rule, not a visa approval guarantee. The exact provider country/centre available to a Sri Lankan citizen must be checked against the latest official list before travel.

Fees and processing — current official TRP fee

The Migration Department fee table lists €80 for examination and issuance of a study-based temporary residence permit under the general procedure. The separate new-card personalisation fee is €75 (so the standard combined state/card amount is €155, excluding any VFS/service charges where applicable). Do not quote the generic €220 TRP fee for an eligible study application. The D-visa fee and Colombo VFS service charges are separate and must be checked on the live appointment page.

After studies / family

The final-year study TRP may be extended at the student's request for up to six months after the expected end of studies under Article 46(5), subject to the law and timely application. Graduates may be able to change to an eligible work-based residence ground, but employment and a new permit are not automatic. From the 2026 amendments, immediate family reunification is available to Master's/doctoral students and residents; Bachelor's students generally face the ordinary residence-duration and other statutory conditions. Confirm the current provision for the specific family relationship.

Sri Lankan applicant — filing location warning

The current VFS Lithuania Sri Lanka page publishes a national D-visa study checklist; that does not mean Colombo accepts TRP applications. The Migration Department confirms that the Sri Lankan external-service-provider branch stopped accepting Lithuanian TRP applications from 1 March 2024. Its notice says the India branch accepts study-based TRP applications only for specified invited study/research categories. Separately, from 1 November 2026, first-cycle Bachelor's applicants already in Lithuania can lodge directly with the Migration Department only if they hold one of the qualifying statuses listed in the new law; a Schengen C visa or another country's national visa is not sufficient. Before a Sri Lankan applicant travels to India or another country to lodge a TRP, obtain written confirmation from the Migration Department/VFS of the authorised centre, nationality jurisdiction and appointment route. An Honorary Consul in Sri Lanka is not automatically authorised to accept or decide applications.

Official sources: Lithuanian Migration Department — 2026 amendments: work hours, family and procedure; Migration Department — Bachelor's student work limits; Migration Department — application procedure changes effective 1 November 2026; Lithuanian Social Security and Labour Ministry — 2026 MMA €1,153; Migration Department — residence permit fees; VFS Lithuania Sri Lanka — national D visa study checklist; Migration Department — Sri Lanka TRP service-provider closure; Lithuanian Embassy in India — visa types; European Commission — Student in Lithuania. Re-audited 18 November 2026. The VFS Sri Lanka page still shows outdated 2025 funds (€277/month and €555 return); the 2026 statutory calculation using MMA €1,153 is €576.50/month plus €1,153 return. Sri Lankan TRP filing location outside Sri Lanka must be confirmed in writing with the Migration Department/VFS before travel.

Other European destinations in the VisaWatch scope — still pending country-by-country verification.

France, Italy, Romania, Bulgaria, Croatia, Greece, Spain, Portugal, Malta, Cyprus, Poland, Czech Republic, Ireland, Latvia and Lithuania now have separate country notes in this review page. Any other destination not individually documented remains Needs official-source review. Student maintenance and work rules are national; do not copy one country's figure or work limit to another. Confirm application-date rules, visa category, permitted work hours, work authorisation and any local consular checklist before client advice.

Counsellor workflow: record the applicant's nationality, age, course level, exact provider/campus, course dates, family members, application date, funding source and intended work. Separate immigration minimum funds from a realistic total budget. Never tell a client that part-time work is guaranteed or will cover tuition/living costs. Re-check official rules immediately before lodgement and obtain reviewer sign-off.

ROMANIA

Romania — D/SD study visa, funds, work and post-study route

Scope: non-EU/EEA applicants, including Sri Lankan nationals, seeking full-time higher education at a Romanian public or private institution accredited or provisionally authorised under Romanian law. Check the exact institution, programme and Ministry of Education acceptance before advising a client.

1. Admission and D/SD eligibility.

Obtain the study acceptance document issued by the Romanian Ministry of Education showing full-time study. The applicant must show payment of tuition fees for at least one academic year. The study-language evidence is required, except for the Romanian-language preparatory year for foreign students. A university offer letter alone is not a substitute for the required Ministry acceptance document.

2. Sri Lanka application route — e-VISA then competent Romanian mission.

Complete the online application through the Romanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs e-VISA portal. After the file is validated, an interview/appointment is arranged at the competent Romanian diplomatic mission or consular post; the applicant must attend in person and present originals and copies. Romania has an Embassy in Colombo, but applicants must contact the Embassy directly to confirm that it accepts D/SD long-stay applications from Sri Lankan residents and obtain its current appointment/document instructions. Do not redirect a student automatically to New Delhi or a VFS centre without written confirmation. The e-VISA portal is the official application channel; it does not itself grant a visa.

3. Financial evidence — monthly minimum-wage benchmark.

The official D/SD checklist requires proof of means of support at least equal to Romania's guaranteed gross national minimum basic salary, monthly, for the entire period specified in the visa. This is not a universal fixed euro amount: calculate using the Romanian gross minimum salary legally in force for the relevant application period and the visa duration, and retain the official effective-date source. Scholarship holders may have specific exemptions. Do not count hoped-for future part-time earnings as the primary proof of funds.

4. Other core documents and insurance.

Prepare valid passport (issued within the last 10 years and valid at least 3 months beyond the requested visa validity), recent photographs, criminal-record certificate, proof of accommodation and travel medical insurance covering the intended visa period across the Member States with at least €30,000 coverage. Documents may need translation/legalisation according to the issuing country and Romanian mission instructions. Confirm the current D/SD visa fee with the Embassy before payment; fee information can differ between general guidance and mission-specific payment instructions.

5. Processing and entry.

The long-stay D/SD visa is normally granted for 90 days. The official e-VISA FAQ recommends lodging long-stay applications at least 45 days before intended travel and no more than 90 days in advance; actual processing varies with mission workload and any National Visa Centre/IGI review. A visa does not guarantee admission at the border. After arrival, apply for the Romanian study residence permit through the General Inspectorate for Immigration (IGI).

6. Residence permit and renewal.

Apply to the territorial IGI office for the residence permit at least 30 days before the visa/right of stay expires. The permit may cover the total duration of studies, subject to enrolment and legal conditions. Keep proof of full-time registration, accommodation, health cover and means of support ready for extensions. The published IGI process indicates a decision period of 30 days, which may be extended by up to 15 days for additional checks.

7. Work during studies.

Current IGI student guidance states that foreign students with a valid study residence right may work without a separate work permit on an individual part-time employment contract for up to 6 hours per day. Older IGI material may still show a 4-hour limit; use the current IGI study page and confirm the applicable law when advising. Self-employment and full-time work are not covered by this student exception. Do not promise a job or use expected earnings as guaranteed tuition funding.

8. After graduation.

Eligible graduates may request an extension of temporary stay for up to 9 months after graduation to seek employment, complete hiring formalities or open a business, by submitting proof of completed studies and meeting IGI conditions. This is an application, not an automatic visa conversion or job guarantee.

9. Schengen distinction.

Romania became a full Schengen member on 1 January 2025. However, a Romanian national long-stay D visa is issued for entry/stay in Romania and is not a residence permit for other Schengen states. Once holding a valid Romanian residence permit, short visits to other Schengen countries remain subject to the usual 90-days-in-any-180-day rule.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before collecting a deposit, verify Ministry of Education acceptance, institution accreditation, full-time status, one-year tuition payment, current gross minimum-wage calculation for the full visa period, sponsor/source-of-funds evidence, accommodation, €30,000 insurance, criminal record and translations, Colombo Embassy acceptance/appointment, live fee, processing lead time, residence-permit deadline and the student's actual work eligibility. Explain that part-time work is not guaranteed.

Official references

  • Romanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — e-VISA supporting documents (D/SD study visa)
  • Romanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — e-VISA FAQ (application, mission, timing and visa procedure)
  • General Inspectorate for Immigration — Studies (current study residence, employment and graduate rules)
  • General Inspectorate for Immigration — How to obtain a long-stay study visa and residence permit
  • Study in Romania — Official visa rules and procedures, including Schengen status
  • Embassy of Romania in Colombo — confirm Sri Lanka long-stay visa appointment and document submission instructions

Source owner: Romanian Ministry of Foreign Affairs (e-VISA) and General Inspectorate for Immigration (IGI). Re-audited 18 November 2026 against the current D/SD supporting-document list, e-VISA FAQ, IGI study guidance and Study in Romania portal. The financial test is tied to the gross national minimum salary for the entire visa period; do not hard-code a monthly EUR figure without checking the applicable effective date. The current IGI study page states a 6-hour-per-day part-time limit; an older IGI page still shows 4 hours, so retain the newer official guidance and recheck the legal text at filing. Sri Lanka applicants should obtain direct written confirmation from the Embassy of Romania in Colombo about D/SD intake and appointment steps.

BULGARIA

Bulgaria — Type D study visa, student residence, funds, work and graduate route

Scope: non-EU/EEA applicants, including Sri Lankan nationals, admitted to full-time study at an eligible Bulgarian higher-education institution. A short course, distance-learning programme or general offer letter should not be assumed to qualify for the student residence route. Bulgaria is a full Schengen member, but its national student residence permission is for Bulgaria.

1. Admission and eligibility.

Obtain the official admission/acceptance evidence for full-time higher education and the documents required by the Bulgarian Ministry of Education and Science and the competent migration authority. Evidence of language proficiency/linguistic examination may be required for admission. Confirm the institution and exact programme are eligible before accepting tuition or marketing a pathway.

2. Sri Lanka application route — Type D visa.

Students normally need a Bulgarian long-stay Type D visa before travel, followed by a residence-permit application in Bulgaria through the Ministry of Interior Migration Directorate. The Bulgarian MFA's general Type D document page sets out the legal document framework, but it does not establish a Sri Lanka-specific submission route. Before booking travel or promising a Colombo filing, contact the Bulgarian MFA/competent diplomatic mission and obtain written confirmation of which mission accepts applications from Sri Lankan residents, the appointment process, and whether submission is directly at the Embassy or through an authorised provider. Do not assume a VFS centre or New Delhi jurisdiction without current official confirmation.

3. Financial evidence — calculate against current Bulgarian law.

The student must show sufficient lawful resources to cover subsistence and return travel. The European Commission's student-country guidance describes the requirement but does not publish a single fixed euro amount for every student case. The Bulgarian MFA Type D document framework uses a subsistence benchmark linked to the national minimum monthly wage or a scholarship for the period of stay. Calculate the amount using the minimum wage legally effective for the relevant application/permit period and the duration requested; do not copy an old BGN or EUR figure into a 2026 client quotation. Scholarship evidence and sponsor funding must be documented and accepted by the competent mission.

4. Accommodation, insurance and supporting documents.

Prepare evidence of accommodation in Bulgaria, valid health/travel insurance covering the required period, proof of funds, admission evidence, valid passport, application form, photographs and any required medical/criminal-record documents. The Bulgarian MFA Type D framework specifies insurance with at least €30,000 coverage for the relevant long-stay visa category. Foreign public documents may require legalisation/apostille and certified Bulgarian translation. Follow the exact checklist issued by the competent mission; requirements can vary by legal basis and applicant.

5. Entry and residence permit.

After the Type D visa is issued, enter Bulgaria and apply in person to the competent Migration Directorate for the student residence permit. The residence permission is normally granted for up to one year at a time and is renewable while the student continues to meet the study conditions. Register the Bulgarian residential address within five days of arrival, as stated in the European Commission and European Education Area guidance. A Type D visa is not a substitute for the post-entry residence permit.

6. Work during studies.

Eligible non-EU students with a valid student residence status may work without a separate work permit for up to 20 hours per week during the academic year and during official holidays, subject to the applicable statutory conditions and employer reporting/registration duties. This is a legal ceiling, not a job guarantee. Do not advise that part-time work will necessarily cover tuition or living expenses.

7. After graduation.

The European Commission's Bulgaria student guidance describes a period of up to nine months after completion of education in which a graduate may seek and start work, subject to the required application/registration with the Employment Agency within seven working days and the applicable national conditions. This is not an automatic extension of the student permit or a guarantee of employment. Confirm the current national procedure with the Migration Directorate and Employment Agency before advising a particular graduate.

8. Schengen travel distinction.

Bulgaria became a full Schengen member on 1 January 2025. A valid Bulgarian residence permit generally allows short visits to other Schengen countries under the 90 days in any 180-day period rule; it does not authorise the student to live, study long-term or work in another Schengen country.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before taking a client deposit, verify the exact full-time programme and institution, official admission evidence, current minimum-wage-based funds calculation for the entire requested period, sponsor/source-of-funds documents, accommodation, insurance, translations/legalisation, competent mission for Sri Lanka, appointment/submission route, current visa and residence fees, residence-permit deadline, address registration, student work eligibility and graduate application deadline. Do not guarantee work, post-study employment or Schengen settlement rights.

Official references

  • European Commission — Student in Bulgaria (Type D, residence, funds, work and graduate route)
  • Bulgarian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Documents required for Type D visa (financial means, accommodation and €30,000 insurance framework)
  • Bulgarian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Current consular fees
  • Bulgarian Ministry of Interior — Migration Directorate (residence permit procedure)
  • European Education Area — Study in Bulgaria (higher education, costs and address registration)
  • Bulgarian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Diplomatic and consular missions (confirm the competent mission for Sri Lanka)

Source owner: Bulgarian Ministry of Foreign Affairs (visa/consular requirements), Ministry of Interior Migration Directorate (residence permissions), European Commission Migration and Home Affairs (student route summary), and European Education Area (study-country guidance). Re-audited 18 November 2026 against the European Commission student-in-Bulgaria page (1 April 2025), Bulgarian MFA Type D document framework, MFA fee directory and European Education Area country profile. The financial test is linked to the applicable national minimum wage/scholarship for the stay period; no stale fixed EUR figure is hard-coded. The European Commission page states 20 hours per week during the academic year and official holidays, and a graduate period up to nine months with Employment Agency registration within seven working days. Confirm the competent mission and Sri Lankan submission route directly with the Bulgarian MFA before filing.

CROATIA

Croatia — university-study temporary stay, D visa, funds, work and graduate route

Scope: non-EU/EEA applicants, including Sri Lankan nationals, admitted to a Croatian higher-education institution for university studies. Croatia is a full Schengen member. A Croatian study temporary-stay permit is a national residence authorisation; it is not permission to live or work long-term in another Schengen state.

1. Admission and eligibility.

Obtain official proof of enrolment/admission at a recognised Croatian higher-education institution and evidence of the purpose of stay. The temporary-stay application also requires sufficient means for subsistence and return travel, health insurance and a valid passport. Check the exact programme and institution with the receiving university; a general course offer should not be assumed to qualify for the university-study residence category.

2. Sri Lankan route — Embassy jurisdiction and Colombo/VFS instructions need care.

The Croatian Embassy in New Delhi officially covers Sri Lanka. The MFA's country visa overview says applications may also be submitted at the visa application centre in Colombo, while the Embassy's detailed enclosed-documents notice says Sri Lankan visa applicants must personally appear at the Embassy in New Delhi; it also describes an exception allowing Sri Lankan applicants to attend the Honorary Consulate in Colombo for personal verification and then submit through a VFS centre in India. These instructions are not fully aligned. For a student, first ask croemb.new-delhi@mvep.hr to confirm in writing the current route for the specific temporary-stay/D-visa stage, whether Colombo VFS accepts that category, and whether Colombo personal verification followed by VFS India is required. Do not treat the Colombo VFS Schengen student checklist as proof that it accepts the national study-residence application.

3. Temporary stay application and D visa sequence.

A third-country national may apply in person at a Croatian diplomatic mission/consular post. Exceptionally, a visa-required student arriving for undergraduate, graduate or postgraduate university study may submit the temporary-stay application at the competent police administration/police station in Croatia. If temporary stay is approved and the student needs a visa to enter Croatia, the student must obtain a Type D visa for entry. The D visa is generally issued after temporary stay is granted; it is not the student residence permit itself. Follow the Embassy's written sequence for applying from Sri Lanka.

4. Financial evidence — no fixed universal EUR amount in the student summary.

The student must prove sufficient resources to cover subsistence during the planned stay and return travel. The European Commission's student guidance does not state a single fixed monthly euro amount for all students. Use the current Croatian legal calculation and the competent mission's checklist for the application date and intended stay; document the applicant's own funds, scholarship or sponsor's lawful ability to support. Do not use the short-stay tourist benchmark of €70 per day as the student residence financial requirement.

5. Core documents, health cover and criminal record.

Prepare valid passport, application form, recent photograph, proof of enrolment, evidence of means of support and return travel, health insurance, and first-permit criminal-record evidence where applicable. The European Commission checklist specifies a duly legalised criminal-record certificate for the first permit. The Croatian VFS page specifies travel medical insurance of at least €30,000 for Schengen visa applications; confirm the exact insurance scope and period for the D visa/residence process with the Embassy. Foreign documents may require apostille/legalisation and Croatian translation.

6. Permit duration, registration and renewal.

University-study temporary stay is normally granted for one year or the shorter study duration. The renewal application must be submitted to the competent police administration/station no later than 60 days before expiry. After entry, register the address/short-term stay with the competent police administration or police station within 3 days. If a D visa is issued, contact the competent police office within 30 days from the beginning of its validity to register the residence and complete biometric residence-card steps. The biometric card is issued in Croatia, not at the Embassy.

7. Fees — residence procedure, not a single visa bundle.

The European Commission lists €46.45 for the temporary-stay procedure, plus biometric residence-card costs of €31.85 (normal) or €59.73 (accelerated), and an additional €9.29 issuance charge. The D-visa fee and any VFS/courier fees are separate and depend on the applicable category and current mission instructions. Do not quote the Colombo VFS short-stay fee as the national D-visa or residence fee.

8. Work during studies.

Students may work or carry out self-employment outside their study time for a maximum of 20 hours per week, according to the European Commission student guidance. This is not a blanket permission to miss classes or work 20 hours during scheduled study time. It is not a job guarantee or a safe basis for funding tuition.

9. After graduation.

After completing studies, eligible third-country nationals may seek an extension of stay for one year, renewable for one further year, to seek employment or set up a business. The graduate must submit the appropriate new application before the existing permit expires; the post-study route is not automatic and does not guarantee employment.

10. Schengen distinction.

Croatia has applied the Schengen acquis since 1 January 2023. A valid Croatian residence permit allows short visits to other Schengen states under the usual 90 days in any 180-day period rule, but it does not authorise long-term study, residence or employment in another member state.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before taking a client deposit, verify the recognised institution and university enrolment, exact temporary-stay category, proof of funds and return travel, health cover, criminal-record/legalisation requirements, Embassy New Delhi jurisdiction, the conflicting Colombo/VFS instructions, current D-visa and residence fees, address-registration and renewal deadlines, study-time work restriction and graduate application timing. Obtain the Embassy's written Sri Lanka-specific submission instructions before booking travel or VFS.

Official references

  • European Commission — Student in Croatia (documents, fees, permit duration, work and post-study route)
  • Croatian Ministry of Interior — Temporary stay of third-country nationals (university students may apply in Croatia)
  • Croatian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Sri Lanka visa requirements and visa-centre information
  • Croatian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Embassy New Delhi jurisdiction includes Sri Lanka; Colombo Honorary Consulate
  • Croatian Ministry of Foreign Affairs — Applying for long-stay visa D
  • Croatian Embassy New Delhi — Sri Lankan applicants' personal appearance and Colombo verification exception
  • VFS Global — Croatia Sri Lanka visa information (check category-specific scope and current charges)

Source owner: Croatian Ministry of Interior (temporary stay), Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs / Embassy New Delhi (visa and Sri Lanka jurisdiction), European Commission Migration and Home Affairs (student rights and fees), and VFS Global (local visa-centre information). Re-audited 18 November 2026. The detailed Embassy notice and general MFA country overview provide different Sri Lanka submission descriptions; this conflict is explicitly flagged for written mission confirmation. The 20-hour weekly work limit applies outside study time. Financial evidence is described as sufficient means for subsistence and return travel; no unsupported fixed monthly euro amount is inserted.

SLOVAKIA

Slovakia — university-study temporary residence, national D visa, RegVIS, work and graduate route

Scope: non-EU/EEA nationals, including Sri Lankan applicants, seeking eligible full-time university study in Slovakia. The residence permission is a national Slovak temporary residence permit for study, not a generic Schengen study visa. A national D visa may be needed to enter Slovakia and complete the residence process.

1. Eligible study and institution.

Study-based temporary residence is available for full-time university students at a Slovak university, as well as specified school/post-secondary and university-preparatory categories subject to age and programme conditions. Confirm the institution and exact programme qualify under Section 24 of Act No. 404/2011. The admission/acceptance confirmation used for the application must generally be no older than 90 days. Conditional admission may require recognition of the Sri Lankan secondary-school leaving certificate; the New Delhi Embassy study D-visa checklist asks for Slovak-language recognition where the institution requires it.

2. Important 2026 appointment process — RegVIS.

For university study and research national D visa applications handled by the Slovak Embassy in New Delhi, the university/research institution must first register the applicant through the Ministry's RegVIS registration portal. If accepted in a registration round, the institution informs the student that they may book an appointment at the designated BLS International or VFS Global visa centre. RegVIS registration does not itself guarantee a specific appointment or visa approval. Do not pay an agent who promises an appointment; the Embassy expressly warns it does not cooperate with paid intermediaries.

3. Sri Lankan jurisdiction and document legalisation.

The Slovak Embassy in New Delhi's instructions explicitly include Sri Lankan citizens in its country-specific process. Sri Lankan public documents must first be legalised by the relevant Sri Lankan Ministry of Foreign Affairs and then superlegalised by the Embassy of Slovakia in New Delhi before subsequent copying or translation steps, as instructed by the Embassy. Documents must be translated into Slovak by an authorised official translator. Confirm the current physical submission location and appointment channel with the Embassy/institution; do not assume a Colombo visa centre accepts this national study category merely because it accepts some other visa categories.

4. University-study national D visa — published New Delhi checklist.

The Embassy's current university-study checklist lists: passport valid at least 90 days beyond intended departure; national visa form; temporary-residence application; recent 3 × 3.5 cm colour photo; €90 administrative fee; Slovak-language university admission decision; criminal-record extract from Sri Lanka (and from another country where the stated residence test applies), apostilled/superlegalised and officially translated into Slovak; flight reservation; and health insurance covering medical expenses in Slovakia from intended entry until the end of the national visa (120 days). The criminal-record certificate must not be older than 90 days; the Embassy recommends one issued within the previous 45 days. The Embassy may require a personal interview; its page says an IELTS or TOEFL certificate removes the interview requirement stated there.

5. Temporary residence application — core evidence.

The Ministry's temporary-residence study checklist identifies a valid passport, proof of admission/study purpose, and clean criminal-record evidence from the applicant's country of nationality and any country where the applicant stayed more than 90 days during six consecutive months within the last three years. Public documents must be authenticated and translated as required. University students are exempt from submitting accommodation evidence with the study temporary-residence application under the Ministry's general study checklist. However, the New Delhi national D visa checklist separately asks for accommodation for the 90-day entry period; follow that visa checklist too.

6. Financial evidence — avoid confusing the D visa and residence tests.

The general Slovak temporary-residence guidance describes financial means at the subsistence-minimum level for each month of residence, with twelve times the subsistence minimum where residence exceeds one year. From 1 July 2026 the adult subsistence minimum is €295.22 per month (12 months = €3,542.64). But the study-specific Ministry checklist does not list a separate bank statement among the required study documents, while the New Delhi D-visa page separately requires proof of sufficient funds for the 90-day stay. The Embassy's general D-visa guidance gives a daily calculation (normally €56/day, or €26/day where accommodation is paid) for the relevant D-visa process. Counsellors must confirm which financial test applies to the student's exact filing route and avoid automatically treating the generic monthly figure or daily D-visa amount as a universal student tuition/living-cost budget.

7. Permit duration and after-arrival obligations.

Study temporary residence may be granted for the expected study period up to a maximum of six years and may be renewed while the study purpose continues. After the residence is granted, enter Slovakia within 180 days. Report the start of stay to the Foreign Police within 3 working days of arrival. Within 3 working days after collecting the residence card, arrange health insurance. Submit a medical report confirming no disease endangering public health within 30 days after collecting the card (the official guidance allows an extension in specified circumstances). Students must generally spend more than half of the granted residence period in Slovakia in a calendar year, subject to the stated mobility exception.

8. Work during study.

Full-time university students holding study residence may work up to 80 hours per month (equivalent to the European Commission's 20-hour-per-week summary) without a separate work permit or changing the purpose of residence. High-school students have a different 40-hours-per-month limit. Work beyond the student limit requires the relevant change to employment residence/work authorisation. Part-time work is not guaranteed and should not be presented as a way to fund all tuition and living expenses.

9. After graduation — 9-month job-search/business extension.

The latest IOM Migration Information Centre guidance, updated 22 July 2026, says a university graduate (including Bachelor's level) may apply to renew study residence for nine months to seek employment or start a business, supported by the diploma or confirmation of graduation. The European Commission portal's older 1 April 2025 summary describes only a 30-day protective period. This is a material source-date discrepancy: use the newer IOM guidance as the current working position, but confirm the applicable statutory provision and filing deadline with Foreign Police before advising a client. The extension is not automatic and does not itself guarantee a job.

10. Processing, fees and Schengen distinction.

The IOM information centre states that the Foreign Police decision period for a study temporary-residence application is up to 90 days. The national D-visa fee shown on the current New Delhi university-study checklist is €90; a separate older Embassy general residence/D-visa information page displays a different €33 equivalent for a different/legacy filing description. Use the current study-specific RegVIS checklist and confirm the fee at appointment; do not combine the two fee figures. A Slovak residence card allows short Schengen visits under the usual 90/180 rule, but does not grant long-term residence or employment rights in other Schengen states.

Counsellor stop-check.

Before accepting a client payment, verify full-time status and recognised university, RegVIS eligibility and appointment round, exact New Delhi submission channel, whether the student needs a national D visa and temporary residence filing, Sri Lankan MFA legalisation followed by Slovak Embassy superlegalisation, Slovak translation, current criminal-record age/validity, separate D-visa and residence financial evidence, accommodation for the visa stage, insurance, medical report deadline, work-hour cap and post-study extension timing. Never promise an appointment, visa approval, job or guaranteed part-time income.

Official references

  • Slovak Embassy New Delhi — National D Visa for Study (RegVIS, current university checklist, €90 fee, documents and interview)
  • Slovak Embassy New Delhi — Residence / national D visa instructions (Sri Lankan legalisation and superlegalisation process)
  • Slovak Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs — Residence of foreign nationals; study requirements and general financial rules
  • IOM Migration Information Centre — Temporary residence for study (updated 22 July 2026; 80 hours/month, 9-month graduate extension and after-arrival obligations)
  • IOM Migration Information Centre — Minimum subsistence level effective 1 July 2026 (€295.22)
  • European Commission — Student in Slovakia (eligibility, work and older post-study summary)

Source owner: Slovak Ministry of Interior / Foreign Police (temporary residence), Slovak Ministry of Foreign and European Affairs / Embassy New Delhi (national visa, RegVIS and Sri Lankan document route), IOM Migration Information Centre (operational residence guidance and 2026 subsistence figure), and European Commission (EU immigration overview). Re-audited 18 November 2026. Explicitly flagged: old EC 30-day graduate summary versus newer IOM 9-month extension; D-visa fees differ across general and current study-specific Embassy pages; financial evidence differs by filing stage. Written confirmation is required where these affect a live client application.

ESTONIA

Estonia — study D visa, temporary residence permit (TRP), Sri Lankan route, funds and work (2026)

Scope: non-EU/EEA students, including Sri Lankan citizens, admitted to an Estonian recognised university or other eligible educational institution. A full-degree student normally needs a national long-stay D visa to enter Estonia and, where studies exceed one year, a temporary residence permit for study (TRP) after arrival.
